| `keep_alive`, any other `type` | always                                                                                                                                                              | ignored                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

A `status` line with a `permissionMode` field that also starts or ends a compaction does both. Ignored lines, except `stream_event` lines, are logged in the chat's log file. Every new or replaced item is appended to the transcript and emitted as `chat.item` {chatID, item} ([Data model](data-model.md#transcript-writes)).

### Item ids

Item ids are formed as in this table. Default chosen at design time.

| Item                         | `id`                                                                         |
| ---------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| user, images, system, result | a new uppercase UUID                                                         |
| questions                    | the question set's `questionSetID` (a new uppercase UUID)                    |
| assistant, thinking, toolUse | `<message id>#<block index>`, for example `msg_011CgbN9DUEEzm4yYRLnxmCV#0`   |
| toolResult                   | `<tool_use_id>#result`, for example `toolu_01JGkKXVVCvtBiysD3rhiEXj#result`  |
| permissionRequest            | `perm-<request_id>`, for example `perm-1967e0c0-4eab-4e9a-8c9e-67ec5e2cb4a0` |

Every item gets `created` = the time the engine creates it, in UTC with seconds. Default chosen at design time.

### Streaming

The engine keeps, per chat and per message id, the blocks opened by `content_block_start` events, in index order.

- Message id of a `stream_event` line: its `api_message_id` field; when absent, the `event.message.id` of the latest `message_start` event with the same `parent_tool_use_id`.
- Model of a message: the `event.message.model` of its `message_start` event, a CLI model id such as `claude-opus-5-5`; for a sub-agent's message, the sub-agent's model. A message whose `message_start` has no `model` string has no model.
- `content_block_start` opens the block `event.index` of that message with the type `event.content_block.type`; its item id is `<message id>#<index>` and its accumulated text starts empty.
- A delta appends its text to the block's accumulated text and to its pending buffer. A delta with empty text changes nothing and never produces a `chat.delta`.
- Flush (Default chosen at design time): at most every 50 ms per chat, the engine emits one `chat.delta` {chatID, itemID, kind, append, model, effort}, where `append` is the pending buffer of the block whose buffer is the oldest, and empties that buffer. Every chat thus stays at 20 `chat.delta` events per second or fewer.
- `model` and `effort` of a `chat.delta` of kind `text`: the model of the block's message and the chat's `settings.effort` when the delta was read, `effort` being null when that model has no effort setting ([Model names](#model-names)) and both being null when the message has no model. A `chat.delta` of kind `thinking` or `toolInput` has neither.
- When the final item of a block is emitted, the block's pending buffer is dropped: the item carries the complete text.
- `chat.delta` events are not persisted and are not replayed to clients that connect later.
- Finalization: when a `result` arrives or the process exits, every open `text` or `thinking` block that has no final item and whose accumulated text is not empty becomes an `assistant` or `thinking` item with that text, an `assistant` item taking the `model` of its message and `effort` as [Assistant blocks](#assistant-blocks) sets it; every open `tool_use` block without a final item is dropped. Then the open blocks and the models of their messages are cleared. Default chosen at design time.

### Assistant blocks

With `--include-partial-messages` the CLI sends one `assistant` line per finished content block, before that block's `content_block_stop`; the lines of one message share `message.id`. For each block of `message.content`, in order, the engine takes the first open block of the same message id with the same type and no final item yet, and uses its index. When none matches (a block that was not streamed), the index is the number of blocks already known for that message id, and the block is recorded as known.

| Content block `type`                         | Item        | Fields                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| -------------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `text`                                       | `assistant` | `text` = block `text`; `messageID` = `message.id`; `parentToolUseID` = line `parent_tool_use_id`; `model` = `message.model`, null when absent; `effort` = the chat's `settings.effort` when the line is read, null when `model` is null or has no effort setting ([Model names](#model-names)). No item when `text` is empty. Default chosen at design time. |
| `thinking`                                   | `thinking`  | `text` = block `thinking`; `parentToolUseID` = line `parent_tool_use_id`. No item when `thinking` is empty.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `tool_use`                                   | `toolUse`   | `toolUseID` = block `id`; `name` and `server` from block `name` (rule below); `input` = block `input`; `parentToolUseID` = line `parent_tool_use_id`.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| any other type (`redacted_thinking`, others) | none        | the block still counts for the index rule                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Name rule ([Data model](data-model.md#tool-use-item)): a tool name `mcp__<server>__<tool>` gives `server` = the text between `mcp__` and the next `__`, and `name` = the rest; any other name gives `name` = the tool name and `server` = null. Example: `mcp__hephaestus__create_ticket` gives `server` `hephaestus` and `name` `create_ticket`.

`model` is the model that wrote the block: the main conversation's model, or a sub-agent's own model for a line with a `parent_tool_use_id`. `effort` is the chat's effort for both, since a sub-agent reports no effort of its own. Changing the Model or Effort menu later leaves the items already written unchanged ([Changing settings](#changing-settings)).

The engine also keeps, for each chat process, the model of the latest `assistant` line or `message_start` event it read, and one record per `tool_use` block named `mcp__hephaestus__add_comment`: the block's `input.ticketID` in uppercase, its `input.text` and the `message.model` of its line. [tickets.addComment](engine-api.md#ticketsaddcomment) takes the model of a comment Claude adds from them. The records are cleared when a `result` arrives and when the process exits. Default chosen at design time.

With Claude Code 2.1.309 and its default thinking display, `thinking` blocks stream empty text (`thinking_display` `updates`), so they produce no item.

### Tool results

Each `tool_result` block of a `user` line becomes one `toolResult` item:

| Field       | Source                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `toolUseID` | block `tool_use_id`                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `text`      | block `content` when it is a string; when it is an array, the `text` of its `text` blocks joined with `\n`, then one line of compact JSON per block that is neither `text` nor `image`                                  |
| `isError`   | block `is_error`; false when absent                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `images`    | one Attachment per `image` block of `content`, stored in the chat's attachment folder with the name `image-<n>.<png, jpg, gif or webp>`, `<n>` counting from 1 in each result ([Data model](data-model.md#attachments)) |

An `image` block whose `source.type` is not `base64` holds no data and is left out; one without `source.media_type` counts as `image/png`. An image of another media type takes the media type's subtype as extension (`image/bmp` gives `bmp`), `bin` when it has none. Default chosen at design time.

A `toolResult` whose `toolUseID` matches no `toolUse` item is kept and rendered alone ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)).

### Session start item

For the first `system/init` of each process the engine adds a `system` item with level info and the text:

`Session started · <model name> · effort <settings.effort> · <permission label>`, followed by ` · Ultracode` when `settings.ultracode` is true.

- `<model name>`: the name of `init.model` given by the engine's cached models ([Model names](#model-names)): the `displayName` of the first cached model whose value is not `default` and whose `resolvedModel` equals `init.model`; else the `displayName` of the model whose `value` equals `init.model`; else `init.model` itself. Example: `claude-opus-5-5` gives `Opus 5.5`.
- `<permission label>`: from `init.permissionMode`, with the labels of the column "System line" in [Permission mode](#permission-mode); a mode the table does not list is shown as the CLI reports it. Example: `default` gives `Ask every time`.
- When `system/init` has no `model` or no `permissionMode`, the chat's `settings.model` or the CLI value of `settings.permissionMode` stands for it. Default chosen at design time.

MCP check: when `init.mcp_servers` has no entry named `hephaestus`, or that entry's `status` is `failed`, `needs-auth` or `disabled`, the engine adds a `system` item with level warning and the text `MCP server hephaestus is <status, or missing when there is no entry>: the HephaestusV2 tools are not available in this session.` Default chosen at design time.

### Turn result

A `result` line becomes a `result` item:

| Field        | Source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `durationMs` | `duration_ms`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `costUSD`    | `total_cost_usd`, the running total of the session, not the cost of this turn; null when absent                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `isError`    | `is_error`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `summary`    | null when `is_error` is false; else `Stopped by the user.` for the first `result` after Stop ([Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn)), whose `errors` hold only a diagnostic of the CLI such as `[ede_diagnostic] result_type=user last_content_type=n/a stop_reason=null` (Default chosen at design time); else `result` when `subtype` is `success`, else the `errors` strings joined with `\n`, else the `subtype` (`error_during_execution`, `error_max_turns`, `error_max_budget_usd`, `error_max_structured_output_retries`) |

The engine derives the chat's cost from the `costUSD` values of its `result` items ([Chat cost](costs.md#chat-cost)). Then the CLI turn closes ([Turn tracking](#turn-tracking)). `queued_turn_count` is not used: Claude Code 2.1.309 sends 0 even when messages wait in its queue.

### Turn tracking

Claude Code 2.1.309 runs the messages as CLI turns: a `system/init` line, the lines of Claude's work, then a `result` line. A message written during a CLI turn waits in the CLI's queue; when the CLI turn ends, the CLI runs every waiting message together as the next CLI turn, which starts with a new `system/init`. The CLI reports each message by the `uuid` of its line ([User messages](#user-messages)) in `command_lifecycle` lines:

```json
{"type":"command_lifecycle","command_uuid":"7C3E1F0A-2B4D-4E6F-8A9B-0C1D2E3F4A5B","state":"started","uuid":"1a63b051-eb56-4e4d-a9b2-7b8fff546781","session_id":"3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301"}
```

| `state`     | Sent when                                                                |
| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `queued`    | the CLI has read the message                                             |
| `started`   | the CLI turn that runs the message begins, just before its `system/init` |
| `completed` | after the `result` of that CLI turn                                      |
| `cancelled` | after the `result` of that CLI turn, when Stop interrupted it            |

For each process the engine keeps the outstanding messages, those it wrote and that are not finished, each with the last `state` reported, and whether a CLI turn is open:

1. Writing a user message to the process adds it as outstanding, with no state.
2. `system/init` opens a CLI turn. When the chat has no turn in progress, a turn starts: the idle timer is cancelled and the state becomes running.
3. `command_lifecycle` records the `state` of the outstanding message `command_uuid`; a state other than `queued` and `started` finishes it. A line that names no outstanding message is ignored.
4. `result` closes the CLI turn and finishes every outstanding message whose state is `started`. When none has that state, it finishes the oldest outstanding message that has no state, so that a turn still ends when the CLI sends no `command_lifecycle` lines.
5. When step 3 or 4 leaves no outstanding message and no open CLI turn during a turn, the turn ends: the metadata file is rewritten, the idle timer starts when the process has no background work running ([Idle termination and quitting](#idle-termination-and-quitting)) and `chat.updated` reports the new state. A turn that ends in `idle` posts a "finished" notification, one that ends in `waitingAnswers` a "needs you" notification ([Notifications](#notifications)).
6. When the process exits, its outstanding messages are dropped ([Exit handling and logs](#exit-handling-and-logs)).

These rules are Default chosen at design time. Example with Claude Code 2.1.309, a new process and the messages A and B written 0.3 s apart:

| Line read, or message written                       | Outstanding         | CLI turn | State    |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | -------- | -------- |
| A written                                           | A                   | closed   | starting |
| `command_lifecycle` A `queued`, then A `started`    | A started           | closed   | starting |
| `system/init`                                       | A started           | open     | running  |
| B written; `command_lifecycle` B `queued`           | A started, B queued | open     | running  |
| `result` of A                                       | B queued            | closed   | running  |
| `command_lifecycle` A `completed`, then B `started` | B started           | closed   | running  |
| `system/init`                                       | B started           | open     | running  |
| `result` of B                                       | none                | closed   | idle     |
| `command_lifecycle` B `completed`                   | none                | closed   | idle     |

### Background work

Claude can start work that keeps running after its CLI turn ends: a shell command run with the Bash tool and `run_in_background` true, an agent started with `run_in_background` true, or a workflow started with the Workflow tool ([Agents](#agents)). Claude Code 2.1.309 reports it in `system` lines, checked against the captures `stream-background-bash.jsonl`, `stream-background-agent.jsonl`, `stream-background-agent-resume.jsonl`, `stream-workflow.jsonl` and `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl` ([Fixture replays](#fixture-replays)):

| `subtype`                  | Sent when                                                                        | Fields the engine reads                                                                    |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `background_tasks_changed` | a background task starts or ends                                                 | `tasks`: every background task still running, each with its `task_id`; `[]` when none runs |
| `task_notification`        | a task ends: a background task, a foreground Bash command, a command of an agent | `task_id`                                                                                  |

```json
{"type":"system","subtype":"background_tasks_changed","tasks":[{"task_id":"bw1kgc2rd","run_id":"0mvyo3daw-a560b8e7","task_type":"local_bash","description":"Sleep 15 seconds then echo bg-done"}],"uuid":"bd0fb796-d44b-4282-a19f-b6868b30b3f2","session_id":"F5055D8F-DDCC-413D-B8D1-A52C765F8076"}
```

When a background task ends while no CLI turn is open, the CLI sends `task_notification` for it and `background_tasks_changed`, then starts a CLI turn on its own that gives Claude the task's result: a `system/init` without `command_lifecycle` lines, ending with a `result` whose `origin.kind` is `task-notification`. When the task ends during a CLI turn, Claude gets its result in that CLI turn and no CLI turn follows.

For each process the engine keeps the ids of its running background tasks, every task id that a `background_tasks_changed` of the process has listed, and whether a notification turn is pending:

1. `background_tasks_changed` replaces the running background tasks with the `task_id` strings of `tasks`; an entry without a `task_id` string is skipped.
2. A `task_notification` whose `task_id` a `background_tasks_changed` of the process has listed, read while no CLI turn is open, makes a notification turn pending, so that the work still counts between the end of the task and the CLI turn that reports it. Other `task_notification` lines change nothing, and neither does the one of a workflow that the engine stopped with `stop_task`: the CLI starts no CLI turn for it ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)).
3. A `system/init` ends the pending notification turn. The CLI turn it opens starts a turn when none is in progress ([Turn tracking](#turn-tracking)), so the chat becomes running in the same `chat.updated` that reports the end of the background work.
4. When the process exits, its background tasks and its pending notification turn are dropped ([Exit handling and logs](#exit-handling-and-logs)).

The process has **background work running** while it has a running background task or a pending notification turn. The chat's runtime key `backgroundWork` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)) is true while the chat's process has background work running; `chat.updated` reports every change. Background work does not change the chat's state ([Chat states](#chat-states)): while it runs after its turn, the chat is idle, or waitingAnswers. The quit confirmation, the restart checks, the idle check before an automatic update and the status bar count it as [Chat states](#chat-states) describes. These rules are Default chosen at design time. While it runs, idle termination does not end the process ([Idle termination and quitting](#idle-termination-and-quitting)).

Example with `stream-background-bash.jsonl`, where Claude runs `sleep 15; echo bg-done` in the background, ends its turn, and reports when the command has ended:

| Lines read                                       | Background tasks | Notification turn | CLI turn | State   | `backgroundWork` |
| ------------------------------------------------ | ---------------- | ----------------- | -------- | ------- | ---------------- |
| 1 `system/init`                                  | none             | no                | open     | running | false            |
| 18 `background_tasks_changed`, tasks `bw1kgc2rd` | `bw1kgc2rd`      | no                | open     | running | true             |
| 29 `result`                                      | `bw1kgc2rd`      | no                | closed   | idle    | true             |
| 32 `task_notification` `bw1kgc2rd`               | `bw1kgc2rd`      | pending           | closed   | idle    | true             |
| 33 `background_tasks_changed`, tasks `[]`        | none             | pending           | closed   | idle    | true             |
| 34 `system/init`                                 | none             | no                | open     | running | false            |
| 43 `result`, `origin.kind` `task-notification`   | none             | no                | closed   | idle    | false            |

### Plan usage

A `rate_limit_event` line reports the usage limits of the Claude account the CLI is signed in to. The engine reads only the weekly window, `seven_day`, of its `rate_limit_info`; the five-hour window and the other windows are not read. Every such line, from any chat of the device, replaces the engine's plan usage ([Engine API › Plan usage](engine-api.md#plan-usage)) with a [PlanUsage](data-model.md#plan-usage-file):

| PlanUsage field         | Taken from `rate_limit_info`                                                                                                                |
| ----------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `utilization`           | `unifiedWindows.seven_day.utilization`: the fraction of the week's limit used, usually 0 to 1; the CLI sends values above 1 past the limit. |
| `resetsAt`              | `unifiedWindows.seven_day.resetsAt`, in Unix seconds.                                                                                       |
| `status`                | `status`: `allowed`, `allowed_warning` or `rejected`. It describes the window that currently limits the account.                            |
| `overageStatus`         | `overageStatus`, for example `rejected`; null when absent.                                                                                  |
| `overageDisabledReason` | `overageDisabledReason`, for example `out_of_credits`; null when absent.                                                                    |
| `isUsingOverage`        | `isUsingOverage`; null when absent.                                                                                                         |
| `received`              | The time the engine read the line ([Plan usage](engine-api.md#plan-usage)).                                                                 |

The strings are stored as the CLI sends them, so a value that a later CLI version adds is kept and shown. An optional field of the wrong type, or a string that is empty or holds a line break, counts as absent; such a `status` makes the line ignored. The line produces no transcript item and is not logged. Default chosen at design time.

### Context usage

The context indicator of the composer ([Context indicator](#context-indicator)) shows how much of the context window the conversation of a chat uses and how much is left before Claude Code compacts it automatically. The engine asks the CLI with the `get_context_usage` request ([Control protocol](#control-protocol)):

- after each `assistant` line of the main conversation, whose `parent_tool_use_id` is null, that is the first one of its message id in the process: the CLI sends one `assistant` line per block of a message ([Assistant blocks](#assistant-blocks)). The lines of sub-agents carry a `parent_tool_use_id` and ask nothing, and the agents of a workflow send no lines ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents));
- after each `result` line;
- after each `compact_boundary` line that adds a compaction line ([Compaction](#compaction)), so that the indicator shows the usage of the compacted conversation.

At most one request is in flight per process. A line that asks while a request waits for its answer writes nothing; once the waiting request gets its answer or is forgotten after 30 s, the engine writes one more request when at least one line asked meanwhile. Default chosen at design time.

The success `response` gives the chat's context usage, a [ContextUsage](data-model.md#context-usage):

| ContextUsage field | Taken from the success `response`                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `usedTokens`       | `totalTokens`: the tokens of the conversation.                                                                                                                                                                |
| `windowTokens`     | `maxTokens`: the context window.                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `bufferTokens`     | The `tokens` of the entry of `categories` whose `id` is `autocompact_buffer`: the autocompact buffer that Claude Code keeps free, at whose start it compacts the conversation; 0 when no entry has that `id`. |
| `received`         | The time the engine read the answer.                                                                                                                                                                          |

- The new value replaces the chat's `contextUsage` ([Chat metadata file](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)): the metadata file is written and `chat.updated` reports it to every client. The value stays after the process has ended and after a restart; it is replaced by the next value and never cleared.
- An error answer, no answer within 30 s, or a success `response` whose `totalTokens` is not a whole number of 0 or more, whose `maxTokens` is not a whole number above 0, or whose `autocompact_buffer` entry does not hold a whole number of `tokens` from 0 to below `maxTokens`, leaves the value unchanged and is logged in the chat's log file. Default chosen at design time.
- Claude Code 2.1.309 answers the request between turns and during a turn, checked against the capture `context-usage-response.json` ([Fixture replays](#fixture-replays)) and a run of Haiku 5.5 with two Bash calls: for a new session of Opus 5.5 or Haiku 5.5, `maxTokens` is 1000000 and the `autocompact_buffer` entry holds 33000 tokens. `totalTokens` is the sum of the `tokens` of the entries whose `kind` is `used`, and grows after each message; the `free_space` entry holds `maxTokens` − 33000 − `totalTokens`.

### Compaction

Claude Code compacts the conversation of a session, replacing its earlier messages with a summary, automatically when the conversation reaches the autocompact buffer ([Context usage](#context-usage)), and when the user sends `/compact`. Claude Code 2.1.309 reports a compaction with these `system` lines, checked against the capture `stream-compact.jsonl` of a `/compact` run ([Fixture replays](#fixture-replays)):

| `subtype`          | Sent when                                                               | Fields the engine reads                                                                                                                                              |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `status`           | the compaction starts: `status` is `compacting`                         | `status`                                                                                                                                                             |
| `status`           | the compaction ends: `status` is `null`, `compact_result` is `success`  | `status`                                                                                                                                                             |
| `compact_boundary` | after the compaction has replaced the earlier messages with the summary | `compact_metadata`: `trigger`, `auto` for an automatic compaction or `manual` for `/compact`, and `pre_tokens`, the tokens of the conversation before the compaction |

```json
{"type":"system","subtype":"compact_boundary","session_id":"6D09531E-C1F0-4CE4-B2BA-6FAC657D0979","uuid":"69aa56f8-24c3-43b0-aab4-2a3897710ec3","compact_metadata":{"trigger":"manual","pre_tokens":24644,"post_tokens":8453,"cumulative_dropped_tokens":16191,"duration_ms":3452},"logical_parent_uuid":"b6f3ac12-64e7-483d-8375-ab98c9b33648"}
```

For `/compact`, the CLI sends the two `status` lines in the CLI turn that runs the message, before its `system/init`, then `compact_boundary`, two `user` lines whose `message.content` is a string, the summary and `<local-command-stdout>Compacted </local-command-stdout>`, which the engine ignores ([Output mapping](#output-mapping)), and a `result` whose `num_turns` is 0.

For each process, the engine keeps whether a compaction runs and since when:

1. A `status` line whose `status` is `compacting` starts a compaction when none runs: the chat's runtime key `compactingSince` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)) becomes the time the engine read the line. While a compaction runs, such a line changes nothing.
2. The next `status` line with another `status`, `null` or a missing `status` included, ends the compaction: `compactingSince` becomes null.
3. A `result` line ends a compaction that still runs, before its `result` item, and the exit of the process ends it with the process ([Exit handling and logs](#exit-handling-and-logs)). Default chosen at design time.
4. `chat.updated` reports every change of `compactingSince` to every client. The key is not persisted: after an engine start no compaction runs.

While a compaction runs, the status line reads `Compacting the conversation… m:ss` ([Chat states](#chat-states)) and the ring of the context indicator fades ([Context indicator](#context-indicator)).

#### Compaction line

A `compact_boundary` line whose `compact_metadata` holds the string `trigger` and the whole number `pre_tokens`, 0 or more, adds a `system` item with level info:

`Conversation compacted (<trigger>, <tokens> tokens before)`

- `<trigger>`: `automatic` for `auto`, `manual` for `manual`; any other value as the CLI sends it. Default chosen at design time for other values.
- `<tokens>`: `pre_tokens` in the token format of the context indicator ([Context indicator](#context-indicator)), for example `24.6K` or `967K`.
- A compaction that ends without a `compact_boundary` line adds no line. A `compact_boundary` line without these fields adds no line and is logged. Default chosen at design time.
- After the line, the engine asks for the context usage, so that the indicator shows the usage of the compacted conversation ([Context usage](#context-usage)).
- A compaction line is a `system` item with level info whose text starts with `Conversation compacted (`; the engine adds no other `system` item that starts so. The tooltip of the context indicator counts the compaction lines of the chat's transcript ([Context indicator](#context-indicator)).

Line 18 of `stream-compact.jsonl` adds `Conversation compacted (manual, 24.6K tokens before)`; with `trigger` `auto` and `pre_tokens` 967000 the line is `Conversation compacted (automatic, 967K tokens before)`.

### Sub-agents

Lines with a non-null `parent_tool_use_id` come from a sub-agent started by the tool call (`Agent`, or `Task` in older CLI versions) with that id. Their `assistant`, `thinking` and `toolUse` items keep it in `parentToolUseID` and are rendered inside that tool call's card ([Tool cards](#tool-cards)); a `toolResult` is rendered in the card of its own `toolUse`, so it nests with it. Each such call is also an agent of the chat, with its own row in the chat lists and its own view ([Agents](#agents)). The agents of a workflow send no lines on the standard output: the engine reads their transcripts from the CLI's workflow folder ([Workflow folder](#workflow-folder)).

### Size limits

In a stored `toolUse.input` (at any depth) and in `toolResult.text`, a string longer than 100,000 characters is cut to 100,000 characters followed by `… [truncated <n> characters]`, where `<n>` is the number of characters removed. Default chosen at design time.

### Fixture replays

`Tests/HephaestusCoreTests/Fixtures/` holds the sanitized captures `stream-bash-allowed.jsonl`, `stream-write-denied.jsonl`, `stream-background-bash.jsonl`, `stream-background-agent.jsonl`, `stream-background-agent-resume.jsonl`, `stream-workflow.jsonl` with the workflow folder of its run, `stream-workflow-folder/`, `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl`, `stream-compact.jsonl`, `initialize-response.json` and `context-usage-response.json`, the answer of Claude Code 2.1.309 to `get_context_usage` for a new Haiku 5.5 session. A test reads `context-usage-response.json` with the rules of [Context usage](#context-usage): `usedTokens` 18554, `windowTokens` 1000000, `bufferTokens` 33000. A test replays each stream through the mapping, with the cached models of `initialize-response.json` and the chat settings of the capture, and compares the items, ignoring the `id` of user, system and result items and every `created`. The settings are model `haiku` and effort `low`, with permission mode `manual` and ultracode false for the first two captures, `bypassPermissions` and ultracode false for the three background captures and `stream-compact.jsonl`, and `bypassPermissions` and ultracode true for the two workflow captures. Line numbers count from 1.

A second test replays each background and workflow capture through the engine, with the in-memory `ClaudeProcessLauncher` ([Development](development.md#test-rules)), and compares the `state` and `backgroundWork` that `chat.updated` reports with the example of [Background work](#background-work) and the tables below, and, for the workflow captures and the sub-agent captures, the chat's workflows and agents and the agent transcripts with the tables below. The test replaces the `Transcript dir:` path of line 30 with a folder of its own temporary directory, and copies the files of `stream-workflow-folder/` into it after replaying line 53, so that the last read of the folder at line 54 finds the complete run.

`stream-background-agent-resume.jsonl` (Claude starts a `general-purpose` agent with model `sonnet`, `run_in_background` true and the prompt `Reply with the single word ok.`, and replies `started`; the agent fails, and Claude replies `ended` in the CLI turn that follows; then the user sends `Resume that agent with the SendMessage tool, message "Reply with the single word ok.". Then reply with the single word resumed. When you learn that the agent has ended, reply with the single word finished.`, Claude resumes the agent with `SendMessage` and replies `resumed`, the agent replies `ok`, and Claude replies `finished` in the CLI turn the CLI starts when the agent has ended. The capture was recorded through a local proxy of the API that refused the agent's requests with HTTP 400 until the user's second message):
