## Permission prompts

### Request

A `can_use_tool` control request carries `request_id` and `request` {`tool_name`, `display_name`, `input`, `description`, `permission_suggestions`, `decision_reason`, `decision_reason_type`, `tool_use_id`, other fields}. The engine:

1. Keeps the request in memory as pending: request id, input and `permission_suggestions`.
2. Adds a `permissionRequest` item: `requestID` = `request_id`, `toolName` = `tool_name`, `input` = `input`, `reason` = `decision_reason` with ANSI escape sequences removed (null when absent), `decision` and `decidedBy` null.
3. Sets the state to waitingPermission.

A `permissionRequest` item is pending while its `decision` is null.

### Answer

`chats.answerPermission` {chatID, requestID, decision, message?} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswerpermission)) writes one line and replaces the item with `decision` and `decidedBy` = the name of the answering device:

| Decision      | Line written to stdin                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `allow`       | `{"type":"control_response","response":{"subtype":"success","request_id":"<id>","response":{"behavior":"allow","updatedInput":<request input>}}}`                                               |
| `allowAlways` | `{"type":"control_response","response":{"subtype":"success","request_id":"<id>","response":{"behavior":"allow","updatedInput":<request input>,"updatedPermissions":<permission_suggestions>}}}` |
| `deny`        | `{"type":"control_response","response":{"subtype":"success","request_id":"<id>","response":{"behavior":"deny","message":"<message>"}}}`                                                         |

- `allowAlways` without `permission_suggestions` (absent or empty) writes the `allow` line. The suggestions are passed unchanged; their `destination` decides how long the CLI keeps them.
- `<message>` is the trimmed `message` parameter, or `Permission denied by the user.` when it is empty or absent. Default chosen at design time. The CLI returns it to Claude as the tool's error result.
- When the last pending request of the chat is answered, the state returns to running.
- Several clients can show the same request: the first answer wins and the others get `conflict`. A client that gets this conflict shows no alert: the card follows the `chat.item` event.
- A change of the chat's permission mode answers the pending requests that the new mode allows, with the `allow` line ([Changing settings](#changing-settings)).

### Cancellation

A pending request is cancelled when the CLI sends `control_cancel_request` for it, when the process exits, or when the app quits: the item gets `decision` `deny` and `decidedBy` `null`, and no line is written. Answering a request that already has a decision fails with `conflict` (`This permission request was already answered.`). When the engine loads a transcript (`chats.get` or a launch) while the chat has no live process, every pending `permissionRequest` item is cancelled the same way. Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned permission card

While the chat has pending requests, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat:

- Header: `Claude wants to use <tool>`, where `<tool>` is the tool name, or the short name followed by a badge `MCP · <server>` for an MCP tool.
- Reason: the `reason`, in secondary text, when present.
- Every text of the card is selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`): the header, the reason and the input preview.
- Ticket and bundle ids are links in the header, the reason and the input preview, the `plan` of `ExitPlanMode` included, with the identifiers, the click and the window choice of [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files). In the monospace texts they keep the monospace font; every id link of the card has the style of the `plan`'s links, SwiftUI's link style.
- The `plan` of `ExitPlanMode`, rendered as Markdown, draws the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers as the question text of the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card) does. The header, the reason and the other previews are plain texts that read ids only, so they show a marker as its text.
- Input preview, at most 20 lines with a `Show All` button that shows the rest:

| Tool           | Preview                                                                                                 |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Bash`         | `command` in monospace, then `description`                                                              |
| `Write`        | `file_path` and `content`, in monospace                                                                 |
| `Edit`         | `file_path`, then `old_string` on a red background and `new_string` on a green background, in monospace |
| `ExitPlanMode` | `plan` rendered as Markdown                                                                             |
| `WebFetch`     | `url` in monospace, then `prompt`                                                                       |
| any other tool | `input` as JSON with 2-space indentation, in monospace                                                  |

Paths and URLs are in SF Mono, as in [Principles](ui.md#principles). When a field that a row needs is missing (`command`; `file_path` and `content`; `file_path`, `old_string` and `new_string`; `plan`; `url`), the preview is `input` as JSON, as for any other tool. Default chosen at design time.

- A single-line field `Message for Claude (optional)`, sent as `message` with Deny.
- Buttons: Allow Once (⌘Return) → `allow`; Always Allow (⌥⌘Return) → `allowAlways`; Deny (⇧⌘Return) → `deny`. The three keys act while the keyboard focus is in the chat view outside its composer, and only once the request shown has been on screen for 1 second; from the composer, and earlier, they do nothing. Default chosen at design time.
- With several pending requests, the card shows the oldest one and `1 of <n>`.
- A change of the Permissions menu answers the card when the new mode allows the request shown: Allow All approves every pending request, Accept Edits the file edits ([Changing settings](#changing-settings)). The card then shows the next pending request, or goes away when none is left.

### Mode changes made by the CLI

The chat's `settings.permissionMode` follows the CLI. When the engine answers `allowAlways` with suggestions that contain an entry `{"type":"setMode","mode":"<mode>"}`, or when a `system/status` line carries `permissionMode`, and that mode differs from the stored one, the engine stores it (the CLI value `default` stored as `manual`) and emits `chat.updated`. Default chosen at design time.

## Questions

Claude never invents unknowns, and asks only true unknowns ([Never invent](workflow.md#never-invent)): it asks with the MCP tool `ask_questions`, which calls `chats.postQuestions` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once. The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#questions-protocol) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common). To have the user choose an SF Symbol, Claude asks with `ask_symbol` instead ([Symbol requests](#symbol-requests)), to have the user choose a color, with `ask_color` ([Color requests](#color-requests)), to have the user choose a sound, in a Swift application project, with `ask_sound` ([Sound requests](#sound-requests)), to have the user choose a keyboard shortcut, in a Swift application project, with `ask_shortcut` ([Shortcut requests](#shortcut-requests)), to have the user choose a font, with `ask_font` ([Font requests](#font-requests)), and to have the user choose an animation, in a Swift application project, with `ask_animation` ([Animation requests](#animation-requests)).

### Posting questions

`chats.postQuestions` accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostquestions)). In an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it then fails with `forbidden` and `This chat implements a ticket and cannot ask questions: decide yourself and note the choice in the completion comment.`, before the other checks ([Implement and feedback chats](#implement-and-feedback-chats)), except for the one question set it accepts after a CI failure report ([Reports to the orchestrator](#reports-to-the-orchestrator)). Otherwise it checks these rules in this order and fails with `invalid` and the message of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#validation) on the first one broken:

| Rule                                                                                                                                                                  |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1 to 26 questions.                                                                                                                                                    |
| Question `n`, counting from 1, has the `n`-th letter of A–Z as `label`: A, B, C… in order.                                                                            |
| `question` is not empty after trimming.                                                                                                                               |
| 2 to 10 options per question.                                                                                                                                         |
| Option labels are not empty after trimming and are unique within the question, ignoring case.                                                                         |
| Each option label is one line of at most 200 characters ([Data model](data-model.md#questions-item)).                                                                 |
| `minChoices` and `maxChoices` appear only on a question whose `kind` is `multiple`.                                                                                   |
| A `single` question: `recommended` is one label, or a list of one label, equal to the `label` of one of the question's options, so exactly one option is recommended. |
| A `multiple` question: `recommended` is one label or a list of labels, at least one, distinct, each the `label` of one of the question's options.                     |
| `minChoices` and `maxChoices` are at least 1.                                                                                                                         |
| `minChoices`, 1 when absent, is at most `maxChoices`.                                                                                                                 |
| The number of recommended labels is at most `maxChoices`.                                                                                                             |

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`). The MCP tool checks its own schema first ([MCP](mcp.md#question-rules)).

A question without `kind` is `single` ([Data model](data-model.md#questions-item)). Once the set passes these rules, the engine moves each question's recommended options to the first places of its `options`, in their order among the options; the other options keep their relative order. It stores `recommended` as one label in a `single` question and as the list of the recommended labels, in that order, in a `multiple` question. Claude's prompts have no rule about the place of the recommended options: the engine does the reordering. Default chosen at design time for the order of several recommended options. The engine then adds a `questions` item {questionSetID (new uppercase UUID), questions in this order, answers `{}`, completed false} and returns the `QuestionSet` with the same order, so the pinned questions card, the transcript's Questions card and every other device list the recommended option first. When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers. Example of `questions`:

```json
[
  {"label":"A","question":"Should Command-W on a locked tab do nothing, or select the next tab?","options":[{"label":"Do nothing and beep","description":"The tab never closes, as its lock icon promises."},{"label":"Select the next tab","description":"Command-W still moves away from the locked tab."}],"recommended":"Do nothing and beep"},
  {"label":"B","question":"Should the beep come with a tooltip?","options":[{"label":"Yes, \"This tab is locked\""},{"label":"No"}],"recommended":"Yes, \"This tab is locked\""},
  {"label":"C","question":"Which tabs can be locked?","kind":"multiple","options":[{"label":"Web tabs"},{"label":"Editor tabs"},{"label":"Docs tabs"}],"recommended":["Web tabs","Editor tabs"],"maxChoices":3}
]
```

### Answering

`chats.answerQuestion` {chatID, questionSetID, label, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswerquestion)) stores one answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise).
- For a `multiple` question, `answer` is `Skipped` or one line `- <choice>` per choice, lines separated by `\n`, each choice not empty after trimming (`invalid` with `answer: write one line "- <choice>" per choice, or Skipped.` otherwise), and its number of lines is at least `minChoices`, 1 when absent, and at most `maxChoices` (`invalid` with `answer: the number of choices must be between minChoices and maxChoices.` otherwise). Default chosen at design time.
- `label` is the label of any question of the set (`invalid` otherwise): the questions are answered in any order, and answering a question that already has an answer replaces that answer.
- A completed set gives `conflict`. A client whose answer fails because the set was completed on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the completed set) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with `answers[label] = answer`. When every question of the set then has an answer, the item also gets `completed` true, and the engine delivers the Answers message as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)).
- Skipping a question stores the answer `Skipped` ([Pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card)). It counts as an answer: a set whose questions are all answered or skipped is completed.

Answers message, with the answers in label order, lines separated by `\n`; an answer keeps its own line breaks, and a skipped question reads `<label>: Skipped`:

```
Answers:
A: Do nothing and beep
B: Skipped
```

An answered `multiple` question reads `<label>:` followed by its stored answer on the next lines, one line `- <choice>` per choice: the labels of the checked options in option order, then the user's texts in field order. With the example of [Posting questions](#posting-questions):

```
Answers:
A: Do nothing and beep
B: Skipped
C:
- Web tabs
- Docs tabs
- Terminal tabs, once they exist
```

Claude leaves a skipped point out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A set stays incomplete across process exits and app restarts; answering its last unanswered question later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while a set is incomplete are delivered normally and do not change the set. Until every question of the set has an answer, the set stays pinned and Claude cannot post another set, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request, a font request or an animation request in this chat. Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned questions card

While the chat has a question set that is not completed, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. It shows one question of the set at a time. Each chat view chooses its displayed question on its own: when the card appears for a set, it displays the first question of the set, in label order, that has no answer.

- Header `Question <label> (<position> of <count>)`, where `<position>` is the displayed question's place in the set counting from 1, for example `Question A (1 of 1)` or `Question B (2 of 3)`.
- At the top right, previous and next arrow buttons, then a minimize button, a chevron pointing down. The arrows display the previous and the next question of the set, in label order, answered or not; they wrap at neither end: the previous arrow is disabled on the first question and the next arrow on the last one.
- The question text rendered as Markdown ([Block model](documentation-viewer.md#block-model)) with the six marker options of [Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup) and the `projectIdentifiers` of [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files). Its markers are drawn as in ticket descriptions ([Tickets](tickets.md#ticket-detail)): an SF Symbol marker of an available symbol shows the symbol's image (`Image(systemName:)`) followed by a space and its name, inline in the text; a color marker of an accepted color a swatch of the color followed by a space and its value; a font marker of a font that follows the [font notation](#font-notation) the text that the font card's rows read, drawn in its font at the size of the text around it; a shortcut marker its key caps as text; a sound marker its play button `▶`, a link that plays the sound, followed by its name ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)); an animation marker its expression; any other marker shows its text unchanged. Ticket and bundle ids of the question text are links: a click on one opens the ticket or the bundle as in [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files).
- One row per option: a radio button, then the label in bold and the description in secondary text. The label and the description are plain text that reads the six kinds of markers and draws them as the question text does ([Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup)), except that the `▶` of a sound marker is text, not a link: a click on it chooses the option. A click anywhere in the row (the radio button, the label, the `Recommended` tag, the description or the empty space) chooses the option. The label and the description are not selectable as text, and the ticket and bundle ids in them stay plain text, so that a click on one chooses the option too. The recommended option carries a `Recommended` tag.
- The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`); the texts of the option rows are not.
- A last radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines). A click on the radio button, the label `Answer another way` or the empty part of its line chooses this row; the field stays editable, and typing in it selects this row; Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- Selection when a question is displayed: a question without an answer appears with its recommended option selected and an empty field. An answered question appears with its answer selected: the option whose label equals the answer, or else `Answer another way` with the answer as its text; a skipped question thus shows `Answer another way` with the text `Skipped`.
- The main button, at the bottom right of the card, reads `Submit` when every question of the set other than the displayed one has an answer, a skipped question counting as answered: answering the displayed question then completes the set and sends the Answers message to Claude ([Answering](#answering)). On every other question it reads `Next`. The label depends on the answers, not on the position: in a set of 3 questions without answers, A and B show `Next`, and C shows `Submit` once A and B are answered; in a set of 2 questions, B displayed with the next arrow while A has no answer shows `Next`, and Next on B stores B's answer and displays A, which shows `Submit`. A set of one question shows `Submit`. The label follows the answers that arrive live through `chat.item`: when another view or another device answers the remaining questions, the button of the displayed question reads `Submit`. The rule is the same for `single` and `multiple` questions.
- Submit or Next (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized, whatever the button reads) calls `chats.answerQuestion` for the displayed question with the selected option's label, or with the field's text. It is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field. Submit or Next on an answered question replaces its answer. A failed call shows the alert `Submit failed` or `Next failed`, after the label the button showed ([Principles](ui.md#principles)).
- Skip, next to Submit or Next, calls `chats.answerQuestion` for the displayed question with the answer `Skipped`.
- After a successful Submit, Next or Skip, the card displays the next question after the displayed one, in label order, that has no answer, wrapping to the first question of the set; when every question has an answer the set is completed and the card disappears.
- The transcript's Questions card of the same chat view marks the displayed question with `➡️` before its label, and the mark moves when the card displays another question ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)).
- Answers given in another view or on another device appear live through `chat.item`. When one is for the displayed question, the card keeps displaying that question and selects the new answer.
- Minimize: the minimize button turns the card into a one-line collapsed bar above the composer, which shows the card's header text and a chevron pointing up. Clicking the bar opens the card again. The minimized state is kept per chat and applies to the question set that was minimized: a new question set of the chat opens the card. It is stored on this device only, never in the chat files and never synced, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)). Every view of the chat on this device shares it.

A `multiple` question changes the rows and the Submit or Next button of the card; the header, the arrows, the question text, the label of that button, Skip and the navigation stay the same:

- Under the question text, a line in secondary text gives the number of choices: `Choose <min> to <max>`, `Choose <n>` when `minChoices` equals `maxChoices`, or `Choose at least <min>` without `maxChoices`, `<min>` being 1 when `minChoices` is absent. For example `Choose 1 to 3`.
- Each option row has a checkbox in place of the radio button. A click anywhere in the row checks the option, or unchecks it when it is checked. Every recommended option carries the `Recommended` tag.
- Below the options, the label `Answer another way`, then one-line text fields, one below the other. When the last field gets text, a new empty field appears below it, so the user can add as many own answers as they want. A field whose text is deleted is removed, except the last field, so the list always ends with one empty field. Each field not empty after trimming holds one own answer, added to the checked options; a line break in a field's text becomes a space in the answer. Default chosen at design time for the layout and the line breaks.
- Each checked option and each field not empty after trimming counts as one choice.
- Selection when a question is displayed: a question without an answer appears with its recommended options checked and one empty field. An answered question appears with its answer split into lines, each line without its leading `- `: the options whose label equals a line are checked, and every other line fills a field, in order, followed by the empty field; a skipped question thus shows no checked option and a field with the text `Skipped`. Default chosen at design time.
- Submit or Next (⌘Return under the same conditions) calls `chats.answerQuestion` for the displayed question with one line `- <choice>` per choice, lines separated by `\n`: the labels of the checked options in option order, then the trimmed texts of the fields in field order. It is enabled while the number of choices is at least `minChoices`, 1 when absent, and at most `maxChoices`, without a maximum when `maxChoices` is absent. Submit or Next on an answered question replaces its answer.

## Symbol requests

When Claude needs the user to choose an SF Symbol, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_symbol`, which calls `chats.postSymbol` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_symbol)). The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#symbol-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting a symbol request

`chats.postSymbol` {chatID, question, suggestions, recommended} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostsymbol)); in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it then fails with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does, before the other checks ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)). It trims each suggestion and `recommended`, then checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#symbol-requests) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. A suggestion is valid when it is a name of the [symbol catalogue](#symbol-catalogue) of the main device, localized variants included.

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds a `symbol` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#symbol-item)) and returns the `SymbolRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#symbolrequest)). When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering a symbol request

`chats.answerSymbol` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswersymbol)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned symbol card](#pinned-symbol-card) sends `![](sf:<name>)` for a chosen symbol, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This symbol request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Symbol: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Symbol: ![](sf:line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle)
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another symbol request, a question set, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request, a font request or an animation request in this chat.

### Symbol catalogue

The SF Symbols of a Mac are read from the catalogue of its macOS, in `/System/Library/CoreServices/CoreGlyphs.bundle/Contents/Resources/`, the first time they are needed, and kept in memory until the app quits (`SFSymbolCatalog`, [Swift types](#swift-types)):

| File                      | What is read                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `name_availability.plist` | `symbols` maps each symbol name to its release, and `year_to_release` maps each release to its OS versions. A name belongs to the catalogue when the `macOS` version of its release is at most the running macOS version, or when its release gives no `macOS` version. |
| `symbol_search.plist`     | The search keywords of each name; a name without entry has none.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

- A localized variant is a name whose last dot-separated component is one of `ar`, `bn`, `el`, `gu`, `he`, `hi`, `ja`, `km`, `kn`, `ko`, `ml`, `mni`, `mr`, `my`, `or`, `pa`, `rtl`, `ru`, `sat`, `si`, `ta`, `te`, `th` and `zh`, for example `character.book.closed.ja`. Claude may suggest a localized variant; the search never lists one.
- `chats.postSymbol` checks the suggestions, and `projects.update` the project's icon ([Project settings](projects.md#project-settings)), against the catalogue of the main device. The pinned symbol card and the Icon popover of Project Settings search the catalogue of the Mac that shows the window, so a window showing a remote device searches the symbols of its own Mac, as it draws them ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)).
- When `name_availability.plist` cannot be read, the catalogue is empty: `ask_symbol` refuses every suggestion, `projects.update` refuses every icon, and the search lists nothing. When `symbol_search.plist` cannot be read, the names have no keywords. The engine logs each failure to `engine.log` the first time `chats.postSymbol` or `projects.update` uses the catalogue. Default chosen at design time.

### Symbol search

The search field of the [pinned symbol card](#pinned-symbol-card), and that of the Icon popover of [Project settings](projects.md#project-settings), search the catalogue as the user types:

1. The query is trimmed and lowercased. An empty query lists nothing; otherwise it is split at white space into words.
2. A name matches by name when it contains every word. It matches by keyword when it does not match by name and each word is contained in the name or in one of its keywords, compared in lowercase. For example `filter` matches `line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle` by keyword.
3. Localized variants are left out, and so are the request's suggestions in the pinned symbol card.
4. The results are the name matches, then the keyword matches, each group in alphabetical order of name. Their number is not limited. Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned symbol card

While the chat has a symbol request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. A chat has at most one waiting request, so the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned [color card](#pinned-color-card), the pinned [sound card](#pinned-sound-card), the pinned [shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card), the pinned [font card](#pinned-font-card) and the pinned [animation card](#pinned-animation-card) never show together.

- Header `Symbol`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- A search field with the placeholder `Search symbols` ([Symbol search](#symbol-search)).
- A grid of icons: the suggestions first, in the order Claude gave them, then the search results; while the search field is empty, the suggestions only. Each cell is 36 × 36 pt and shows the symbol's image (`Image(systemName:)`) centered, with the symbol's name as tooltip; a suggestion that the Mac showing the window has no symbol for shows `questionmark.square.dashed`. The grid has as many columns as fit the card's width, shows at most 4 rows, and scrolls vertically inside itself when it has more.
- The recommended suggestion's cell carries a star badge (`star.fill`) at its top trailing corner.
- A click on a cell selects its symbol: the cell gets a rounded background in the accent color. Below the grid, the selected symbol's name, followed by the `Recommended` tag of the pinned questions card when it is the recommended suggestion; the line is empty while `Answer another way` is selected. The names in the grid and below it are not selectable as text.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned questions card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row and deselects the selected cell; a click on a cell chooses that symbol again. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the recommended suggestion is selected, and the field and the search field are empty.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized) calls `chats.answerSymbol` with `![](sf:<name>)` for the selected symbol, or with the field's text. It is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerSymbol` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Symbol` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Symbol`, `Search symbols`, `Answer another way`, `Recommended`, Skip and Submit, the star badge, the grid size, the answer forms and the [search](#symbol-search) order and scope come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Color requests

When Claude needs the user to choose a color, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_color`, which calls `chats.postColor` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_color)). The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#color-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting a color request

`chats.postColor` {chatID, question, suggestions, recommended, lightAndDark} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostcolor)); in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it then fails with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does, before the other checks ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)). It trims each color of the suggestions and of `recommended`, then checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#color-requests) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. A color is valid when it is a hex color `#RRGGBB`, its six hex digits in either letter case, or a name of [System colors](#system-colors), with its letter case. The check is the same on every device: it does not depend on the colors a Mac can draw.

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds a `color` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, lightAndDark (false when absent), suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#color-item)) and returns the `ColorRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#colorrequest)). The colors are stored trimmed, as Claude wrote them otherwise. When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering a color request

`chats.answerColor` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswercolor)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned color card](#pinned-color-card) sends `![](color:<color>)` for one color, `Light ![](color:<color>), Dark ![](color:<color>)` for a light and dark pair, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This color request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Color: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Color: Light ![](color:#FFFFFF), Dark ![](color:#1C1C1E)
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another color request, a question set, a symbol request, a sound request, a shortcut request, a font request or an animation request in this chat.

### System colors

The system color names are the SwiftUI standard colors and the UIKit and AppKit system colors, in this order, which is also the order of the [pinned color card](#pinned-color-card)'s grid (`SystemColors`, [Swift types](#swift-types)):

| Group                                                                                 | Names                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| SwiftUI standard colors                                                               | `red`, `orange`, `yellow`, `green`, `mint`, `teal`, `cyan`, `blue`, `indigo`, `purple`, `pink`, `brown`, `gray`, `black`, `white`, `primary`, `secondary`, `accentColor`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| UIKit and AppKit standard colors                                                      | `systemRed`, `systemOrange`, `systemYellow`, `systemGreen`, `systemMint`, `systemTeal`, `systemCyan`, `systemBlue`, `systemIndigo`, `systemPurple`, `systemPink`, `systemBrown`, `systemGray`, `systemGray2`, `systemGray3`, `systemGray4`, `systemGray5`, `systemGray6`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| UIKit semantic colors, by their UIKit name                                            | `label`, `secondaryLabel`, `tertiaryLabel`, `quaternaryLabel`, `placeholderText`, `link`, `separator`, `opaqueSeparator`, `systemFill`, `secondarySystemFill`, `tertiarySystemFill`, `quaternarySystemFill`, `systemBackground`, `secondarySystemBackground`, `tertiarySystemBackground`, `systemGroupedBackground`, `secondarySystemGroupedBackground`, `tertiarySystemGroupedBackground`, `tintColor`, `darkText`, `lightText`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| AppKit semantic colors that UIKit lacks, by their AppKit name with its `Color` suffix | `quinaryLabelColor`, `quinarySystemFillColor`, `textColor`, `textBackgroundColor`, `textInsertionPointColor`, `selectedTextColor`, `selectedTextBackgroundColor`, `unemphasizedSelectedTextColor`, `unemphasizedSelectedTextBackgroundColor`, `keyboardFocusIndicatorColor`, `windowBackgroundColor`, `windowFrameTextColor`, `underPageBackgroundColor`, `controlBackgroundColor`, `controlAccentColor`, `controlColor`, `controlTextColor`, `selectedControlColor`, `selectedControlTextColor`, `disabledControlTextColor`, `alternateSelectedControlTextColor`, `selectedContentBackgroundColor`, `unemphasizedSelectedContentBackgroundColor`, `selectedMenuItemTextColor`, `headerTextColor`, `gridColor`, `findHighlightColor`, `highlightColor`, `shadowColor` |

- The fixed colors of UIKit and AppKit (`darkGrayColor`, `magentaColor`, `clearColor`…), their deprecated colors, `scrubberTexturedBackgroundColor`, a pattern, and `alternatingContentBackgroundColors`, a list, are not system color names.
- A Mac draws a system color as it appears in the window's light or dark appearance. A SwiftUI name takes the AppKit color SwiftUI draws on the Mac: `NSColor.systemRed` for `red` and so on, `systemGray` for `gray`, `labelColor` for `primary`, `secondaryLabelColor` for `secondary` and `controlAccentColor` for `accentColor`; `black` and `white` are fixed. A UIKit name takes the AppKit color of the same name, with the `Color` suffix in AppKit (`label` is `NSColor.labelColor`), and an AppKit name its own color.
- The UIKit names that AppKit lacks take UIKit's values on iOS, from this table; `tintColor` takes `controlAccentColor`.

| Name                               | Light                     | Dark                      |
| ---------------------------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------------- |
| `systemGray2`                      | `#AEAEB2`                 | `#636366`                 |
| `systemGray3`                      | `#C7C7CC`                 | `#48484A`                 |
| `systemGray4`                      | `#D1D1D6`                 | `#3A3A3C`                 |
| `systemGray5`                      | `#E5E5EA`                 | `#2C2C2E`                 |
| `systemGray6`                      | `#F2F2F7`                 | `#1C1C1E`                 |
| `opaqueSeparator`                  | `#C6C6C8`                 | `#38383A`                 |
| `systemBackground`                 | `#FFFFFF`                 | `#000000`                 |
| `secondarySystemBackground`        | `#F2F2F7`                 | `#1C1C1E`                 |
| `tertiarySystemBackground`         | `#FFFFFF`                 | `#2C2C2E`                 |
| `systemGroupedBackground`          | `#F2F2F7`                 | `#000000`                 |
| `secondarySystemGroupedBackground` | `#FFFFFF`                 | `#1C1C1E`                 |
| `tertiarySystemGroupedBackground`  | `#F2F2F7`                 | `#2C2C2E`                 |
| `darkText`                         | `#000000`                 | `#000000`                 |
| `lightText`                        | `#FFFFFF` at 60 % opacity | `#FFFFFF` at 60 % opacity |

The names come from the user's choice of the SwiftUI, UIKit and AppKit colors and of the `Color` suffix for the AppKit names; the exclusions, the order and the drawing on the Mac are Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned color card

While the chat has a color request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. A chat has at most one waiting request, so the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned color card, the pinned [sound card](#pinned-sound-card), the pinned [shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card), the pinned [font card](#pinned-font-card) and the pinned [animation card](#pinned-animation-card) never show together. The card holds one color, or two rows `Light` and `Dark` while its toggle `Light and dark` is on.

- Header `Color`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- A grid of swatches: the suggestions first, in the order Claude gave them, then every name of [System colors](#system-colors) in its order, except the names that are a suggestion. Its cells, columns and rows are those of the symbol grid: 36 × 36 pt cells, as many columns as fit the card's width, at most 4 rows visible, scrolling vertically inside itself when it has more ([Pinned symbol card](#pinned-symbol-card)).
- Each cell shows a swatch, 28 × 28 pt with corners of radius 6 and a 1 pt border of the secondary color at 30 % opacity, centered, with the color as tooltip: the hex or the name. A system color is drawn as [System colors](#system-colors) says, in the window's appearance.
- A pair suggestion shows a split swatch, its light color above the diagonal from the bottom leading corner to the top trailing corner and its dark color below it, with the tooltip `Light <color>, Dark <color>`. While the toggle is off, it shows and gives its light color only, with that color as tooltip.
- The recommended suggestion's cell carries a star badge (`star.fill`) at its top trailing corner.
- A cell whose color equals the color of the card, or of the selected row while the toggle is on, has a rounded background in the accent color; a pair cell has it while the toggle is on and its two colors equal the two rows. Colors are compared as in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#color-requests). While `Answer another way` is selected, no cell has it.
- The toggle `Light and dark` (a switch with this label). When the card appears for a request, it is on when the request has `lightAndDark` true and off otherwise.
- While the toggle is off, one row: the color's swatch and color panel button, then the hex field. While it is on, two rows labelled `Light` and `Dark`, each with its label, its swatch and color panel button, and its hex field. One of the two rows is selected and has a rounded background in the accent color at 15 % opacity; a click on a row, its label, its swatch or its hex field selects it. The Light row is selected when the toggle turns on, and when the card appears for a pair request.
- The swatch and color panel button of a row are a macOS color well in its expanded style (`NSColorWell.Style.expanded`): the swatch shows the row's color, and its button opens the macOS color panel, which then edits that row. The panel shows no opacity slider while the card's well is active. A color chosen in the panel or the well is converted to sRGB, its opacity is dropped, and the row takes it as a hex color `#RRGGBB` in uppercase.
- The hex field of a row shows the row's color: its hex in uppercase for a hex color, its name for a system color. Typing in it gives the row the trimmed text once it is a hex color `#RRGGBB`, in either letter case, written back in uppercase when the field loses the focus, or a name of [System colors](#system-colors). The well, the panel and the field show the same color at all times.
- A click on a single color, a suggestion of a one-color request or a system color, sets the color of the card while the toggle is off and the color of the selected row while it is on. A click on a pair suggestion sets both rows while the toggle is on, and the color of the card, to its light color, while the toggle is off.
- Turning the toggle on gives both rows the color of the card. Turning it off gives the card the color of the Light row.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned symbol card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row; a click on a cell, a well or a hex field, or turning the toggle, chooses the color again. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the color is chosen, the card's color is the recommended suggestion (for a pair request the Light and Dark rows are its two colors), and the field is empty.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized) calls `chats.answerColor` with `![](color:<color>)` while the toggle is off, `Light ![](color:<light>), Dark ![](color:<dark>)` while it is on, or the field's text. A hex color is written in uppercase. Submit is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field, and while a hex field shown holds neither a hex color nor a system color name.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerColor` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Color` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Color`, `Light and dark`, `Light`, `Dark`, `Answer another way`, Skip and Submit, the star badge, the grid of every system color laid out as the symbol grid, the split swatch of a pair, the color panel and the hex field kept in sync, the hex field showing the name of a system color, the toggle's start, its effect on the clicks and on the pair suggestions, and the answer forms come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Sound requests

When, in a project whose type is Swift application ([Project types](projects.md#project-types)), Claude needs the user to choose a sound for an app of an Apple platform, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_sound`, which calls `chats.postSound` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_sound)). The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#sound-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting a sound request

`chats.postSound` {chatID, question, suggestions, recommended} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostsound)), and only in a chat whose project has the type `swiftApplication`: in a project of another type it fails with `forbidden` (`ask_sound is only available in Swift application projects.`) and nothing is shown to the user. Before that check, in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it fails with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)). It then trims each suggestion and `recommended`, checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#sound-requests) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. A suggestion is valid when it is a name of the [sound catalogue](#sound-catalogue) of the main device for the chat's project.

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds a `sound` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#sound-item)) and returns the `SoundRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#soundrequest)). When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering a sound request

`chats.answerSound` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswersound)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned sound card](#pinned-sound-card) sends `![](sound:<name>)` for a chosen sound, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This sound request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Sound: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Sound: ![](sound:iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf)
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another sound request, a symbol request, a color request, a shortcut request, a font request, an animation request or a question set in this chat.

### Sound catalogue

The sound catalogue of a project is the list of the sound names that `chats.postSound` accepts, that the [sound search](#sound-search) lists and that `sounds.read` reads ([Engine API](engine-api.md#sounds)). The engine of the main device builds it from three sources (`SoundCatalog`, [Swift types](#swift-types)):

| Source            | Files                                                                                                            | Name                                         | Example                                                     |
| ----------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- |
| macOS             | the files directly in `/System/Library/Sounds`                                                                   | `macOS/<file name>`                          | `macOS/Glass.aiff`                                          |
| Simulator runtime | the files under `<runtime root>/System/Library/Audio/UISounds` of each installed Simulator runtime, at any depth | `<platform> <version>/<path under UISounds>` | `iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf`, `iOS 26.1/New/Anticipate.caf` |
| Project           | the files of the project's code search [file list](search.md#file-list)                                          | `Project/<path from the project root>`       | `Project/Sounds/alert.caf`                                  |

- A sound is a regular file whose extension is `.caf`, `.aiff`, `.wav` or `.mp3`, in any letter case. In `/System/Library/Sounds` and the runtime folders, names that start with `.` and symbolic links are skipped.
- The part of a name before its first `/` is the sound's source: `macOS`, the runtime's `<platform> <version>`, or `Project`; the part after it is the sound's file.
- Simulator runtimes: the engine runs `/usr/bin/xcrun simctl list runtimes --json` with the environment of [Child processes](architecture.md#child-processes) and a timeout of 30 s, and reads the `name` and the `runtimeRoot` of each entry of `runtimes`. The name is the runtime's `<platform> <version>`, for example `iOS 26.1`, `watchOS 26.1`, `tvOS 26.1` or `visionOS 26.1`. When two runtimes have the same name, only the first one listed is read. When `xcrun` fails to start, exits with a non-zero status, times out or prints no readable JSON, for example on a Mac without Xcode, the catalogue has no runtime sounds.
- Project: the files listed for the root folder of the chat's project on the main device (`rootPath`), also for an orchestrated ticket chat that works in a worktree ([Working directory](#working-directory)). The code search file list leaves out the files git ignores and the folders of sub projects.
- Order: the macOS sounds, then the sounds of each runtime in the order of the runtime names, then the project's sounds; the runtime names and the paths within each source in Finder order (`localizedStandardCompare`).
- A name that holds a line break or parentheses that do not balance, or that starts or ends with white space, is left out, since a [sound marker](#links-images-and-files) could not carry it.
- The macOS and runtime sounds are read the first time the engine needs the catalogue and kept in memory until the app quits, so a runtime installed later appears after the next launch. The project's sounds are read again at each `chats.postSound`, `sounds.list` and `sounds.read` call. The engine logs a failed `xcrun` run to `engine.log` the first time it reads the runtimes.
- A window that shows a remote device searches and plays the sounds of the main device: the pinned sound card gets the catalogue with `sounds.list`, and every sound is played through `sounds.read` ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)).

The three sources and the forms of the names come from the user; the folders read, the `xcrun` command, the order and the reading times are Default chosen at design time.

### Sound search

The search field of the [pinned sound card](#pinned-sound-card) searches the catalogue as the user types:

1. The query is trimmed and lowercased. An empty query lists nothing; otherwise it is split at white space into words.
2. A sound matches when its name, lowercased, contains every word. The name includes the source, so `ios 26.1 sms` lists the iOS 26.1 sounds whose path contains `sms`, and `project` the project's sounds.
3. The request's suggestions are left out.
4. The results keep the order of the catalogue. Their number is not limited.

The search rules are Default chosen at design time.

### Sound playback

The pinned sound card and the sound markers of every Markdown view of the app ([Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup)) play a sound the same way:

- The window calls `sounds.read` with the chat's project, or for a marker outside a chat view the project of its view ([Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup)), and the sound's name through its engine connection ([Engine API](engine-api.md#soundsread)), so a window that shows a remote device plays the file of the main device.
- It plays the data with `NSSound(data:)` on the Mac that shows the window. One sound plays at a time in the window's app: starting a sound, from any view, stops the one that plays.
- A failed call shows the alert `Play Sound failed` with the error's message; data that `NSSound` cannot read shows it with the message `The sound cannot be played.` ([UI › Principles](ui.md#principles)).

The player and the alert are Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned sound card

While the chat has a sound request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. A chat never has two requests waiting together, so the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned [color card](#pinned-color-card), the pinned sound card, the pinned [shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card), the pinned [font card](#pinned-font-card) and the pinned [animation card](#pinned-animation-card) never show together.

- Header `Sound`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- A search field with the placeholder `Search sounds` ([Sound search](#sound-search)). When the card appears for a request, it calls `sounds.list` for the chat's project; until the list arrives, and when the call fails, the search lists nothing.
- A list: the suggestions first, in the order Claude gave them, then the search results; while the search field is empty, the suggestions only. Each row is one line: the sound's file, then ` · ` and its source in secondary text, for example `sms-received1.caf · iOS 26.1`, `Glass.aiff · macOS` or `Sounds/alert.caf · Project`, with the sound's name as tooltip. The list shows at most 8 rows and scrolls vertically inside itself when it has more.
- The recommended suggestion's row carries a star (`star.fill`) at its trailing end.
- A click on a row selects its sound and plays it ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)), also when the row is already selected: the selected row gets a rounded background in the accent color. The texts of the rows are not selectable.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned symbol card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row and deselects the selected row; a click on a row chooses that sound again. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the recommended suggestion is selected without playing, and the field and the search field are empty.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized) calls `chats.answerSound` with `![](sound:<name>)` for the selected sound, or with the field's text. It is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerSound` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Sound` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Sound`, `Search sounds`, `Answer another way`, Skip and Submit, the row texts, the star, the preselection, the playback on selection, the answer forms and the scope of the search come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Shortcut requests

In a project whose [type](projects.md#project-types) is Swift application (`swiftApplication`), when Claude needs the user to choose a keyboard shortcut, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_shortcut`, which calls `chats.postShortcut` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_shortcut)). The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#shortcut-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting a shortcut request

`chats.postShortcut` {chatID, question, suggestions, recommended, inUse} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostshortcut)); in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it then fails with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)). In a chat whose project does not have the type `swiftApplication`, it fails with `forbidden` and `ask_shortcut is available only in Swift application projects.`. It then trims each suggestion, `recommended`, and each `shortcut` and `command` of `inUse`, checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#shortcut-requests) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. A suggestion, and the `shortcut` of an entry of `inUse`, is valid when it is written in the [shortcut notation](#shortcut-notation).

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds a `shortcut` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, inUse in Claude's order, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#shortcut-item)) and returns the `ShortcutRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#shortcutrequest)). When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering a shortcut request

`chats.answerShortcut` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswershortcut)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card) sends `![](shortcut:<shortcut>)` for a chosen or recorded shortcut, written as [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files) says, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This shortcut request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Shortcut: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Shortcut: ![](shortcut:⇧⌘K)
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another shortcut request, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a font request, an animation request or a question set in this chat.

### Shortcut notation

A shortcut is written as macOS menus write it: its modifiers, then its key, with nothing between them, for example `⇧⌘K`, `⌃⌥K`, `⌘↩`, `⎋` or `⌃Space` (`KeyShortcut`, [Swift types](#swift-types)).

- The modifiers are `⌃` (Control), `⌥` (Option), `⇧` (Shift) and `⌘` (Command), each at most once, in this order: `⇧⌘K` is a shortcut, `⌘⇧K` is not. A shortcut may have no modifier, for example `⎋` or `F5`.
- The key is exactly one of the keys of this table:

| Key                                                  | Written                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A letter                                             | The letter in uppercase, for example `K`; a lowercase letter is refused.                                                                                                                              |
| A digit, a punctuation mark, any other character key | The character the key types without Shift or Option in the keyboard layout in use, for example `1`, `/`, `,` or `[`. With Shift, `⇧` is written before the key: Shift-Command-/ is `⇧⌘/`, never `⌘?`. |
| Return, keypad Enter                                 | `↩`, `⌤`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Escape                                               | `⎋`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Tab                                                  | `⇥`                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Space bar                                            | `Space`                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Delete, Forward Delete                               | `⌫`, `⌦`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Left, Right, Up and Down Arrow                       | `←`, `→`, `↑`, `↓`                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Home, End                                            | `↖`, `↘`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Page Up, Page Down                                   | `⇞`, `⇟`                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Function keys                                        | `F1` to `F20`                                                                                                                                                                                         |

- A character key is one character that is not white space, a control or private-use character or one of `⌃`, `⌥`, `⇧` and `⌘`, and that stays the same in uppercase; the glyphs of the table are such characters.
- Two shortcuts are the same when they are written the same.
- A shortcut is drawn as key caps: one key cap per modifier and one for the key, in the order written, so `⇧⌘K` gives the key caps `⇧`, `⌘` and `K`, and `⌃Space` gives `⌃` and `Space`.

The glyphs of macOS menus, the strict order of the modifiers, the uppercase letters, the unshifted key written with `⇧`, and the keys of the table come from the user; the character-key rule is Default chosen at design time.

### Standard macOS shortcuts

The [pinned shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card) warns when the selected shortcut is one of the standard macOS shortcuts below (`StandardShortcuts`, [Swift types](#swift-types)). The list is fixed and the same on every Mac. It is taken from Apple's article [Mac keyboard shortcuts](https://support.apple.com/en-us/102650): its sections Cut, copy, paste, and other common shortcuts; Sleep, log out, and shut down shortcuts; and Accessibility shortcuts, plus the entries of its section Finder and system shortcuts that act in every app: Mission Control, the application windows, the input sources, the Dock and Siri. The combinations that need Fn or Globe, the Power button, a media key or the mouse cannot be written in the [notation](#shortcut-notation) and are left out, and a shortcut that the article lists twice appears once. The sections come from the user; each action is a short name of Apple's description, Default chosen at design time.

| Shortcut  | Action                    |
| --------- | ------------------------- |
| `⌘X`      | Cut                       |
| `⌘C`      | Copy                      |
| `⌘V`      | Paste                     |
| `⌘Z`      | Undo                      |
| `⇧⌘Z`     | Redo                      |
| `⌘A`      | Select All                |

| `⇧⌘G`     | Find Previous             |
| `⌘H`      | Hide                      |
| `⌥⌘H`     | Hide Others               |
| `⌘M`      | Minimize                  |
| `⌥⌘M`     | Minimize All              |
| `⌘O`      | Open                      |
| `⌘P`      | Print                     |
| `⌘Q`      | Quit                      |
| `⌘S`      | Save                      |
| `⌘T`      | New Tab                   |
| `⌘W`      | Close Window              |
| `⌥⌘W`     | Close All Windows         |
| `⌥⌘⎋`     | Force Quit                |
| `⌘Space`  | Spotlight                 |
| `⌥⌘Space` | Spotlight in Finder       |
| `⌃⌘Space` | Character Viewer          |
| `⌃⌘F`     | Full Screen               |
| `Space`   | Quick Look                |
| `⌘⇥`      | Switch Apps               |
| ⌘&#96;    | Switch Windows            |
| `⇧⌘3`     | Screenshot                |
| `⇧⌘4`     | Screenshot                |
| `⇧⌘5`     | Screenshot or Recording   |
| `⇧⌘N`     | New Folder                |
| `⌃⌘N`     | New Folder with Selection |
| `⌘,`      | Settings                  |
| `⌃⌘Q`     | Lock Screen               |
| `⇧⌘Q`     | Log Out                   |
| `⌥⇧⌘Q`    | Log Out Immediately       |
| `⌥⌘D`     | Show or Hide Dock         |
| `⌃↑`      | Mission Control           |
| `⌃↓`      | Application Windows       |
| `⇧⌘6`     | Ask Siri About Selection  |
| `⇧⌘Space` | Ask Siri About Window     |
| `⌃Space`  | Previous Input Source     |
