| `⌃⌥Space` | Next Input Source         |
| `⌃⌥⌘8`    | Invert Colors             |
| `⌃⌥⌘,`    | Reduce Contrast           |
| `⌃⌥⌘.`    | Increase Contrast         |
| `⌃F2`     | Focus Menu Bar            |
| `⌃F3`     | Focus Dock                |
| `⌃F4`     | Focus Window              |
| `⌃F5`     | Focus Toolbar             |
| `⌃F6`     | Focus Floating Window     |
| `⌃⇧F6`    | Focus Previous Panel      |
| `⌃F7`     | Tab Focus Mode            |
| `⌃F8`     | Focus Status Menu         |
| ⇧⌘&#96;   | Previous Window           |
| ⌥⌘&#96;   | Focus Window Drawer       |
| `⇥`       | Next Control              |
| `⇧⇥`      | Previous Control          |
| `⌃⇥`      | Next Control              |
| `⌃⇧⇥`     | Previous Control Group    |
| `←`       | Move to Adjacent Item     |
| `→`       | Move to Adjacent Item     |
| `↑`       | Move to Adjacent Item     |
| `↓`       | Move to Adjacent Item     |
| `⌃←`      | Move to Adjacent Control  |
| `⌃→`      | Move to Adjacent Control  |
| `⌥⌘F5`    | Accessibility Shortcuts   |

### Shortcut recorder

The recorder field of the [pinned shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card) records a key combination the user presses:

- The field shows its recorded shortcut as key caps, or while it has none the placeholder `Record Shortcut` in secondary color.
- A click on the field selects it, as a click on a suggestion selects that suggestion, and starts recording. Selected, the field has the rounded accent background of a selected suggestion; while it records, it has the text background color and a 2 pt border in the accent color instead.
- While the field records, every key press of the window goes to it: no menu item, no other shortcut of the app, ⌘Return of the cards included, and no other control of the window receives it. A key press of a modifier alone (`⌃`, `⌥`, `⇧`, `⌘`, Caps Lock, Fn) records nothing.
- The first key press of another key records the shortcut made of that key and of the modifiers held among `⌃`, `⌥`, `⇧` and `⌘`, written in the [notation](#shortcut-notation); Caps Lock and Fn are not part of it. The field shows the shortcut as key caps, recording stops, and the shortcut becomes the card's selected shortcut. Escape, Return, Tab, Space and the arrows are recorded as any other key, with or without modifiers.
- A key that the notation cannot write, such as Help or a keypad Clear key, records nothing, and recording goes on.
- A click on the field while it has a shortcut starts a new recording; the shortcut stays shown and selected until the next combination replaces it.
- A click elsewhere in the window, or the window losing its key status, stops recording; the field keeps its shortcut.
- The combinations that macOS handles before any app, such as `⌘⇥`, never reach the field: macOS acts on them. The user can still choose such a shortcut among the suggestions, or give it with `Answer another way`.

The placeholder `Record Shortcut` and the recording of every combination, ⌘Return included, come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

### Shortcut warnings

While a shortcut is selected in the card, a suggestion or the shortcut of the recorder field, the card shows below the recorder field one line per use of that shortcut:

- `Used by macOS: <action>` when it is a [standard macOS shortcut](#standard-macos-shortcuts), `<action>` being its action, for example `Used by macOS: Spotlight` for `⌘Space`;
- `Used by the app: <command>` when it is the `shortcut` of an entry of the request's `inUse`, `<command>` being that entry's command, for example `Used by the app: New Ticket` for `⌘N`.

Both lines show when both apply, the macOS line first. Each line starts with the symbol `exclamationmark.triangle.fill`, and the symbol and the text are in the orange system color. No line shows while `Answer another way` is selected or while the recorder field is selected without a shortcut. A warning does not block: Submit stays enabled, and Claude receives the shortcut. The texts and the non-blocking rule come from the user; the symbol and the color are Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned shortcut card

While the chat has a shortcut request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. A chat never has two requests waiting together, so the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned [color card](#pinned-color-card), the pinned [sound card](#pinned-sound-card), the pinned shortcut card, the pinned [font card](#pinned-font-card) and the pinned [animation card](#pinned-animation-card) never show together.

- Header `Shortcut`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- The suggestions, in the order Claude gave them, each a button that shows the shortcut's key caps, with the shortcut as tooltip; the buttons wrap onto as many rows as needed to fit the card's width. The recommended suggestion's button carries a star badge (`star.fill`) at its top trailing corner. A click on a button selects its shortcut: the button gets a rounded background in the accent color. The key caps are not selectable as text.
- The recorder field ([Shortcut recorder](#shortcut-recorder)), then the [warnings](#shortcut-warnings) of the selected shortcut.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned questions card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row and deselects the selected suggestion or recorder field; a click on a suggestion or on the recorder field chooses it again. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the recommended suggestion is selected, the recorder field has no shortcut and does not record, and the answer field is empty.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown, the card is not minimized and the recorder field does not record) calls `chats.answerShortcut` with `![](shortcut:<shortcut>)` for the selected suggestion or the recorder field's shortcut, or with the field's text. It is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field, and while the recorder field is selected without a shortcut.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerShortcut` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Shortcut` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Shortcut`, `Record Shortcut`, `Answer another way`, Skip and Submit, the key caps, the star badge, the preselected recommended suggestion and the answer forms come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Font requests

When Claude needs the user to choose a font, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_font`, which calls `chats.postFont` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_font)). The rules Claude follows are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#font-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting a font request

`chats.postFont` {chatID, question, sample, suggestions, recommended} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostfont)); in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, it then fails with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does, before the other checks ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)). It trims `sample`, each suggestion and `recommended`, then checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#font-requests) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. A suggestion is valid when it follows the [font notation](#font-notation) and is a font of the [font catalogue](#font-catalogue) of the main device for the chat's project.

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds a `font` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, sample, suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#font-item)) and returns the `FontRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#fontrequest)). When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering a font request

`chats.answerFont` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsanswerfont)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned font card](#pinned-font-card) sends `![](font:<font>)` for a chosen font, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This font request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Font: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Font: ![](font:System Rounded/Semibold/17)
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another font request, a question set, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request or an animation request in this chat.

### Font notation

A font is written in one of two forms (`FontNotation`, [Swift types](#swift-types)):

| Form        | Examples                                                 | Meaning                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----------- | -------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Text style  | `.headline`                                              | A Dynamic Type text style: `.largeTitle`, `.title`, `.title2`, `.title3`, `.headline`, `.subheadline`, `.body`, `.callout`, `.footnote`, `.caption` or `.caption2`. |
| Family font | `System Rounded/Semibold/17`, `Avenir Next/Demi Bold/15` | `<family>/<weight>/<size>`: a family of the [font catalogue](#font-catalogue), one of the family's weights and a size in points.                                    |

- A text that starts with `.` is a text style and must be one of the eleven. Any other text is a family font: it has exactly three parts separated by `/`, the family and the weight not empty. Family and weight names are written as the catalogue names them, spaces and letter case included.
- The size is 1 to 999 points with at most one decimal: digits without a leading zero, then optionally `.` and one digit. `17`, `15.5` and `17.0` are sizes; `0.5`, `1000`, `15.25`, `15,5` and `017` are not.
- A font reads `<family> · <weight> · <size> pt` for a family font, its size as written (`System Rounded · Semibold · 17 pt`), and its notation for a text style (`.headline`). The [pinned font card](#pinned-font-card), the transcript's `Font` card and the font markers ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)) show fonts this way.
- A text style has the size and weight of its macOS Dynamic Type style, in the system font: `.headline` is bold, `.caption2` medium, the others regular.

The sizes come from the user; the other details of the notation, except the forms and examples of the table, are Default chosen at design time.

### Font catalogue

The font catalogue of a project on a Mac holds these entries (`FontCatalog`, [Swift types](#swift-types)):

| Entry              | Names                                                                                                                                     | Weights                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| System designs     | `System`, `System Rounded`, `System Serif` and `System Monospaced`: the system font in its default, rounded, serif and monospaced designs | The nine SwiftUI weights `Ultralight`, `Thin`, `Light`, `Regular`, `Medium`, `Semibold`, `Bold`, `Heavy` and `Black`                                                                         |
| Installed families | Every font family installed on the Mac, as CoreText names it (`CTFontManagerCopyAvailableFontFamilyNames`)                                | The style names of the family's faces, as macOS names them, italic faces included: for example `Regular`, `Italic`, `Ultra Light`, `Medium`, `Demi Bold`, `Bold` and `Heavy` for Avenir Next |
| Project font files | The family name of each font of the project's font files                                                                                  | The style names of those fonts                                                                                                                                                               |
| Text styles        | The eleven text styles of the [font notation](#font-notation)                                                                             | none                                                                                                                                                                                         |

- The project's font files are the files of the project's [code search file list](search.md#file-list) whose name ends in `.ttf`, `.otf` or `.ttc`, in any letter case. A collection (`.ttc`) gives every font it holds. A file that CoreText cannot read is skipped.
- A project family with the name of an installed family is the same entry: the faces of the project's files are added to the installed ones, except a face whose style name the installed family already has.
- An installed or project family named like a system design is left out, so the four names always mean the system designs. Default chosen at design time.
- Each weight has a value from -1 to 1: for the system designs, the value of the SwiftUI weight (Ultralight -0.8, Thin -0.6, Light -0.4, Regular 0, Medium 0.23, Semibold 0.3, Bold 0.4, Heavy 0.56, Black 0.62); for a face, the CoreText weight trait of its font. A family's weights are listed by value, then upright faces before italic ones, then by name. Default chosen at design time.
- `chats.postFont` checks the suggestions against the catalogue of the main device: its installed families and the font files of the chat's project. The installed families are read the first time they are needed and kept in memory until the app quits; the project's font files are read at each call. Default chosen at design time.
- The [pinned font card](#pinned-font-card) and the font markers of every Markdown view of the app ([Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup)) use the families installed on the Mac that shows the window and the project families of the main device, which `files.fonts` returns ([Engine API](engine-api.md#filesfonts)); they read the files of a project family with `files.read` to draw it. A window showing a remote device thus lists and draws the families installed on its own Mac and the project's font files of the main device. Default chosen at design time.

### Font search

The search field of the [pinned font card](#pinned-font-card) searches the catalogue as the user types:

1. The query is trimmed and lowercased, then split at white space into words.
2. A family matches when its name, lowercased, contains every word; a text style when its notation, lowercased, contains every word, for example `.largetitle` for `large`. An empty query matches every family and every text style.
3. The text styles that are suggestions of the request are left out. A family is listed even when a suggestion uses it, since selecting it keeps the weight and size.
4. The results are the system designs in the order `System`, `System Rounded`, `System Serif`, `System Monospaced`, then the other families in alphabetical order (`localizedStandardCompare`), then the text styles in the order of the [font notation](#font-notation). Their number is not limited.

The listing of every font for an empty query and the order come from the user; the matching rules are Default chosen at design time.

### Nearest weight

Selecting a family of the search results keeps the weight of the `Weight` menu when the family has a weight of that name. Otherwise the menu takes the family's weight whose value ([Font catalogue](#font-catalogue)) is nearest the value of the current weight in the family it was chosen in; on a tie, an upright face before an italic one, then the first in the family's order. For example, with `Semibold` (0.3) of a system design selected, choosing Avenir Next selects `Demi Bold` (0.3). The rule comes from the user; the tie rule is Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned font card

While the chat has a font request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. A chat has at most one waiting request, so the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned [color card](#pinned-color-card), the pinned [sound card](#pinned-sound-card), the pinned [shortcut card](#pinned-shortcut-card), the pinned font card and the pinned [animation card](#pinned-animation-card) never show together.

- Header `Font`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- A search field with the placeholder `Search fonts` ([Font search](#font-search)).
- A list of one-line rows: the suggestions first, in the order Claude gave them, then the search results. A family suggestion reads `<family> · <weight> · <size> pt`, for example `System Rounded · Semibold · 17 pt`, drawn in its family and weight at the card's text size; a text style reads its notation, for example `.headline`, drawn in that style; a family of the search results reads its name, drawn in its `Regular` weight, or in its weight whose value is nearest 0 when it has none. A font that the Mac showing the window cannot draw is drawn in the card's font. The list shows at most 8 rows and scrolls vertically inside itself when it has more.
- The recommended suggestion's row carries a star (`star.fill`) at its trailing edge.
- A click on a row selects its font: the row gets a rounded background in the accent color and white text. The texts of the rows are not selectable.
- Below the list, a `Weight` menu, then a `Size` field followed by `pt`:
  - The menu lists the weights of the selected family ([Font catalogue](#font-catalogue)); the field holds a size of the [font notation](#font-notation).
  - Selecting a family suggestion sets them to its weight and size. Selecting a family of the search results keeps them, the weight following the [nearest weight](#nearest-weight) rule.
  - Before a family suggestion sets them, they hold `Regular` and `17`.
  - While a text style or `Answer another way` is selected, both are disabled.
- A preview: the `sample` text drawn in the selected font, a family font in its family and weight at the size of the field, a text style in that style. It wraps, is at most 200 pt high and scrolls vertically inside itself when it is taller. It is empty while `Answer another way` is selected, and while the field's text is not a size.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned symbol card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row and deselects the selected row; a click on a row chooses that font again. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the recommended suggestion is selected, its weight and size set when it is a family font, and the field and the search field are empty.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized) calls `chats.answerFont` with `![](font:<font>)` for the selected font, or with the field's text. The font of a selected family is `<family>/<weight>/<size>` with the menu's weight and the field's size, written as a whole number when its decimal is 0 (`17`) and with one decimal otherwise (`15.5`); the font of a text style is its notation. Submit is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field, and while a family is selected and the field's text is not a size.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerFont` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Font` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Font`, `Search fonts`, `Weight`, `Size`, `pt`, `Answer another way`, Skip and Submit, the row texts and fonts, the star, the preview, the behaviour of `Weight` and `Size` and the answer forms come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Animation requests

In a project whose [type](projects.md#project-types) is Swift application, when Claude needs the user to choose an animation, it asks with the MCP tool `ask_animation`, which calls `chats.postAnimation` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#ask_animation)). The rules Claude follows, the presets, their parameters and the parameter ranges are in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#animation-requests) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

### Posting an animation request

`chats.postAnimation` {chatID, question, suggestions, recommended} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostanimation)). It fails with `notFound` when the chat does not exist, then with `forbidden` as `chats.postQuestions` does in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked` ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)), then with `forbidden` and `ask_animation is available only in Swift application projects.` when the chat's project does not have the type `swiftApplication` ([Projects](projects.md#project-types)). It then trims each suggestion and `recommended`, checks the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md#animation-validation) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken. Nothing is shown to the user when the call fails.

A chat has at most one waiting request: posting fails with `conflict` while a question set is not completed (`Questions are already waiting for answers in this chat.`), a symbol request has no answer (`A symbol request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a color request has no answer (`A color request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a sound request has no answer (`A sound request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a shortcut request has no answer (`A shortcut request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`), a font request has no answer (`A font request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`) or an animation request has no answer (`An animation request is already waiting for an answer in this chat.`).

Once the request passes these checks, the engine adds an `animation` item {requestID (new uppercase UUID), question, suggestions in Claude's order, recommended, answer null} ([Data model](data-model.md#animation-item)) and returns the `AnimationRequest` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#animationrequest)). When the turn that posted it ends, the state becomes waitingAnswers.

### Answering an animation request

`chats.answerAnimation` {chatID, requestID, answer} ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsansweranimation)) stores the answer:

- `answer` is trimmed and has 1 to 10,000 characters (`invalid` otherwise). The [pinned animation card](#pinned-animation-card) sends `![](animation:<expression>)` for a chosen animation, the field's text for `Answer another way`, and `Skipped` for Skip.
- A request that already has an answer gives `conflict` (`This animation request was already answered.`). A client whose answer fails because the request was answered on another device (`conflict` while `chat.item` already carries the answer) shows no alert.
- The item is replaced with its `answer`, and the engine delivers the message `Animation: <answer>` as a user message whose `sender` is the name of the answering device; the answer keeps its own line breaks. During a turn it is delivered like any message ([Chat states](#chat-states)), and a closed chat reopens as for the Answers message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

```
Animation: ![](animation:.spring(duration: 0.4, bounce: 0.2))
```

Claude leaves a skipped request out: it does not act on it or guess, and it mentions it in its reply ([Default: common](skills.md#default-common)).

A request stays waiting across process exits and app restarts; answering it later starts a process with `--resume`. Messages typed in the composer while it waits are delivered normally and do not change it. Until it has an answer, the request stays pinned and Claude cannot post another animation request, a question set, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request or a font request in this chat.

### Lanes

A lane shows how an animation moves: a circle slides from the left end of a track to its right end with that animation.

- The animation is the SwiftUI `Animation` of the expression ([Animation expressions](workflow.md#animation-expressions)): `.smooth`, `.snappy`, `.bouncy`, `.spring`, `.easeIn`, `.easeOut`, `.easeInOut` and `.linear` are SwiftUI's presets of the same names, called with the parameters written, the parameters left out taking SwiftUI's defaults.
- The circle rests at the left end until the lane plays. Playing moves it at once to the left end, then animates it to the right end, where it stays; a spring with bounce can pass the right end before it settles. Playing a lane again, while it plays or after, starts the circle again from the left end.
- In the [pinned animation card](#pinned-animation-card), a lane is a capsule track 160 pt wide and 14 pt high filled with the quaternary fill style, and a circle 14 pt across in the accent color, animated with SwiftUI. In the transcript, the lane of an [animation marker](#links-images-and-files) is drawn by the page.

The track and circle sizes and colors are Default chosen at design time.

### Pinned animation card

While the chat has an animation request without answer, a native SwiftUI card is pinned above the composer of every view of the chat, below the permission card when both exist. Its layout follows the [pinned symbol card](#pinned-symbol-card).

- Header `Animation`, then at the top right a minimize button, a chevron pointing down.
- The question text rendered as Markdown with the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers and ticket and bundle id links, as in the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card). The header and the question text are selectable (`textSelection(.enabled)`).
- One row per suggestion, in the order Claude gave them: a radio button, the expression as Claude wrote it in a monospaced font, a star (`star.fill`) in the accent color after the recommended suggestion's expression, and at the trailing end the suggestion's [lane](#lanes). A click anywhere in the row selects the suggestion and plays its lane.
- A `Custom` row: a radio button, the label `Custom`, a preset menu, the row's expression in a monospaced font, and its own lane. The preset menu lists `.smooth`, `.snappy`, `.bouncy`, `.spring`, `.easeIn`, `.easeOut`, `.easeInOut` and `.linear`, in this order. Below them, one slider per parameter of the chosen preset, each with its label before it and its value after it: `Duration`, from 0.1 to 2 s, for every preset; `Bounce`, from -1 to 1, for `.spring`; `Extra Bounce`, from 0 to 1, for `.smooth`, `.snappy` and `.bouncy`. A slider's value is kept rounded to two decimals and shown with two decimals, followed by ` s` for `Duration`.
- The Custom row's expression is its preset with every parameter of the preset, in the order of [Animation expressions](workflow.md#animation-expressions): `.<preset>(<label>: <value>, …)`, each value with at most two decimals and without trailing zeros, for example `.spring(duration: 0.5, bounce: 0)` or `.easeInOut(duration: 0.25)`.
- A click on the Custom row's radio button, its label or the empty part of its line selects the row and plays its lane. Choosing a preset or moving a slider selects the row; choosing a preset and releasing a slider play its lane. Changing the preset keeps the values of the three sliders; only those of the preset's parameters show.
- `Play All`, a button with the symbol `play.fill`, plays every lane of the card together, the Custom lane included; each play starts the circles again from the left end. It sits at the leading end of the line of Skip and Submit.
- A radio row `Answer another way` with a multi-line text field (1 to 6 visible lines), as in the pinned symbol card: a click on the radio button, the label or the empty part of its line, or typing in the field, chooses this row. Return inserts a line break. While its text fits in 6 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture; a longer text scrolls inside the field.
- When the card appears for a request, the recommended suggestion is selected; the Custom row holds the recommended suggestion's preset and parameters, the parameters it leaves out taking SwiftUI's defaults; the field is empty; and every circle rests at the left end.
- Submit (⌘Return while the focus is in the chat view outside its composer, no permission card is shown and the card is not minimized) calls `chats.answerAnimation` with `![](animation:<expression>)`, `<expression>` being the selected suggestion as Claude wrote it, or the Custom row's expression while the Custom row is selected; or with the field's text. It is disabled while `Answer another way` is selected with an empty field.
- Skip, next to Submit, calls `chats.answerAnimation` with the answer `Skipped`.
- Once the request has its answer, from this view, another view or another device (`chat.item`), the card disappears.
- Minimize works as for the pinned questions card: the collapsed bar shows `Animation` and a chevron pointing up, and the minimized state is kept per chat for the request that was minimized, on this device only, and survives an app restart ([UI › Persistence](ui.md#persistence)).

The texts `Animation`, `Custom`, `Duration`, `Bounce`, `Extra Bounce`, `Play All`, `Answer another way`, Skip and Submit, the star, the symbol `play.fill`, the presets, the slider ranges, the lanes and the answer forms come from the user; the other details are Default chosen at design time.

## Shown images

When Claude shows the user an image, for example one it rendered in `/tmp`, it calls the MCP tool `show_image` with the paths of the images and an optional caption; the tool calls `chats.postImages` for the chat that owns the token and returns at once ([MCP](mcp.md#show_image)). The rule Claude follows is in [Claude workflow](workflow.md#showing-images) and in the internal skill [common](skills.md#default-common).

- `chats.postImages` {chatID, paths, caption} accepts only the chat's own Claude ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatspostimages)). It asks the user nothing, so it is accepted in every chat kind and state, in an implement chat whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, and while a question set or a request waits for an answer.
- It checks the [image rules](mcp.md#image-rules) in their order and fails with `invalid` and the message of the first one broken: only PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP files are accepted, and a path that is missing, unreadable or not a regular file fails the whole call. Nothing is then stored and the transcript shows nothing new.
- It copies each image into the chat's attachment folder `chats/<chat id>/` ([Data model](data-model.md#attachments)), then adds one `images` item {caption, images} ([Data model](data-model.md#images-item)). The images stay visible after their source files are deleted, also after the chat is reopened or the app restarts.
- The CLI runs the call after it has sent the `tool_use` block, and receives its result before it writes the `tool_result` block, so the item sits between the `toolUse` item of the call and its `toolResult` item: at the place of the call in the transcript ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)).
- The item is the main conversation's, whatever agent made the call: it has no `parentToolUseID` and is not nested in a tool card.
- Every window shows the item, local or remote: its images are chat files read with `attachments.read` ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)).
- The images are not sent back to Claude, which has the files already; the tool result is the text `images shown in the chat`.

## Chat settings

### Fields and defaults

`ChatSettings` {model, effort, permissionMode, ultracode, contextWindow} ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-settings)) is stored in each chat's `settings`; stored settings without `contextWindow` have the context window Auto. A new chat takes the `settings` parameter of the method that creates it; when absent, the `settings` of its orchestrator for an [orchestrated ticket chat](#orchestrated-ticket-chats) or a [CI chat](#ci-chats); otherwise the project's `chatDefaults`; when absent, the host setting `defaultChatSettings` (model `default`, effort `high`, permissionMode `manual`, ultracode false, contextWindow `auto`). Implement and Implement All pass the settings chosen in the [Implement sheet](tickets.md#implement-sheet), and the Feedback sheet those of its [chat settings menus](feedback.md#chat-settings-menus); `implement_ticket`, `implement_bundle` and `watch_ci` create chats without the parameter.

### Model

- The Model menu lists every entry of `claude.models` in order: `displayName` as title, `description` as subtitle, a checkmark on the entry whose `value` equals `settings.model`. A divider and the toggle `Ultracode` follow.
- The menu button shows the `displayName` of the selected value (the raw value when no entry matches), followed by ` · Ultracode` when ultracode is on.
- `settings.model` stores the entry's `value`, for example `default`, `opus`, `fable`, `sonnet`, `haiku` or `claude-opus-4-8`. At launch it is the value of `--model`; live it is sent with `set_model`.

### Ultracode

The `Ultracode` toggle, with the help text `Standing dynamic-workflow orchestration for this chat, at any effort level.`, sets `settings.ultracode`. At launch, true adds `--settings {"ultracode":true}`. Live, the engine sends `apply_flag_settings` with `{"ultracode":true}` or `{"ultracode":false}`. With ultracode, Claude runs most of its work as workflows with the Workflow tool; their agents show under the chat in the chat lists ([Agents](#agents)).

### Effort

| UI label   | Value  |
| ---------- | ------ |
| Low        | low    |
| Medium     | medium |
| High       | high   |
| Extra High | xhigh  |
| Max        | max    |

The Effort menu button shows `Effort: <UI label>`, in every composer and at every width where it is shown. Items whose value is not in the selected model's `supportedEffortLevels` are disabled; when that list is empty the whole menu is disabled with the help text `This model has no effort setting`; when `settings.model` matches no entry of `claude.models`, every item is enabled. The stored effort is never rewritten when the model changes; the CLI applies its own downgrade. Default chosen at design time. At launch the value goes to `--effort`; live the engine sends `apply_flag_settings` with `{"effortLevel":"<value>"}`.

### Permission mode

| UI label       | Stored value      | `--permission-mode` | `set_permission_mode` and `init.permissionMode` | System line    | Menu subtitle                                       |
| -------------- | ----------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| Ask Every Time | manual            | manual              | default                                         | Ask every time | Claude asks before it edits files or runs commands. |
| Accept Edits   | acceptEdits       | acceptEdits         | acceptEdits                                     | Accept edits   | File edits are accepted; other actions ask.         |
| Plan Only      | plan              | plan                | plan                                            | Plan only      | Claude plans and changes nothing.                   |
| Auto           | auto              | auto                | auto                                            | Auto           | A classifier approves or denies each request.       |
| Don't Ask      | dontAsk           | dontAsk             | dontAsk                                         | Don't ask      | Anything not pre-approved is denied without asking. |
| Allow All      | bypassPermissions | bypassPermissions   | bypassPermissions                               | Allow all      | Every action runs without asking.                   |

- The Permissions menu button shows the UI label. The `Allow All` item is drawn in red.
- While Allow All is selected, the Permissions menu button is drawn as a red capsule with the white text `Allow All`: the red badge of the composer.
- The menu subtitles are those of the table. Default chosen at design time.

### Context window

| UI label | Stored value | `--autocompact` |
| -------- | ------------ | --------------- |
| Auto     | auto         | not passed      |
| 200K     | 200k         | `200000`        |
| 1M       | 1m           | `1000000`       |

- Auto keeps the model's own context window, 1M tokens for the current models. 200K and 1M set the window after which Claude Code compacts the conversation, with `--autocompact` at launch ([Command line](#command-line)).
- The Context menu lists Auto, 200K and 1M, with a checkmark on the selected one. Its button shows `Context: <UI label>`, for example `Context: Auto`, in every composer and at every width where it is shown.
- The stored values: Default chosen at design time.
- A running Claude process keeps the window it was launched with: Claude Code 2.1.309 accepts `apply_flag_settings` with `autoCompactWindow`, but `get_context_usage` still reports the old window. The engine sends no control request for it and ends the process instead ([Changing settings](#changing-settings)).

### Changing settings

`chats.updateSettings` stores the five fields ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsupdatesettings)) and emits `chat.updated`. Settings are `invalid` when `model` is empty or contains white space, or when `effort`, `permissionMode` or `contextWindow` is not one of the stored values of [Effort](#effort), [Permission mode](#permission-mode) and [Context window](#context-window); `chats.create`, `chats.implementTicket`, `chats.implementBundle` and `chats.createFeedback` check their `settings` parameter the same way. When the chat has a live process, the engine sends one control request per changed field (model → `set_model`, permission mode → `set_permission_mode`, effort and ultracode → `apply_flag_settings`), during a turn as well; a changed context window sends none, as below. Without a live process the new values apply at the next launch. Every menu change in the composer of an existing chat calls `chats.updateSettings` at once; in a draft chat it changes the settings the chat is created with. A failed call shows the alert `Chat Settings failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). Default chosen at design time.

A new context window needs a new process. While the chat's stored `contextWindow` differs from the one its live process was launched with, the engine ends that process as soon as the chat has no turn in progress and the process has no background work running ([Background work](#background-work)): at once when the change arrives in that state, otherwise when the turn or the background work ends. It ends it with the closing sequence of idle termination ([Idle termination and quitting](#idle-termination-and-quitting)), and the idle timer does not start. The next message starts Claude with `--resume` ([Session and resume](#session-and-resume)) and the new `--autocompact`, so the conversation continues. A message sent before the process ends, during the turn, goes to the running Claude as any message and runs with the old window. When the stored value comes back to the process's window before the process ends, the process is kept. In a chat without a live process, the new value applies at the next launch.

A new permission mode chosen during a turn applies within that turn: the CLI decides every permission request Claude makes after it received `set_permission_mode` with the new mode, so after a switch to Allow All no permission card appears for the rest of the turn. Every launch passes `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` ([Command line](#command-line)), with which the CLI accepts a switch to `bypassPermissions` in a chat launched in another mode. The requests already pending stay with the engine, which answers them itself: when the CLI answers `set_permission_mode` with success and the mode it asked for is still the chat's `settings.permissionMode`, the engine approves at once each pending request that the new mode allows, as if the mode had been set before the request:

| New mode       | Pending requests approved                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Allow All      | every pending request                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Accept Edits   | each request of `Edit`, `Write` or `NotebookEdit` whose file (`file_path`, `notebook_path` for `NotebookEdit`; a relative path taken from the chat's working directory) is inside the chat's working directory ([Working directory](#working-directory)), symbolic links resolved |
| any other mode | none: the card stays                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

- Each approval writes the `allow` line of [Answer](#answer) and replaces the item with `decision` `allow` and `decidedBy` the name of the device that changed the mode, so the transcript shows `Allowed once by <device>`. When the last pending request is approved, the state returns to running.
- A request answered before the CLI's answer arrives keeps its answer. An error answer, or no answer within 30 s, approves nothing and adds the warning item of [Control protocol](#control-protocol).
- Approving only after the success answer, the tools counted as file edits and `decidedBy`: Default chosen at design time.

## Composer

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ [☑ DEMO-12 ×] [</> app.go:7 ×] [image.png ×] [build.log ×]  linked, resource, attachment chips  │
│ Message Claude…                                                         text, 1–10 lines        │
│ (paperclip)  Opus 5.5 ▾  Effort: High ▾  Context: Auto ▾  Ask Every Time ▾  ◔ 98% left  ■  ↑    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

| Control           | Behaviour                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Text field        | Multi-line, grows from 1 to 10 visible lines, then scrolls; while its text fits in 10 lines, the field neither scrolls nor bounces under a scroll gesture. Placeholder `Message Claude…`. Return sends; Shift-Return inserts a line break. A `/` at its start shows the [skill list](#skill-completion). |
| Linked item chips | Only in the inspector: one chip per item linked from the window ([Linked items](#linked-items)), before the attachment chips in the same row: the SF Symbol of the item's sidebar section, its short name, and an unlink button (×).                                                                     |
| Resource chips    | In every composer: one chip per resource added with `Add Resource…` ([Added resources](#added-resources)), after the linked item chips and before the attachment chips: its SF Symbol, its short name, and an unlink button (×).                                                                         |
| Attachment chips  | One chip per attachment: a thumbnail for images or a file icon, the name and the size, and a remove button (×).                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Attach button     | SF Symbol `paperclip`. Shows a menu of two items: `Add Files…` opens an open panel for files (multiple selection, no folders), and `Add Resource…` opens the search panel to link a resource ([Added resources](#added-resources)).                                                                      |
| Model menu        | [Model](#model).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Effort menu       | [Effort](#effort).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Context menu      | [Context window](#context-window).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Permissions menu  | [Permission mode](#permission-mode).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Context indicator | A ring followed by `<n>% left`, left of Stop and Send, once the chat has a context usage ([Context indicator](#context-indicator)).                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Stop (■)          | SF Symbol `stop.fill`, shown left of Send only while the chat is running; calls `chats.interrupt`.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Send (↑)          | SF Symbol `arrow.up.circle.fill`. Enabled when the text is not blank or there is an attachment, the window's device is connected and the CLI is available.                                                                                                                                               |

- The controls row stays on one line. When it does not fit the composer's width, the Model, Effort, Context and Permissions menus are replaced by one menu button with the SF Symbol `slider.horizontal.3` holding the submenus Model (with its Ultracode toggle), Effort, Context and Permissions, followed by the red `Allow All` capsule while that mode is selected. Default chosen at design time.

- While the [skill list](#skill-completion) is closed, Return in the text field does what an enabled Send does. While a turn is in progress, a sent message is delivered at once and Claude reads it after the current turn ([Chat states](#chat-states)).
- While the skill list is closed, Escape in the text field calls `chats.interrupt` while the chat is running; ⌘. does not.
- Sending clears the text field and the attachment chips; the linked item chips and the resource chips stay ([Linked items](#linked-items)). When the call fails, the alert `Send failed` shows the error, and the message and its attachments come back when the text field and the attachment chips are still empty. Default chosen at design time.
- Files dropped anywhere on the chat view, and files pasted with ⌘V, become attachments; dropped folders are refused with the alert `Folders cannot be attached.` Pasted image data without a file becomes a PNG attachment named `Pasted Image <yyyy-MM-dd> at <HH.mm.ss>.png`, in local time, as in [Adding attachments](tickets.md#adding-attachments). Pasted text is inserted as text.
- On a remote device, attachments are read on that device and sent as `AttachmentUpload` {name, contentType, dataBase64}.
- An attachment larger than 20 MB ([Engine API](engine-api.md#limits)) is refused with the alert `<name> is larger than 20 MB.` Default chosen at design time.
- While `ClaudeStatus.checking` is true, Send is disabled and the line `Checking the Claude CLI…` with a small spinner shows above the composer.
- When the CLI of the window's device is unavailable and `checking` is false, a banner above the composer shows the error. In a window showing This Mac it is `ClaudeStatus.error` with an `Open Settings` button that opens Settings on General. In a window showing a remote device it is `Claude CLI is not available on <device name>: <ClaudeStatus.error>`, without a button, because the Settings window edits only This Mac ([Settings window](ui.md#settings-window)).
- When the window's device is disconnected, the composer is disabled and its placeholder is `Not connected to <device name>`.
- In a closed chat ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)), the composer is hidden and the text `This conversation is over` is shown in its place, in every chat view of the chat on every client, local windows and remote devices. The CLI banner and the line `Checking the Claude CLI…` are hidden with it. Reopen in the More menu shows the composer again ([Chat view](#chat-view)).
- The unsent text and attachments of an existing chat, and its added resources ([Added resources](#added-resources)), are kept in memory in `DeviceWorkspace.drafts` by chat id ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)); those of a draft chat stay in its view. They are not persisted.

### Context indicator

The context indicator shows the chat's `contextUsage` ([Context usage](#context-usage)), with `used` its `usedTokens`, `window` its `windowTokens` and `buffer` its `bufferTokens`:

- `left` = `window` − `buffer` − `used`, at least 0. `<n>` = `left` / (`window` − `buffer`) × 100, rounded to the nearest whole number.
- The ring has the drawing of the [Weekly usage](ui.md#weekly-usage) ring: 12 points wide, filled clockwise from the top to the used part of `window` − `buffer`, 1 − `left` / (`window` − `buffer`), its unfilled part in the separator colour. It is green while `<n>` is 20 or more, orange while `<n>` is under 20 and red while `<n>` is under 10.
- `<n>% left` has the font and colour of the text of the composer's menu buttons.
- The tooltip is `<used> of <window> tokens used · <left> left before compaction`. A token amount below 1,000 is written as a whole number; from 1,000 it is written in `K`, from 1,000,000 in `M`, rounded to the nearest with at most one decimal and no trailing `.0`: `850`, `17.7K`, `120K`, `1M`, `1.5M`. The tooltip is also the indicator's accessibility label. Default chosen at design time for the accessibility label.
- After the chat's first compaction, the tooltip has a second line `Compacted <n> times`, `<n>` being the number of compaction lines in the chat's transcript ([Compaction](#compaction)), or `Compacted once` when `<n>` is 1. Without compaction line the tooltip has its first line only. The accessibility label holds both lines.
- While a compaction runs (`compactingSince` set, [Compaction](#compaction)), the ring fades from full opacity to 30% and back, 1 s per cycle, repeated until the compaction ends; then it is drawn at full opacity again. `<n>% left` does not fade.
- When the controls row does not fit the composer's width and the Model, Effort, Context and Permissions menus fold into the `slider.horizontal.3` menu ([Composer](#composer)), the ring stays, and fades during a compaction, and `<n>% left` is hidden; the tooltip still gives the numbers.
- The indicator changes with each new value ([Context usage](#context-usage)), each compaction line and each change of `compactingSince`, in every chat view of the chat on every client, local windows and remote devices. It keeps the last value after the Claude process has ended and after a restart. A chat that has never had a value, a draft chat or a chat from before the indicator, shows no indicator, and so does a value whose `bufferTokens` is not below its `windowTokens`. In a closed chat it is hidden with the composer ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).

The value of the capture `context-usage-response.json`, `usedTokens` 18554, `windowTokens` 1000000 and `bufferTokens` 33000, gives `left` 948446, a green ring filled to 2%, `98% left` and the tooltip `18.6K of 1M tokens used · 948.4K left before compaction`. In a chat whose transcript holds two compaction lines, the tooltip of that value is:

```text
18.6K of 1M tokens used · 948.4K left before compaction
Compacted 2 times
```

### Linked items

A chat composer links items of HephaestusV2 to the messages it sends and shows them as chips: the items open in the app, in the inspector only, and the resources the user adds, in every chat composer ([Added resources](#added-resources)).

The composer of the inspector's chat, or of its draft, shows the items open in the centre column of its window ([Inspector](ui.md#inspector)) as linked item chips. Chat windows, the Chats section, and the Chats tabs and Mentioned In tabs of tickets and bundles link no open item, and neither does the inspector while its window shows the Global Memories view or the Internal Skills view. While its window shows the detail of a virtual machine, the inspector links that virtual machine ([Detail](virtual-machines.md#detail)).

| Item            | `kind`           | Chip symbol                               | Short name                 | `<item>`                                                              |
| --------------- | ---------------- | ----------------------------------------- | -------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Ticket          | `ticket`         | `checklist`                               | the ticket id              | `ticket <id> “<title>”`                                               |
| Bundle          | `bundle`         | `shippingbox`                             | the bundle id              | `bundle <id> “<title>”`                                               |
| Chat            | `chat`           | `bubble.left.and.bubble.right`            | the chat's title           | `chat “<title>” (<chat id>)`                                          |
| Code file       | `codeFile`       | `chevron.left.forwardslash.chevron.right` | the file name              | `code file <project path>`                                            |
| Docs file       | `docsFile`       | `doc.text`                                | the file name              | `docs file <project path>`                                            |
| Web tab         | `webTab`         | `globe`                                   | the title, else the URL    | `web tab “<title>” <url>`, or `web tab <url>` when the title is empty |
| Skill           | `skill`          | `wand.and.stars`                          | the skill's name           | `skill <name>`                                                        |
| Virtual machine | `virtualMachine` | `display`                                 | the virtual machine's name | `virtual machine “<name>” (<VM id>)`                                  |

- The chip symbol is the SF Symbol of the item's [sidebar section](ui.md#sidebar), and `display`, the symbol of the Virtual Machines tab of [Settings](ui.md#settings-window), for a virtual machine; the chip's tooltip is `<item>`.
- × unlinks the item: its chip disappears and Send no longer passes it, for as long as it stays open ([Inspector](ui.md#inspector)).
- Send passes the linked items with the message, in chip order, in `ChatMessageInput.linkedItems`: for each, its `kind`, its short name as `name` and `<item>` as `text` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatmessageinput)). The message text is the typed text only.
- The `user` item stores the linked items ([Data model](data-model.md#user-item)), and its bubble shows them as chips on every client ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)).
- Claude receives one line `Open in the app: <item>` per linked item, in chip order, as context outside the message text ([Linked items hook](#linked-items-hook)). A message sent with no linked item and no added resource adds no context.
- The items stay linked after Send, so every message sent while they are open passes them.
- Linked items alone do not enable Send: Send keeps the rule of the [Composer](#composer) table.

The context Claude receives with a message sent while two tickets are linked:

```text
Open in the app: ticket DEMO-12 “Fix the login form”
Open in the app: ticket DEMO-14 “Remember the last account”
```

#### Added resources

In every chat composer, in the inspector, in chat windows, in the Chats section, in the Chats tabs and Mentioned In tabs of tickets and bundles, and in draft chats, the user links any item the search finds, in any project of the device: `Add Resource…` of the paperclip menu ([Composer](#composer)) opens the search panel, and the result the user picks there is added to that composer as a resource chip ([Adding a resource](search.md#adding-a-resource)).

| Result  | `kind`     | Chip symbol                               | Short name              | `<item>`                     |
| ------- | ---------- | ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| Project | `project`  | `folder`                                  | the identifier          | `project <ID> “<name>”`      |
| Ticket  | `ticket`   | `checklist`                               | the ticket id           | `ticket <id> “<title>”`      |
| Bundle  | `bundle`   | `shippingbox`                             | the bundle id           | `bundle <id> “<title>”`      |
| Doc     | `docsFile` | `doc.text`                                | the file name           | `docs file <path>`           |
| Code    | `codeFile` | `chevron.left.forwardslash.chevron.right` | `<file name>:<line>`    | `code file <path>:<line>`    |
| Chat    | `chat`     | `bubble.left.and.bubble.right`            | the chat's title        | `chat “<title>” (<chat id>)` |
| Skill   | `skill`    | `wand.and.stars`                          | the skill's name        | `skill <name>`               |
| Web tab | `webTab`   | `globe`                                   | the title, else the URL | as for an open web tab       |

- `<path>` is the project path of the file and `<line>` the line of the code result. A docs or code file of another project than the chat's gets ` in project <ID>` at the end of its `<item>`, `<ID>` being the identifier of the file's project, for example `code file Sources/App/main.swift:12 in project DEMO`.
- A resource chip looks like a linked item chip: the chip symbol, the short name and an unlink button (×), with `<item>` as tooltip. The symbol is the SF Symbol of the item's [sidebar section](ui.md#sidebar), and `folder` for a project, as in the results of the search panel. The resource chips come after the linked item chips and before the attachment chips, in the order added.
- A resource chip keeps the short name and the `<item>` that the result had when it was added: it follows neither a later change of the item, a new title for example, nor its deletion. Default chosen at design time.
- The composer never shows two chips with the same `<item>`: adding a resource whose `<item>` a chip of the composer already shows, a linked item chip or a resource chip, adds nothing; and an item open in the centre column whose `<item>` is that of an added resource shows no linked item chip, only the resource chip. Default chosen at design time.
- An added resource stays linked until the user removes it with ×: every later message of the chat passes it, as an open item is passed while it stays open. Send clears neither the linked item chips nor the resource chips ([Composer](#composer)).
- The added resources of an existing chat are kept with its composer draft in `DeviceWorkspace.drafts` ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)): every view of the chat in this app shows them, while the app runs, and they are not kept after a restart. Those of a draft chat stay in its view, and stay with the chat that its first Send creates.
- Added resources do not count as unsent text: a composer that holds nothing else does not hold back the restart for an update ([Restart rules](updater.md#restart-rules)), and the restart drops them. Default chosen at design time.
- Send passes the added resources in `ChatMessageInput.linkedItems`, after the linked items, in chip order: for each, its `kind`, its short name as `name`, `<item>` as `text`, and `added` true ([Linked item](data-model.md#linked-item)). The `user` item stores them and its bubble shows them as chips, like the linked items ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)).
- Claude receives one line `Linked by the user: <item>` per added resource, in chip order, after the `Open in the app: <item>` lines of the linked items ([Linked items hook](#linked-items-hook)).
- Added resources alone do not enable Send: Send keeps the rule of the [Composer](#composer) table.

The context Claude receives with a message sent from the inspector while the ticket DEMO-12 is open and a code line of the project API is added:

```text
Open in the app: ticket DEMO-12 “Fix the login form”
Linked by the user: code file Sources/App/main.swift:12 in project API
```

### Skill completion

Typing `/` at the start of a message lists the skills of the chat's project, so that the user completes the call of one of them, `/hephaestus-skills:<name>` ([Calls](skills.md#calls)).

- Every chat composer has the list: in the inspector, in chat windows, in the Chats section, in the Chats tabs and Mentioned In tabs of tickets and bundles, and in draft chats.
- The list shows while the text field has the focus, its text starts with `/` and the insertion point is in that first word: the `/` and the characters after it up to the insertion point hold no white space or line break. The typed text is the characters between the `/` and the insertion point.
- Skills offered: the skills the chat's project can use, the same set as its [generated plugin](skills.md#generation): the skills of the project's `skills` list that are skills of the library, and the called [internal skills](skills.md#internal-skills) `feedback`, `finish-implementation`, `implement-bundle` and `implement-ticket`. The ten [system prompt skills](skills.md#system-prompt-skills), which the plugin does not link, are not offered. Names of the project's list that are missing from the library are not offered ([Per-project enablement](skills.md#per-project-enablement)). The set does not depend on a CLI process, so draft chats get it too. On a remote device, it comes from that device's library and project. It follows `skills.changed` and `project.updated`, also while the list shows.
- Matching: a skill matches when its name contains the typed text, in any letter case. Names that start with the typed text come first, then the other matching names; each group is sorted by name. A lone `/` offers every skill of the set.
- Rows: each row shows the skill's name, then its description on one line, truncated at its end.
- Place: as the comment composer's mention list ([Mentioning Claude](tickets.md#mentioning-claude)), the list shows just above the text field, at its left edge, with its first row selected, drawn in the accent color. It is as wide as the text field, at most 420 points, and shows at most 8 rows, then scrolls. Default chosen at design time for the width and the number of rows.
- ↑ and ↓ move the selection, which stops at the first and the last rows, and scroll the list to keep the selected row visible. Each change of the matching skills selects the first row again. Default chosen at design time.
- Return, Tab or a click on a row replaces the `/` and the typed text with `/hephaestus-skills:<name> `, the call of the row's skill followed by one space, and closes the list. The characters after the insertion point stay. Default chosen at design time for the characters after the insertion point.
- Escape closes the list for that `/`: the list stays closed until that `/` is deleted or replaced, so typing a `/` again at the start of an empty text shows it again.
- The list closes when no skill matches, when the insertion point leaves the first word, when white space is typed after the `/`, and when the text field loses the focus. It shows again once the conditions above hold again, except after Escape.
- While the list shows, Return does not send, Tab inserts no tab, ↑ and ↓ do not move the insertion point, and Escape does not stop the running chat. While it is closed, Return, Tab, ↑, ↓ and Escape keep their behaviour of the [Composer](#composer).

The rows in a project that enables `release-notes` and `swift-testing`:

| Text    | Rows, in order                                                                                                |
| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `/`     | `feedback`, `finish-implementation`, `implement-bundle`, `implement-ticket`, `release-notes`, `swift-testing` |
| `/f`    | `feedback`, `finish-implementation`, then `swift-testing`, which contains `f` without starting with it        |
| `/IMPL` | `implement-bundle`, `implement-ticket`, then `finish-implementation`, which contains `impl`                   |
| `/zzz`  | none: the list is closed                                                                                      |
| `/f x`  | none: white space after the `/` closes the list                                                               |

## Transcript view

### Web view and bridge

The transcript of a chat view is one `WKWebView` loading the bundled `chat.html`, `markdown.css`, `chat.css`, `markdown.js` and `chat.js` (`Sources/HephaestusUI/Resources/`), so the text of every bubble is selectable as one document. Swift renders each item to an HTML fragment (Markdown through the HephaestusText HTML renderer, with the rules of [Documentation viewer](documentation-viewer.md)), and `chat.js` places the fragments. The fragment of a `questions` item that is not completed also depends on the question that the pinned questions card of the same chat view displays: when that question changes, Swift renders the fragment again and sends it with `upsert`, so the `➡️` mark of [Item rendering](#item-rendering) moves with the card. The fragments of `user`, `assistant` and `images` items also depend on the current day, through the date and time of their captions: when the day of the Mac changes while the page is shown, at midnight or with a change of time zone, Swift renders them again and sends the changed ones with `upsert`. `markdown.js`, which the doc preview also loads, draws and plays the markers ([Preview web view](documentation-viewer.md#preview-web-view)); `chat.js` calls its `decorate` on each fragment it places and its `handleClick` for every click. The page runs no other script, except the user script of the inspector's transparent background below, and never navigates. Its context menu keeps Copy, Copy Link, Copy Image and Look Up, as the doc preview's ([Preview web view](documentation-viewer.md#preview-web-view)). The bridge and the context menu are Default chosen at design time.

In the inspector the transcript has a transparent background, so that the inspector's frosted material shows through it ([Inspector](ui.md#inspector)): the web view draws no background (`drawsBackground` false, `underPageBackgroundColor` clear), and a user script injected at document start adds the class `transparent` to the page's root element, for which `chat.css` makes the `html` and `body` backgrounds transparent. The bubbles, cards and buttons keep their backgrounds. In the other chat views the page keeps the opaque page background of `chat.css`. The user script is Default chosen at design time.

Calls from Swift (`evaluateJavaScript`) on the global object `HephaestusChat`:

| Call                                      | Effect                                                                                                                                                            |
| ----------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `reset({items, hasEarlier, placeholder})` | Replaces the transcript with `items` (fragments in order); shows the `Show Earlier Messages` button when `hasEarlier`; shows `placeholder` when `items` is empty. |
| `prepend({items, hasEarlier})`            | Inserts older fragments before the first one.                                                                                                                     |
| `upsert(fragment)`                        | Adds the fragment, or replaces the fragment with the same `id`, and removes the streaming bubble with that id.                                                    |
| `stream({itemID, kind, html})`            | Creates or updates the streaming bubble `itemID` of kind `text`, `thinking` or `toolInput`.                                                                       |
| `clearStreams()`                          | Removes every streaming bubble.                                                                                                                                   |

A fragment is `{id, type, html, parentToolUseID, toolUseID}`: `type` is the item type, `parentToolUseID` the item's value or null, `toolUseID` the value of `toolUse` and `toolResult` items or null.

Messages to Swift (`window.webkit.messageHandlers.hephaestus.postMessage`):

| Message                                              | Effect                                                                    |
| ---------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `{"action":"ready"}`                                 | the page is loaded; Swift calls `reset`                                   |
| `{"action":"openLink","url":"…","commandKey":false}` | [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)                        |
| `{"action":"copy","text":"…"}`                       | puts the text on the general pasteboard as plain text                     |
| `{"action":"openAttachment","path":"…"}`             | opens the attachment (`path` relative to `.hephaestus/`)                  |
| `{"action":"loadEarlier"}`                           | Swift calls `prepend` with the 300 items before the first shown           |
| `{"action":"playSound","name":"…"}`                  | plays the sound of the chat's project ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)) |

### Item rendering

| Item                | Rendering                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `user`              | Right-aligned bubble with the accent color as background and white text, rendered as Markdown with `softBreaksAsLineBreaks` ([Renderer](documentation-viewer.md#renderer)): each line break of the text shows as a line break, so the Answers message ([Answering](#answering)) shows `Answers:` and each `<label>: <answer>` on its own line. A caption above it, right-aligned, shows `sender` in semibold followed by `<created>` in secondary color, as the header of a ticket comment ([Comments](tickets.md#comments)). Between the caption and the bubble, the message's linked items (`linkedItems`) as chips in their order, each drawn as the composer's linked item chip without its unlink button: the chip symbol of its `kind` and its `name`, with `text` as tooltip ([Linked items](#linked-items)); the row's place, as in the composer above the text: Default chosen at design time. Images below the text as thumbnails (at most 240 × 240 pt), other files as chips (icon, name, size). |
| `assistant`         | Left-aligned bubble with the secondary background, rendered as Markdown; code blocks highlighted, each with a Copy button.
