A caption above it, left-aligned, shows `<Claude name>` in semibold followed by `<created>` in secondary color, for example `Claude (Opus 5.5 · Extra High) 16:29`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `thinking`          | Left-aligned disclosure `Thinking`, collapsed; expanded, it shows the text as Markdown in secondary color.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `toolUse`           | A tool card ([Tool cards](#tool-cards)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `toolResult`        | Inside the card of its `toolUse`; a standalone card titled `Tool result` when no card has its `toolUseID`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `permissionRequest` | One small line: `<tool> · ` (the tool name, or for an MCP tool its short name followed by the badge `MCP · <server>`, as in the [pinned permission card](#pinned-permission-card)) followed by `Waiting for permission` (pending), `Allowed once by <decidedBy>`, `Always allowed by <decidedBy>`, `Denied by <decidedBy>`, or `Cancelled` (decision `deny` with `decidedBy` null); when `reason` is present, a second small line with the reason.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `questions`         | A card titled `Questions`: each question as `<label>. <question>`, preceded by `➡️ ` (`➡️ B. <question>`) for the question that the [pinned questions card](#pinned-questions-card) of the same chat view displays while the set is not completed, then one line per option, `<option label> — <description>` (the ` — <description>` part left out when absent), each recommended option followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`. The answer of a `multiple` question that is not `Skipped` shows `Answer:` alone on its line, then one line `- <choice>` per choice.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `symbol`            | A card titled `Symbol`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion with the symbol's icon followed by its name, drawn as the SF Symbol marker `![](sf:<name>)`, the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `color`             | A card titled `Color`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion, the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`. A suggestion of one color is drawn as the color marker `![](color:<color>)`, a swatch followed by the color; a pair as `Light ![](color:<light>), Dark ![](color:<dark>)`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `sound`             | A card titled `Sound`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion drawn as the sound marker `![](sound:<name>)`, a play button followed by its name, the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `shortcut`          | A card titled `Shortcut`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion with the shortcut drawn as its key caps ([Shortcut notation](#shortcut-notation)), the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `font`              | A card titled `Font`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion drawn as the font marker `![](font:<font>)`, the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `animation`         | A card titled `Animation`: the question rendered as Markdown, then one line per suggestion drawn as the animation marker `![](animation:<expression>)`: its replay button, its lane and its expression ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)), the recommended one followed by `(recommended)`, then `Answer: <answer>` or `Not answered yet`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `images`            | Left-aligned, as an `assistant` bubble: a caption above it shows `Claude` in semibold followed by `<created>` in secondary color; then the item's `caption`, when it has one, in a left-aligned bubble with the secondary background, rendered as Markdown with the rules of the `assistant` bubble; then the images as thumbnails (at most 240 × 240 pt), in their order, left-aligned, as the images of a `user` item. Hovering a thumbnail shows its `created` time as a tooltip, and clicking it opens the [image viewer](tickets.md#image-viewer).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `system`            | Small centered line; secondary color for info, orange for warning, red for error.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `result`            | Small centered footer line: `Done · <duration> · session cost <cost>`, or `Error · <duration> · <summary>` when `isError`. The part ` · session cost <cost>` is left out when `costUSD` is null.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

- `<Claude name>` of an `assistant` caption, from the item's `model` and `effort`: `Claude (<model> · <effort>)`, where `<model>` is the name of `model` given by the `claude.models` of the window's device ([Model names](#model-names)) and `<effort>` the UI label of `effort` ([Effort](#effort)), `Extra High` for `xhigh`; `Claude (<model>)` when `effort` is null, the model having no effort setting; `Claude` when `model` is null, for an item written before the model was stored or a message without model. The whole name is in semibold. Default chosen at design time. When `claude.models` changes, the captions whose name changes are rendered again.
- `<created>` of a caption: the item's `created` date in the user's locale and time zone. For a message created today, only the short time (`15:12`); for an older message, the abbreviated date and short time, as in the ticket list's Created column ([Ticket list](tickets.md#ticket-list)) (`1 Nov 2026 at 15:12`). When the day changes while a transcript is shown, its captions are rendered again ([Web view and bridge](#web-view-and-bridge)).
- `<duration>` from `durationMs`: below 60 s, one decimal and ` s` (`3.5 s`); below one hour, `<m> min <s> s` (`2 min 5 s`); else `<h> h <m> min` (`1 h 2 min`).
- `<cost>`: `< $0.01` below 0.01, else `$` and two decimals (`$0.42`). These formats are Default chosen at design time.
- Every card of the transcript, and every Markdown text and marker-reading plain text of the transcript, reads the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers, all six kinds, with the options of [Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup), and draws them as [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files) says: the `user`, `assistant` and `thinking` bubbles, the caption bubble of `images` items and the streaming bubbles, which are Markdown; the `questions` card: its question texts, which are Markdown, and its option lines and answers, which are plain text; the `symbol`, `color`, `sound`, `shortcut`, `font` and `animation` cards: their question, which is Markdown, and their suggestion lines and answer, which are plain text. The other items, tool cards, `permissionRequest` lines, `system` and `result` lines and the `Preparing tool call…` card, show a marker as its text.
- Ticket and bundle ids of the projects of the window's device are links ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)) in the `user`, `assistant` and `thinking` bubbles, in the caption bubble of `images` items and in the streaming bubbles, which are Markdown; in the `questions` card: in its question texts, which are Markdown, and in its option lines and answers, which are plain text; in the `symbol`, `color`, `sound`, `shortcut`, `font` and `animation` cards: in their question, which is Markdown, and in their answer, which is plain text; in the two lines of a `permissionRequest` item, the tool line and the reason, which are plain text; and in tool cards ([Tool cards](#tool-cards)). Plain texts find ids with `MarkdownItemIDs`, and with `MarkdownSymbolMarkers` where they also draw SF Symbol, color, sound, shortcut, font and animation markers ([Ticket and bundle ids](documentation-viewer.md#ticket-and-bundle-ids)). The suggestion lines of the `symbol`, `color`, `sound`, `shortcut`, `font` and `animation` cards, the `system` and `result` lines and the `Preparing tool call…` card show ids as text.
- Hovering a bubble shows its `created` time as a tooltip and a Copy button that copies its Markdown source.
- `chat.css` follows the light and dark appearance (`prefers-color-scheme`); code colors are those of the [Code editor](code-editor.md).

### Tool cards

A tool card is collapsed by default. Its header row shows an icon, the tool name (the short name for MCP tools), the badge `MCP · <server>` for MCP tools, a one-line summary truncated with an ellipsis, and at the trailing edge a spinner (no result yet while the chat is running), the red tag `Error` (result with `isError`), or nothing. A spinner turns without restarting when its card is redrawn (a new step count, a new input or any other change of the card), when the card that holds it in its `Steps` is redrawn or expanded, and when a `Preparing tool call…` card updates ([Streaming bubbles](#streaming-bubbles)): every spinner of the transcript page, in a chat view or an [agent view](#agent-view), shows the angle of the page's clock, one turn every 0.8 s since the page loaded, so a spinner drawn again goes on at the angle of the one it replaces. One clock for all spinners: Default chosen at design time. Clicking the header expands the card:

- `Input`: `input` as JSON with 2-space indentation; for `Bash`, the `command` as a shell code block; for `Edit`, `old_string` and `new_string` as two code blocks; for `Write`, `content` as a code block whose language word is the extension of `file_path`, lowercased: the languages of [Code blocks](documentation-viewer.md#code-blocks) are highlighted, other extensions are plain.
- `Result`: the result text in monospace, first 200 lines with a `Show All` button; result images as thumbnails.
- `Steps`: the items whose `parentToolUseID` is this card's `toolUseID`, in order, rendered with the same rules. A collapsed card that has steps shows `<n> steps` in its header, `1 step` for one. The card of an `Agent` or `Task` call keeps its steps; its sub-agent also has a row in the chat lists and a view of its own ([Agents](#agents)).
- Ticket and bundle ids are links in the header's summary, in the `Input`, its code blocks included (the JSON, the `Bash` command, the `Edit` strings and the `Write` content), and in the `Result` text ([Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files)). The summary and the result text find them with `MarkdownItemIDs`; each code block of the input is written by `renderCodeBlock` with the transcript's `projectIdentifiers` ([Ticket and bundle ids](documentation-viewer.md#ticket-and-bundle-ids)). A click on an id of the header opens its ticket or bundle and neither expands nor collapses the card.

| Tool                                    | Icon      | Summary                                                                       |
| --------------------------------------- | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Bash`                                  | terminal  | first line of `command`                                                       |
| `Read`, `Write`, `Edit`, `NotebookEdit` | document  | `file_path` (or `notebook_path`), relative to the project root when inside it |
| `Glob`, `Grep`                          | magnifier | `pattern`, followed by ` in <path>` when `path` is set                        |
| `WebFetch`                              | globe     | `url`                                                                         |
| `WebSearch`                             | globe     | `query`                                                                       |
| `Agent`, `Task`                         | people    | `description`                                                                 |
| `TodoWrite`                             | checklist | `<n> todos`, the number of elements of `todos`                                |
| MCP tools                               | plug      | generic rule                                                                  |
| any other tool                          | wrench    | generic rule                                                                  |

Generic rule: the first of the input fields `command`, `file_path`, `path`, `pattern`, `url`, `query`, `description`, `prompt`, `name`, `title`, `ticketID`, `bundleID`, `projectID` that holds a non-empty string; else `input` as compact JSON. Summaries are cut at 120 characters with `…`. The icons are inline SVG drawings in `chat.js`. Icons and summaries: Default chosen at design time.

### Streaming bubbles

- The first `chat.delta` of an item id without a fragment creates a streaming bubble: kind `text` an assistant bubble whose caption shows `<Claude name>` alone, made as [Item rendering](#item-rendering) makes it from the `model` and `effort` of that `chat.delta` (`Claude (Opus 5.5 · Extra High)`), the `<created>` of [Item rendering](#item-rendering) being added when the item replaces it, kind `thinking` an expanded `Thinking` block, kind `toolInput` a card `Preparing tool call…` that shows the last 300 characters of the partial JSON in monospace.
- Each later `chat.delta` appends to that item's streaming buffer (`TranscriptState`, [UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)); text and thinking bubbles are rendered again as Markdown on every update. The `Preparing tool call…` card shows the new last 300 characters on every update, and its spinner turns without restarting ([Tool cards](#tool-cards)).
- A `chat.item` with the same id replaces the streaming bubble in place.
- When `chat.updated` reports the state idle, waitingAnswers or error, every remaining streaming bubble is removed.

### Links, images and files

- Clicking an `http` or `https` link opens it in a new web tab of the chat's project (`webTabs.open`) and navigates to the new tab (`webTab` target) in the chat view's main window, or, from a chat window, in the main window chosen by [Choosing the window](ui.md#choosing-the-window); ⌘-click opens it in the default browser of the Mac showing the window. Default chosen at design time.
- A relative link, or a `file` URL inside the project root, opens the file: in the Docs section when its name ends in `.md` (any letter case) and it is under `docsPath`, else in the Code section. A `.markdown` file opens in the Code section. In the Code section the line of a GitHub line fragment (`L<n>`, `L<n>-L<m>`, `L<n>C<c>`) is shown, as in the doc preview ([Link activation](documentation-viewer.md#link-activation)). Other links, a `#fragment` link included, do nothing. The same window choice applies. Default chosen at design time.
- Ticket and bundle ids: the transcript renders with `projectIdentifiers` set to the identifiers of every project of the window's device, sub projects included, so the ids of these projects are links ([Ticket and bundle ids](documentation-viewer.md#ticket-and-bundle-ids)); the pinned cards use the same identifiers. A click on an id, with or without ⌘, navigates to the `ticket` target, or to the `bundle` target for an id of the bundle form ([Resolving a target](ui.md#resolving-a-target)), with the same window choice: the chat view's main window, or from a chat window the main window chosen by [Choosing the window](ui.md#choosing-the-window). A ticket or bundle that does not exist shows `Ticket <id> was not found.` or `Bundle <id> was not found.`. In the transcript, id links look like its other links: the `.md a` style of [Styling](documentation-viewer.md#styling), white and underlined in `user` bubbles, and the same style outside Markdown text (`a.md-id` in `chat.css`). The same rules apply to a chat view shown in a ticket's or a bundle's Chats tab or Mentioned In tab.
- A link click that fails, such as a failed `webTabs.open`, shows the alert `Open Link failed`; a file of the transcript that cannot be read shows the alert `Open failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). Default chosen at design time.
- Images use URLs `hephaestus-attachment://attachment/<percent-encoded path relative to .hephaestus/>`, served by a `WKURLSchemeHandler` that calls `attachments.read` for the chat's project through the window's engine connection; results are cached per window.
- Clicking an image opens the [image viewer](tickets.md#image-viewer). Clicking another file opens it with Quick Look on a temporary copy, as for ticket attachments ([Tickets](tickets.md#showing-attachments)).
- The images of an `images` item ([Shown images](#shown-images)) are chat files, like the images of user messages: they use the same URLs, and the image viewer steps through them with the other images of the chat's items, in transcript order. A Markdown image of the caption follows the rules of [Images](documentation-viewer.md#images), so an image outside the project folder is refused there; Claude shows such an image with `show_image`.
- An SF Symbol marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `sf`, for example `![](sf:line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle)` ([SF Symbol markers](documentation-viewer.md#sf-symbol-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `isSymbolAvailable` true for a name when `NSImage(systemSymbolName:accessibilityDescription:)` gives an image for it on the Mac that shows the window. A marker of such a name is drawn as the symbol's icon followed by its name; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](sf:no.such.symbol)`.
- The icon is the `md-symbol-icon` element of the marker: 1.25em wide and 1em high, 0.25em before the name, filled with the color of the text around it through a mask that holds the symbol's image, scaled to fit and centered. `markdown.js` sets that mask to `hephaestus-symbol://symbol/<name>`, the name encoded with `encodeURIComponent`, and the page's Content Security Policy allows `hephaestus-symbol:` images. Default chosen at design time.
- `SymbolSchemeHandler`, registered for `hephaestus-symbol` on the transcript web view and the doc preview ([Preview web view](documentation-viewer.md#preview-web-view)), answers a GET whose host is `symbol` with a PNG of the symbol named by the percent-decoded path without its leading `/`, drawn from `NSImage(systemSymbolName:accessibilityDescription:)` at 48 points and scale 2. The response has status 200, `Content-Type: image/png`, `Content-Length` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, since WebKit loads mask images in CORS mode. It makes no engine call, so a window showing a remote device draws the symbols of its own Mac. A name without symbol fails with `URLError(.fileDoesNotExist)` and a malformed URL with `URLError(.noPermissionsToReadFile)`. Default chosen at design time.
- A color marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `color`, for example `![](color:#FF9500)` or `![](color:systemBlue)` ([Color markers](documentation-viewer.md#color-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `colorSwatch`, which accepts a hex color `#RRGGBB`, in either letter case, or a name of [System colors](#system-colors), and gives the color's light and dark values on the Mac that shows the window. A marker of such a color is drawn as a swatch of the color followed by the color as written; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](color:#FF950080)`.
- The swatch is the `md-color-swatch` element of the marker: a 0.9em square with corners of radius 0.2em and a 1px border of the text color at 30 % opacity, 0.25em before the color, aligned with the text. Its background is the element's `data-light` value, or its `data-dark` value while the page is in the dark appearance (`prefers-color-scheme: dark`); `markdown.js` copies them into the CSS variables `--md-color-light` and `--md-color-dark` of the element. A hex color has its own value in both; a system color has the values the Mac that shows the window gives it in the light (Aqua) and dark (Dark Aqua) appearances ([System colors](#system-colors)), written `#RRGGBB`, or `#RRGGBBAA` when it is not opaque. Default chosen at design time.
- A sound marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `sound`, for example `![](sound:macOS/Glass.aiff)` ([Sound markers](documentation-viewer.md#sound-markers)). The name, a name of the [sound catalogue](#sound-catalogue), is written as it is, spaces included: `![](sound:iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf)`. The marker ends at the `)` that closes its `(`, so a name may also hold balanced parentheses, as in `![](sound:Project/Sounds/alert (2).caf)`; the CommonMark form with angle brackets, `![](<sound:iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf>)`, is read too. The transcript renders Markdown with `readsSoundMarkers` true and draws every marker as a play button followed by the name; the name is not checked against the catalogue, so the play button of a sound that does not exist fails when clicked ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)).
- The play button is the `md-sound-play` button of the marker, which holds the `play.circle.fill` icon, drawn as the icon of an SF Symbol marker (`md-symbol-icon`); the name follows it. A click on the button posts `playSound` with the name ([Web view and bridge](#web-view-and-bridge)), and Swift plays the sound of the chat's project ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)). Default chosen at design time.
- In the question texts of the pinned cards, which SwiftUI draws, the play button of a sound marker is the character `▶` drawn as a link, followed by a space and the name; a click on it plays the sound. The option rows of the pinned questions card draw `▶` as text, so that a click in a row always chooses the option ([Marker setup](documentation-viewer.md#marker-setup)). Default chosen at design time.
- A shortcut marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `shortcut`, for example `![](shortcut:⇧⌘K)` ([Shortcut markers](documentation-viewer.md#shortcut-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `shortcutKeys` giving the key caps of a shortcut written in the [shortcut notation](#shortcut-notation), and nothing for any other text. A marker of such a shortcut is drawn as its key caps, inline with the text: each key cap is a `kbd` element, a rounded box with a thin border in the color of the text around it, 0.15em apart; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](shortcut:⌘⇧K)`. Default chosen at design time for the drawing.
- In a shortcut marker, a key `(`, `)` or `\` is written with a `\` before it, as Markdown requires in a link destination: `![](shortcut:⌘\()` names the shortcut `⌘(`. The pinned shortcut card writes its answers this way. Default chosen at design time.
- A font marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `font` followed by a font of the [font notation](#font-notation), for example `![](font:.headline)` or `![](font:System Rounded/Semibold/17)` ([Font markers](documentation-viewer.md#font-markers)). A family or weight name with spaces is written with its spaces, `![](font:Avenir Next/Demi Bold/15)`: CommonMark takes no space in an image source, so the renderers find such a marker in the text around it.
- The transcript renders Markdown with a `fontMarker` that accepts every font that follows the [font notation](#font-notation). A marker of such a font is drawn as the rows of the pinned font card read it, `System Rounded · Semibold · 17 pt` or `.headline`, at the size of the text around it: a family font in its family and weight, a text style in the system font with the weight of its macOS style. Any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](font:Helvetica)`.
- The marker is the `md-font` element of [Font markers](documentation-viewer.md#font-markers). For a system design, `markdown.js` sets its CSS `font-family` to `system-ui`, `ui-rounded`, `ui-serif` or `ui-monospace` and its `font-weight` to 100 for `Ultralight` up to 900 for `Black`, in the order of the nine weights; for a text style, `system-ui` with the weight 700 for `.headline`, 500 for `.caption2` and 400 for the others. For another family, the marker's face URL is `hephaestus-font://face/<family>/<weight>`, each name percent-encoded as `encodeURIComponent` does; `markdown.js` adds that face to the page's fonts (`FontFace`) the first time a marker uses it, and draws the marker in it, or in `system-ui` while it is not loaded or when it does not load. The page's Content Security Policy allows `hephaestus-font:` fonts. Default chosen at design time.
- `FontSchemeHandler`, registered for `hephaestus-font` on the transcript web view and the doc preview, answers a GET whose host is `face` with the font of the face named by the two percent-decoded components of the path: a face of a family installed on the Mac that shows the window, else a face of a project family of the chat's project, or of the doc's project in the doc preview, read with `files.fonts` and `files.read` through the window's engine connection ([Font catalogue](#font-catalogue)). The data is one OpenType font rebuilt from the face's tables, so a face of a collection loads too. The response has status 200, `Content-Type: font/otf` for a font with CFF outlines and `font/ttf` otherwise, `Content-Length` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. A face that does not exist fails with `URLError(.fileDoesNotExist)` and a malformed URL with `URLError(.noPermissionsToReadFile)`. Default chosen at design time.
- An animation marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `animation`, for example `![](animation:.spring(duration: 0.4, bounce: 0.2))` ([Animation markers](documentation-viewer.md#animation-markers)). The expression follows `animation:` as written, its spaces and parentheses included; as a CommonMark image source cannot hold spaces, the renderers find the marker in the text, from `![` to the `)` that closes the expression's own parentheses. The transcript renders Markdown with `animationCurve`, which gives, for an expression of [Animation expressions](workflow.md#animation-expressions), the duration and the easing of its animation as SwiftUI plays it. A marker of such an expression is drawn as a replay button with the symbol `play.fill`, then a small lane, then the expression; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](animation:.wobbly)`. The pinned cards, which SwiftUI draws, show an animation marker as its expression, as ticket descriptions do ([Tickets](tickets.md#ticket-detail)).
- The marker is the `md-animation` element: the button `md-animation-play`, which holds the `md-symbol-icon` of `play.fill` drawn as the icon of an SF Symbol marker, the lane `md-animation-lane`, a track 3em wide and 0.8em high with round ends filled with the color of the text around it at 20% opacity, which holds the circle `md-animation-dot`, 0.8em across in the color of the text, and the expression. A click on the button plays the marker's [lane](#lanes): `markdown.js` cancels the circle's running animation, then animates it with the Web Animations API from the left end to the right end of the track, for the marker's `data-duration` seconds with the CSS easing of its `data-easing`, and leaves it at the right end. The click does nothing else. Default chosen at design time.
- `animationCurve` samples the SwiftUI animation at 61 times evenly spaced from 0 to its duration: for `.smooth`, `.snappy`, `.bouncy` and `.spring`, the duration is the settling duration of SwiftUI's `Spring` with the same parameters, at most 10 s, and 10 s for a spring that never settles, and the values are that spring's values for a move from 0 to 1, a value SwiftUI cannot compute counting as 1; for `.easeIn`, `.easeOut`, `.easeInOut` and `.linear`, the duration is the expression's and the values those of SwiftUI's `UnitCurve` of the same name. `data-duration` is the duration in seconds with three decimals, and `data-easing` the CSS function `linear(<value>, …)` with each value rounded to four decimals, so a spring that passes the right end draws the circle past it. Default chosen at design time.

### Selection and copying

All text of the transcript is selectable across bubbles. ⌘A selects the whole transcript and ⌘C copies the selection as plain text. Code blocks have a Copy button; bubbles have a Copy button on hover ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)). Find (⌘F) shows the find bar of the Docs preview above the transcript ([Modes](documentation-viewer.md#modes)).

### Scrolling and long transcripts

- When the view is scrolled within 40 pt of the bottom, new content keeps it at the bottom; otherwise a `Jump to Latest` button appears at the bottom edge.
- A transcript renders its last 300 items; the `Show Earlier Messages` button at the top adds the 300 previous ones each time. Default chosen at design time.
- The transcript page scrolls vertically only, in every chat view: `chat.css` hides the page's horizontal overflow (`overflow-x: hidden` on `html`) and keeps the bubbles and cards within the page width, wrapping long words. Wide code blocks and tables scroll horizontally inside their own box ([Code blocks](documentation-viewer.md#code-blocks), [Tables](documentation-viewer.md#tables)). `markdown.css` is unchanged, so the Docs preview keeps its own scrolling.

### Empty transcript

Placeholders: Default chosen at design time.

| Kind     | Placeholder                                                   |
| -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| project  | Ask Claude to plan, create tickets or work on <project name>. |
| ticket   | Ask Claude about <ticket id>, or click Implement.             |
| bundle   | Ask Claude about <bundle id>, or click Implement All.         |
| feedback | never empty: the feedback message is sent at creation         |
| ci       | never empty: the CI message is sent at creation               |

## Agents

A chat's Claude can start agents that work beside it: the agents of a workflow it runs with the Workflow tool, which [ultracode](#ultracode) makes it use for most of its work, and the sub-agents it starts with the Agent tool ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents)). The engine records the workflows and agents of each chat, follows them live, keeps them as long as the chat exists, and gives every client, remote devices included, their states and transcripts ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chats)). The user sees them as rows under their chat in the chat lists ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)), reads each agent's transcript in its [agent view](#agent-view), and stops a running workflow with all its agents ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)). The rules below are checked against `stream-workflow.jsonl`, `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl`, `stream-background-agent.jsonl` and `stream-background-agent-resume.jsonl` ([Fixture replays](#fixture-replays)).

### Workflows and agents

| Record         | Created when                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `id`                   | Title                                                                  |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| workflow       | a `system/task_started` line with `task_type` `local_workflow` whose `task_id` names no workflow of the chat                                                                                                                           | the line's `task_id`   | its `summary`: the line's `description`, else `Workflow`               |
| workflow agent | a `workflow_agent` entry with an `agentId` string in the `workflow_progress` of a `system/task_progress` line of the workflow, or a `started` entry with an `agentId` string in the journal of its [workflow folder](#workflow-folder) | the `agentId`          | the entry's `label`, else `Agent`                                      |
| sub-agent      | a `toolUse` item named `Agent` or `Task` whose `server` is null ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents))                                                                                                                                            | the call's `toolUseID` | the call's input `description`, else its `subagent_type`, else `Agent` |

- A workflow is a [ChatWorkflow](data-model.md#chatworkflow): its id, the `toolUseID` of its `Workflow` call (the line's `tool_use_id`), its `runID` ([Workflow folder](#workflow-folder)), its `name` (the line's `workflow_name`), its `summary`, its state, and its `started` and `ended` dates.
- An agent is a [ChatAgent](data-model.md#chatagent): its id, its kind (`workflow` for a workflow agent, `subagent` for a sub-agent), the `workflowID` of its workflow, its title, its `phase` (the entry's `phaseTitle`, or the journal entry's `phase`; none for a sub-agent), the `taskID` of a sub-agent, its state, and its `started` and `ended` dates.
- A `task_progress` line of a workflow whose `summary` string differs from the workflow's replaces it. A later `workflow_agent` entry of a known agent replaces its title and phase with its `label` and `phaseTitle` strings.
- `started` is the time the engine created the record and `ended` the time the record ended, in UTC with seconds.
- `system/task_started` lines of other task types (`local_bash`, `local_agent`, others) create no record: a sub-agent comes from its `Agent` call, which the engine reads first. A `local_agent` line whose `tool_use_id` is the call of a sub-agent without `taskID` gives it the line's `task_id` as `taskID`: the CLI's id of the agent, which a later `task_started` names when the agent is resumed ([Agent states](#agent-states)).
- The titles of agents, the label of a workflow agent and the description of a sub-agent's call, and the fallback titles: Default chosen at design time.

### Agent states

`ChatAgentState` is the state of a workflow or an agent:

| State     | Meaning                                                             | Mark in the chat lists |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| running   | It works.                                                           | a spinner              |
| completed | It ended with its result.                                           | none                   |
| failed    | It ended with an error.                                             | a red dot              |
| stopped   | It was stopped, or its chat's Claude process ended while it worked. | none                   |

The marks are those of chat rows for the states running, idle and error ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)). A record is created running. A sub-agent that has ended runs again when its chat's Claude resumes it; every other record changes only while it runs, and one that has ended keeps its state:

| Record         | Event                                                                                                          | New state                             |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| workflow       | `system/task_updated` of the workflow with `patch.status` `completed`, `failed` or `killed`                    | completed, failed or stopped          |
| workflow       | `system/task_notification` of the workflow with `status` `completed`, `failed` or `stopped`                    | completed, failed or stopped          |
| workflow agent | its `workflow_agent` entry has the `state` `done` or `error`                                                   | completed or failed                   |
| workflow agent | a `result` entry with its `agentId` in the journal of the workflow folder                                      | completed                             |
| workflow agent | its workflow has ended and the last read of the folder did not end it                                          | stopped                               |
| sub-agent      | a `system/task_started` with `task_type` `local_agent` whose `task_id` is its `taskID`, while it has ended     | running                               |
| sub-agent      | a `system/task_notification` whose `tool_use_id` is its call, with `status` `completed`, `failed` or `stopped` | completed, failed or stopped          |
| sub-agent      | a `system/task_notification` whose `task_id` is its `taskID`, with `status` `completed`, `failed` or `stopped` | completed, failed or stopped          |
| sub-agent      | the `toolResult` item of its call, when the call's input has no `run_in_background` true                       | failed when `isError`, else completed |
| sub-agent      | a `result` line, when the call's input has no `run_in_background` true                                         | stopped                               |
| any            | the chat's Claude process exits                                                                                | stopped                               |
| any            | the engine reads it from the chat's agents file while it is running there                                      | stopped                               |

- The `workflow_agent` states `start` and `progress` leave an agent running. A sub-agent started in the background (`run_in_background` true) gets its result through `task_notification`; the `toolResult` of its call only says that it was launched ([Background work](#background-work)).
- A sub-agent that has ended, completed, failed or stopped, runs again when its chat's Claude resumes it with the `SendMessage` tool, as many times as Claude resumes it. Claude Code 2.1.309 reports it, checked against `stream-background-agent-resume.jsonl`, with a `system/task_started` line of `task_type` `local_agent` whose `task_id` is the agent's `taskID` and whose `tool_use_id` is the `SendMessage` call. The resumed agent's lines keep the `parent_tool_use_id` of its `Agent` call, so its new work is added to its transcript ([Sub-agent transcripts](#sub-agent-transcripts)), and its end comes in a `task_notification` with the same `task_id` and the `SendMessage` call as `tool_use_id`. On that `task_started` line the sub-agent becomes running and its `ended` is cleared; the change is stored and emitted as every change ([Storage of agents](#storage-of-agents)). A resumed sub-agent runs in the background: the `toolResult` and `result` rows no longer apply to it, and it ends by its `task_notification`, its process's exit or a later read of the agents file. A `task_started` line of a sub-agent that runs changes nothing.
- A sub-agent recorded without `taskID`, by an engine that did not record it yet, keeps its state when it is resumed. Default chosen at design time.
- The last row covers an engine that stopped, with the app, while a workflow or an agent worked: its chat's process ended with it. The engine does not rewrite the file for it.

### Workflow folder

The result of the `Workflow` call that launched a workflow ([Tool results](#tool-results)) is a text whose lines include:

```
Workflow launched in background. Task ID: wflx39ys6
Summary: Two probe agents
Transcript dir: /Users/example/.claude/projects/-tmp-example/1EADA864-B5CE-4D35-9071-C2FBF075F979/subagents/workflows/wf_0afd7514-9e5
Run ID: wf_0afd7514-9e5
```

The engine takes the workflow whose id follows `Task ID: `, sets its `runID` to the text after `Run ID: ` and follows the folder named after `Transcript dir: `, when that is an absolute path whose last component is the run id. A result without these lines changes nothing; a result whose task id names no workflow of the chat yet is applied when the `task_started` line of that task arrives. The CLI writes in that folder:

| File                        | Content                                                                                                                                                                        | Read by the engine                                                                                  |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `journal.jsonl`             | one JSON object per line: `launched`, a `started` entry with `agentId`, `label` and `phase` for each agent that starts, and a `result` entry with `agentId` for each that ends | `started` entries create workflow agents, `result` entries complete them; other entries are ignored |
| `agent-<agentId>.jsonl`     | the agent's transcript, one JSON object per line                                                                                                                               | mapped to items ([Agent transcript lines](#agent-transcript-lines)), for each agent of the workflow |
| `agent-<agentId>.meta.json` | the agent's `description` and `workflowPhase`                                                                                                                                  | not read                                                                                            |

- The engine reads the folder when the tool result arrives, then every second while the workflow runs, and a last time once it has ended. Each read takes the complete lines added to each file since the previous read. The interval: Default chosen at design time.
- A file that does not exist or cannot be read yet is read again at the next read; a line that is not valid JSON is skipped.
- The engine only reads these files; it never writes, moves or deletes them. What it read stays in the chat's own files ([Storage of agents](#storage-of-agents)), so the agents stay viewable after the CLI removes its session files.

### Agent transcript lines

Each line of `agent-<agentId>.jsonl` becomes items of the agent's transcript, of the item types of the chat transcript ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-items)):

| Line                                                                      | Items                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `assistant`                                                               | one item per block of `message.content`, mapped as [Assistant blocks](#assistant-blocks) map them: `assistant` for `text`, with `model` and `effort` null, `thinking` for a `thinking` block whose text is not empty, `toolUse` for `tool_use`, with `parentToolUseID` null; id `<message.id>#<n>`, `<n>` being the block's index in `message.content` plus the line's integer `apiBlockIndex`, 0 when the line has none |
| `user` whose `message.content` is a string and whose `isMeta` is not true | one `user` item with that text, `sender` `Claude` and no attachment; id the line's `uuid`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `user` whose `message.content` is an array                                | one `toolResult` item per `tool_result` block, mapped as [Tool results](#tool-results) map them, without images; and when it has `text` blocks, one `user` item with their texts joined with `\n`, `sender` `Claude`, id the line's `uuid`                                                                                                                                                                               |
| any other line (`attachment`, `api-request`, others)                      | none                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

- `created` is the line's `timestamp` truncated to seconds, else the time the engine read the line.
- The [size limits](#size-limits) apply. An item whose id the agent's transcript already holds is not added again.
- The sender `Claude` of the agent's task, the images left out, and the `model` and `effort` left null, so that the captions of a workflow agent's text read `Claude`: Default chosen at design time.

### Sub-agent transcripts

The transcript of a sub-agent is made of the chat's own items, which hold its work ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents)):

1. A `user` item with the call's input `prompt` (empty when it has none), `sender` `Claude`, id `<toolUseID>#prompt` and the `created` of the call's `toolUse` item.
2. Then, in transcript order, every `assistant`, `thinking` and `toolUse` item whose `parentToolUseID` is the call's `toolUseID`, or the `toolUseID` of an `Agent` or `Task` call among these items at any depth, and the `toolResult` item of each of these `toolUse` items.

When the engine creates a sub-agent, and when it adds to the chat's transcript an item of a sub-agent's transcript, it emits `chat.agentItem` with that item for the sub-agent, and for each sub-agent whose transcript holds the item. These transcripts are not stored a second time. Default chosen at design time.

### Storage of agents

- Each new record, and each change of a record, appends the whole record to the chat's agents file `chats/<chat id>/agents.jsonl` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-agents-file)), then the engine emits `chat.agents` with the chat's complete workflows and agents.
- Each item of a workflow agent's transcript is appended to the file `chats/<chat id>/agents/<agent id>.jsonl` ([Data model](data-model.md#agent-transcript-file)) and emitted as `chat.agentItem` {chatID, agentID, item}.
- The engine reads a chat's agents file the first time it needs the chat's records: for `chats.listAgents` of its project, for `chats.agentTranscript`, or when the chat's process reports a workflow or a sub-agent.
- The records of a closed chat stay; the chat lists hide them with their chat. `chats.delete` moves the chat's folder `chats/<chat id>/`, these files with it, to the Trash ([Persistence](#persistence)).

### Stopping a workflow

Stop Workflow in the context menu of a workflow row or of one of its agent rows, the Stop button of its [workflow view](#workflow-view) and the Stop button of the [agent view](#agent-view) of one of its agents call `chats.stopWorkflow` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsstopworkflow)) while the workflow runs. The engine writes the control request `stop_task` with the workflow's id to the chat's live process ([Control protocol](#control-protocol)), in every chat state, and remembers that it stopped that task. Claude Code 2.1.309 stops the workflow and its agents and reports it with these lines, checked against `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl`:

| Line                                                           | Engine reaction                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `system/task_updated` of the workflow, `patch.status` `killed` | the workflow and its running agents become stopped ([Agent states](#agent-states)) |
| `system/task_notification` of the workflow, `status` `stopped` | no notification turn becomes pending ([Background work](#background-work))         |
| `system/background_tasks_changed` without the workflow         | the process's background work ends when nothing else runs                          |
| `control_response` success for the request, `response` `{}`    | nothing                                                                            |

- The CLI starts no CLI turn for a workflow stopped this way: Claude learns that it was stopped with its next message.
- An error answer, or no answer within 30 s, adds the system item `The workflow “<summary>” was not stopped: <description>.` with level warning, `<description>` being the CLI's error or `no answer`; the workflow keeps running. Default chosen at design time.
- Sub-agents have no Stop control. Stop in the composer stops the turn as [Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn) describes; a sub-agent then ends as [Agent states](#agent-states) describes.

### Agent view

Selecting an agent row ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)) shows the agent view in place of the chat view: beside the list in the Chats section, under the list in the Chats tab of a ticket or a bundle.

- The header, 36 pt high as the chat view's header ([Chat view](#chat-view)), shows the agent's title, a chip with `Agent`, followed by ` · <phase>` for an agent with a phase, and the agent's state mark. For an agent of a running workflow, a `Stop` button with the SF Symbol `stop.fill` at its trailing edge stops the whole workflow ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)); a sub-agent's view has none.
- Under the header, the agent's transcript, loaded with `chats.agentTranscript` and kept current by `chat.agentItem` ([UI state](#ui-state)), in the transcript web view of [Transcript view](#transcript-view): the same [item rendering](#item-rendering), [tool cards](#tool-cards), links, find and copying. A tool card without result shows its spinner while the agent runs.
- The view is read-only: it has no composer, no status line and no pinned cards.
- Layout, texts and symbol: Default chosen at design time.

### Workflow view

Selecting a workflow row shows the workflow view in the same place:

- The header, 36 pt high as the chat view's header ([Chat view](#chat-view)), shows the workflow's summary, a chip with `Workflow`, followed by ` · <name>` when the workflow has a name, the workflow's state mark and, while it runs, a `Stop` button with the SF Symbol `stop.fill` that stops it ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)).
- Under the header, one row per agent of the workflow, in the order of their `started`: the agent's state mark, its title, its phase in secondary color, and at the trailing edge its state, `Running`, `Completed`, `Failed` or `Stopped`. A click on a row selects that agent's row and shows its agent view. A workflow without agent yet shows `No agents yet`.
- Layout and texts: Default chosen at design time.

## Chat views and windows

### Where chats appear

| Place                    | Content                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Inspector (⌥⌘0)          | The project chat panel: a picker of the project's chats of kind project, New Chat, and the selected chat's view ([UI](ui.md#inspector)).                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Chats section (⌘4)       | The project chats and feedback chats of the project, and its ticket, bundle and CI chats whose ticket or bundle no longer exists ([Deleting](tickets.md#deleting)), in one list without any group title, newest `updated` first; beside it, the selected chat's view, or `<n> Chats Selected` and the Close button while several are selected. |
| Ticket detail, Chats tab | The ticket's chats, each followed by the CI chats it orchestrates, Implement and New Chat ([Tickets](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)).                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Bundle detail, Chats tab | The bundle's chats, each followed by the ticket chats and CI chats it orchestrates, Implement All and New Chat ([Tickets](tickets.md#implement-all)).                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Mentioned In tabs        | The other chats of the device that mention the ticket or the bundle, closed chats included, in the ticket and bundle details ([Tickets](tickets.md#mentioned-in-tab)).                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Chat window              | One chat in its own window, opened by Open in New Window ([UI](ui.md#windows)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

- The Chats section lists the chats the user starts in the project, its project chats and feedback chats, and the chats whose ticket or bundle was deleted ([Deleting](tickets.md#deleting)): a ticket chat whose `ticket`, a bundle chat whose `bundle`, and a CI chat whose `ticket` or `bundle` no longer exists. While its ticket or bundle exists, a ticket chat is listed only in its ticket's Chats tab, a bundle chat only in its bundle's Chats tab, and a CI chat only under its orchestrator in the Chats tab of its ticket or bundle ([CI chats](#ci-chats)); an orchestrated ticket chat is listed in its ticket's Chats tab and under its bundle chat in the bundle's Chats tab ([Orchestrated ticket chats](#orchestrated-ticket-chats)). The list has no group titles, and a feedback chat's row is the same as a project chat's row. A navigation to a chat shows it where it is listed ([Resolving a target](ui.md#resolving-a-target)).
- While the tickets or the bundles of the project are not loaded yet, a chat linked to a ticket or a bundle counts as listed on it, so the Chats section does not show it. Default chosen at design time.
- A row of the Chats section shows the title, the linked ticket or bundle id, a state mark (none for idle, a spinner for starting and running, an orange dot for waitingPermission and waitingAnswers, a red dot for error) and the relative `updated` time. The list starts with its title row: the title `Chats` in the list's pane header, 36 pt high, its bottom separator on one line with those of the chat view, of the inspector and of the other sections ([Pane headers](ui.md#pane-headers)). The list header is under the title row's separator, 36 pt high, its buttons centred vertically, with a separator under it; it has a New Chat button (`plus.bubble`). With no chat, the section shows `No chats yet` and the same button, without a title row and without a header. The button shows a draft project chat in the section's chat view with the focus in its composer; its first Send creates the chat, which the list shows and which becomes the only selected chat (`selectedChatIDs`). Default chosen at design time.
- Selection: click, ⌘-click and ⇧-click; ⌘A selects every visible chat, as in the [Ticket list](tickets.md#ticket-list). With one chat selected, its view shows beside the list; with several, the section shows `<n> Chats Selected`, `<n>` being the number of selected chats, and a Close button, which closes them ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); with none, the area beside the list is empty. The selection is `ProjectViewState.selectedChatIDs` ([WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). When a selected chat is deleted, the section removes it from its selection. The Chats tabs of tickets and bundles keep a single selected chat ([Ticket chats and Implement](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)), and so do their Mentioned In tabs ([Mentioned In tab](tickets.md#mentioned-in-tab)).
- The context menu of the chat rows of the Chats section acts on the selected chats; right-clicking a chat row that is not selected selects it first. Its items are Open in New Window, Rename…, Close and Delete…. With one chat selected every item is enabled, except Close while that chat's `closed` is true; with several, Close is the only enabled item, enabled when at least one of them has `closed` false ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)), and Open in New Window, Rename… and Delete… are disabled. Rename… edits the row's title in place, as the chat view's title. ⌫ and ⌘⌫ act as Delete… while one chat is selected ([Menus and keyboard shortcuts](ui.md#menus-and-keyboard-shortcuts)). The place of Close in the menu: Default chosen at design time.
- A chat is closed while its `closed` is true ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)), or while its linked ticket, the `ticket` of a ticket chat, of a CI chat or of a feedback chat linked to the ticket it created, has the status `done` ([Status rules](tickets.md#status-rules)). It is open again once it is reopened, or a message delivered to it sets `closed` to false, and its ticket is no longer `done`, for example after the user sets it back to To Do or In Progress. Each client computes it from the chats and tickets it holds, so the lists change as `chat.updated` and `ticket.updated` arrive.
- The list header of the Chats section has a filter button with the symbol `line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle`, left of New Chat, that hides or shows the closed chats. Its symbol is filled (`line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle.fill`) while closed chats are hidden. Its tooltip is `Show Closed Chats` while closed chats are hidden and `Hide Closed Chats` while they are shown. The place of the button: Default chosen at design time.
- Closed chats are hidden by default. The filter state is `ProjectViewState.hidesClosedChats` ([WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)): one value per window and project, shared by the Chats section and the Chats tabs of tickets and bundles ([Ticket chats and Implement](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)), so a click on the button in any of these lists changes all of them. It is persisted with the window and restored after a relaunch, for every project the window showed.
- When selected chats become hidden, because the filter is turned on or the chats become closed, for example after Close, their rows disappear and the area beside the list, the chat's view or `<n> Chats Selected`, stays until another chat is selected. A selection made in the list holds only the chats it lists.
- While the filter hides every chat of the project, the list shows `No open chats` and a New Chat button under its header, which keeps the filter button. The inspector's picker lists closed chats too.
- The Chats section's list, its title row and its header included, has the same background as the [Ticket list](tickets.md#ticket-list) and the list of the [Bundles section](tickets.md#bundles-section): the system control background colour (`NSColor.controlBackgroundColor`), in light and dark appearance.
- Under each chat row of the Chats section and of the Chats tabs of tickets and bundles, the ticket chats and CI chats under their orchestrator included, come the rows of the chat's workflows and sub-agents ([Agents](#agents)), in the order of their `started`: a workflow row titled with the workflow's summary, indented one step, followed by the rows of its agents, indented two steps, in the order of their `started`; and a row for each sub-agent, indented one step. A row shows an SF Symbol, `point.3.connected.trianglepath.dotted` for a workflow and `person.crop.circle` for an agent, its title, the phase of a workflow agent in secondary color, and the mark of its state ([Agent states](#agent-states)). Symbols and indentation: Default chosen at design time.
- A running workflow, with every agent row of it, and a running sub-agent are always shown. Once a workflow or a sub-agent has ended, its rows fold under the chat row, which then shows a disclosure triangle at its leading edge: pointing right while the ended rows are folded, down while they are shown; a click on it shows or folds them. They start folded. The unfolded chats are kept per window and per project while the window is open, shared by the Chats section and the Chats tabs (`expandedAgentChats` of [WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). Default chosen at design time.
- A click on a workflow row shows its [workflow view](#workflow-view), and on an agent row its [agent view](#agent-view), in place of the chat view: beside the list in the Chats section, under the list in a Chats tab (`selectedChatAgent` and `chatsTabAgentSelection` of [WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). Selecting a chat row shows its chat view again, and so does a navigation to a chat. In the Chats section a workflow or agent row is selected alone, and its chat becomes the only selected chat (`selectedChatIDs`): a selection of several rows, made with ⌘-click, ⇧-click or ⌘A, holds only the chats among them.
- While a workflow runs, the context menu of its workflow row and of each of its agent rows has the item Stop Workflow ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)); a failed call shows the alert `Stop Workflow failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). These rows have no other item, sub-agent rows have no context menu, and right-clicking these rows does not select them.
- The rows of a chat hidden by the closed-chats filter are hidden with it. When the chat of the shown workflow or agent is deleted, the list clears its selection, as for a deleted chat.
- Closing a chat window does not stop Claude. Window size, title, subtitle and restoration are in [UI](ui.md#windows).

### Draft chats

New Chat (⌥⌘N, or a New Chat button) shows a draft: an empty chat view whose composer uses the default settings ([Fields and defaults](#fields-and-defaults)) and has the keyboard focus. Nothing is stored until the first Send, which calls `chats.create` with the message and the draft's settings. A draft that is closed or replaced is discarded. Default chosen at design time.

### Chat view

From top to bottom: the header, the transcript, the pinned permission card, the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned color card, the pinned sound card, the pinned shortcut card, the pinned font card or the pinned animation card, the status line ([Chat states](#chat-states)), the composer. The pinned cards take at most half of the view's height. They scroll inside that space only when they are taller than it; while they fit, they neither scroll nor bounce under a scroll gesture. In both cases, wide code blocks and tables inside the cards scroll horizontally inside their own box. A scroll gesture over a text field that does not scroll, because its text fits in its visible lines, goes to the area around the field: over the `Answer another way` field of a card, the cards scroll when they are taller than their space.

The chat view's background is the window background colour, except in the inspector, where it draws none and shows the inspector's frosted material, with a transparent transcript ([Web view and bridge](#web-view-and-bridge)).

The [agent view](#agent-view) and the [workflow view](#workflow-view) take the place of the chat view while an agent row or a workflow row is selected ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)); they have the chat view's background.

Header (in the Chats section, the Chats tabs and Mentioned In tabs of tickets and bundles and chat windows; the inspector shows its picker instead), on one row 36 pt high, its content centred vertically. In the Chats section it is the chat view's pane header, its bottom separator on one line with those of the list, of the inspector and of the other sections ([Pane headers](ui.md#pane-headers)); the Chats tabs, the Mentioned In tabs and the chat windows give it the same height. Default chosen at design time. The header shows:

- The title. Double-click edits it in place: Return calls `chats.rename`, Escape and leaving the field cancel. Default chosen at design time.
- A chip with the kind and, for ticket, bundle, CI and linked feedback chats, the ticket or bundle id; clicking the id navigates to it (`ticket` or `bundle` target), with the same window choice as [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files).
- Open in New Window (hidden inside a chat window) and a More menu with Rename… and Delete…, preceded by Close while the chat's `closed` is false and by Reopen while it is true. The header of a draft shows `New Chat` and the kind chip only, since nothing is stored yet. Default chosen at design time.
- Close asks `Close the chat “<title>”?` with the buttons Close and Cancel, then closes the chat ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); `This conversation is over` replaces the composer in every view of the chat. A failed call shows the alert `Close failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)).
- Reopen calls `chats.reopen` ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); the composer shows again in every view of the chat. A failed call shows the alert `Reopen failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). The inspector shows its picker instead of the header, so a project chat is closed, and a closed project chat reopened, from a chat window or the Chats section.
- Delete… asks `Delete the chat “<title>”?` with the informative text `Its transcript and attachments are moved to the Trash.` and the buttons Delete (destructive) and Cancel.

### UI state

`DeviceWorkspace.transcripts` keeps one `TranscriptState` per chat shown in a view of that device ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)): the items in transcript order and the streaming buffers. Pending permission requests, the incomplete question set, the symbol request without answer, the color request without answer, the sound request without answer, the shortcut request without answer, the font request without answer and the animation request without answer are read from the items. Every view of the chat in windows of the same device shares this state. Opening a chat calls `chats.get`; the events received while it is in flight are applied after its result, in arrival order ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)).

`DeviceWorkspace.agents` keeps the workflows and agents of every chat of the device, by chat id: the workspace loads them with `chats.listAgents` for every project, after its `chats.list`, and keeps them current with `chat.agents`. `DeviceWorkspace.agentTranscripts` keeps one `TranscriptState` per agent shown in a view, by `<chat id>/<agent id>`: opening the agent view calls `chats.agentTranscript`, `chat.agentItem` adds or replaces its items, and the state is dropped when the last view of the agent closes. Both follow the replay rule ([Seeding calls and the replay rule](remote.md#seeding-calls-and-the-replay-rule)).

## Implement and feedback chats

| Method                  | Chat created                                                                                                                                                                                                     | First message (`sender`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
