| `chats.implementTicket` | kind ticket, `ticket` = the ticket id, `questionsBlocked` true; the chat id is appended to the ticket's `chats`; `orchestrator` = the calling chat for an [orchestrated ticket chat](#orchestrated-ticket-chats) | `/hephaestus-skills:implement-ticket <ticket id>`, or `/hephaestus-skills:implement-ticket <ticket id> <bundle id>` for an orchestrated ticket chat, with the bundle of its orchestrator (the name of the user's device; `Claude` when called by `implement_ticket`) |
| `chats.implementBundle` | kind bundle, `bundle` = the bundle id; the chat id is appended to the bundle's `chats`                                                                                                                           | `/hephaestus-skills:implement-bundle <bundle id>` (the name of the user's device; `Claude` when called by `implement_bundle`)                                                                                                                                        |
| `chats.createFeedback`  | kind feedback, `ticket` null                                                                                                                                                                                     | `/hephaestus-skills:feedback`, a line break, then the [feedback message](#feedback-message), with the screenshot, when the feedback has one, and the added files as its attachments (the name of the device that sent the feedback)                                  |
| `chats.watchCI`         | kind ci, `orchestrator` = the calling chat, `ticket` or `bundle` = the calling chat's; the chat id is appended to that ticket's or bundle's `chats` ([CI chats](#ci-chats))                                      | the CI message `Watch the CI of commit <commit>.` (`Claude`)                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

- A done ticket, and a bundle without a ticket left to implement, are refused with `conflict` ([Tickets](tickets.md#errors)).
- The chat that `chats.implementTicket` creates is an implement chat ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#implement-chats)): until the user writes in it, Claude asks no question, except how to proceed after a CI failure ([CI chats](#ci-chats)), and makes the choices itself. Its metadata holds `questionsBlocked: true` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)), and while the key is set `chats.postQuestions`, `chats.postSymbol`, `chats.postColor`, `chats.postSound`, `chats.postShortcut`, `chats.postFont` and `chats.postAnimation` fail with `forbidden` and `This chat implements a ticket and cannot ask questions: decide yourself and note the choice in the completion comment.`, so `ask_questions` and the other ask tools show nothing to the user ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)), except the one question set that `ask_questions` posts after a CI failure report ([Reports to the orchestrator](#reports-to-the-orchestrator)).
- The first message the user sends in an implement chat with `chats.send`, from any device, removes the key, in the metadata write that follows its `user` item; the first message that calls `implement-ticket` does not.
- Each first message calls an [internal skill](skills.md#internal-skills), which holds the steps of the chat's task: the CLI runs the skill with the ids, or the feedback message, as its arguments ([Calls](skills.md#calls)). The ids are written as stored, for example `/hephaestus-skills:implement-ticket HEPH-12 HEPH-B2`. From then on Claude asks as in the other chats. A ticket chat created by `chats.create`, with New Chat, never has the key ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatssend)).
- The new chat's settings are the `settings` param: the settings chosen in the [Implement sheet](tickets.md#implement-sheet) for Implement and Implement All, those of the feedback sheet's [chat settings menus](feedback.md#chat-settings-menus) for feedback. `implement_ticket` and `implement_bundle` send none, so the chat takes the project's `chatDefaults`, else the host's `defaultChatSettings`, except an orchestrated ticket chat, which takes its orchestrator's settings ([Fields and defaults](#fields-and-defaults)). `watch_ci` sends none either: a CI chat takes its orchestrator's settings ([CI chats](#ci-chats)).
- The client that called the method selects the new chat: in the ticket Chats tab, in the bundle Chats tab, or, for feedback, in the Chats section of a main window chosen as [Feedback sheet](feedback.md#feedback-sheet) specifies, without opening a chat window. Chats started by `implement_ticket`, `implement_bundle` and `watch_ci` appear in the Chats tabs of their ticket or bundle and are not opened. A ticket, bundle or CI chat is listed on its ticket or bundle, and in the Chats section only once that ticket or bundle is deleted; a feedback chat is listed in the Chats section ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)).
- The screenshot of a feedback chat is the first attachment of its first user message, sent to Claude as an `image` block before the text ([User messages](#user-messages)); the first message of feedback sent without a screenshot has no such attachment ([Feedback message](#feedback-message)). The link between the feedback chat and the ticket Claude creates is in [Feedback](feedback.md#resulting-ticket).
- The files that the user added in the feedback sheet ([Added files](feedback.md#added-files)) are the attachments of that message that follow the screenshot, and its only attachments when the feedback has no screenshot ([Feedback message](#feedback-message)).

### Orchestrated ticket chats

The bundle chat started by Implement All or `implement_bundle` is the orchestrator of its bundle: it starts the ticket chats of its tickets, which run in parallel, each on its own branch in its own git worktree, waits for their reports and merges their branches ([Implement All](tickets.md#implement-all), [Claude workflow](workflow.md#bundles)).

- `chats.implementTicket` called by a `claude` actor whose chat is of kind `bundle`, for a ticket that this chat's bundle lists, creates an orchestrated ticket chat: its `orchestrator` is the calling chat ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)), and its first message is `/hephaestus-skills:implement-ticket <ticket id> <bundle id>`, with the bundle of the calling chat. Every other call creates a ticket chat without `orchestrator`, whose first message is `/hephaestus-skills:implement-ticket <ticket id>`.
- The orchestrated ticket chat takes the orchestrator's `settings`, its model, effort, permission mode and ultracode, as they are when `chats.implementTicket` is called. A change of the orchestrator's settings applies to the ticket chats it starts afterwards; the ticket chats already started keep theirs, and the composer of each changes them.
- The orchestrated ticket chat is in its ticket's `chats`. It is listed in the ticket's Chats tab, and in the bundle's Chats tab under its orchestrator ([Bundles section](tickets.md#bundles-section)). It is an implement chat ([Implement and feedback chats](#implement-and-feedback-chats)): it asks no questions until the user writes in it, and it asks its permission prompts in its own chat, where the user answers them.

#### Branch and worktree

Before it creates an orchestrated ticket chat, `chats.implementTicket` creates the chat's branch and worktree in the git repository of the project root. `<branch>` is `<bundle id>/<ticket id>`, with the bundle of the orchestrator chat, for example `HEPH-B18/HEPH-56`. `<worktree>` is `<data dir>/Worktrees/<project id>/<ticket id>` ([Data model](data-model.md#device-data-directory)). The steps, each a `git` command run in the project root ([Child processes](architecture.md#child-processes)):

1. `git worktree list --porcelain`. When `<worktree>` is one of the worktrees listed, `git worktree remove --force --force <worktree>`.
2. When the folder `<worktree>` still exists, the engine deletes it; then `git worktree prune`.
3. When `git show-ref --verify --quiet refs/heads/<branch>` exits with status 0, `git branch -D <branch>`.
4. `git worktree add -b <branch> <worktree> HEAD`: the branch starts from the commit of the project root's current branch, and the worktree checks it out.

- Steps 1 to 3 remove the worktree and the branch that an earlier chat of the ticket left: after a retry, a skipped ticket or a stopped bundle, the new chat starts on a fresh branch, and the work left on the old branch and in the old worktree is lost.
- When a step cannot be done, `chats.implementTicket` creates no chat and fails with `unavailable` and `Cannot create the branch and worktree of <TICKET-ID>: <reason>`:

| The step                                                                                         | `<reason>`                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| needs `git`, which is not available ([Child processes](architecture.md#child-processes), rule 5) | `git is not available`                                                                         |
| runs a command that cannot start                                                                 | `git cannot start: <error>`                                                                    |
| runs a command that does not end within 60 seconds                                               | `git did not finish within 60 seconds`                                                         |
| runs a command that exits with a non-zero status (except `git show-ref`)                         | the command's standard error, trimmed, or its standard output when the standard error is empty |
| cannot delete the remaining folder, or create the folder `Worktrees/<project id>/`               | `<path> cannot be deleted: <error>` or `<path> cannot be created: <error>`                     |

- In a project that is not a git repository, step 1 fails with git's `fatal: not a git repository` message.
- The engine prepares one worktree at a time, so that calls made together never run git in the same repository at once.

The commands, the deletion of a remaining folder, the timeout, the reasons and preparing one worktree at a time: Default chosen at design time.

#### Working directory

- An orchestrated ticket chat works in its worktree: its Claude process starts there at every launch, a resume included ([Launch](#launch)), and its system prompt names the worktree and the branch as its working directory (`{{workingDirectory}}`, [Placeholders](#placeholders)).
- The folder of the worktree it works in is `<worktree>` when the project root is the top folder of its git repository (the folder that holds `.git`). For a project whose root is a subfolder of the repository, a sub project for example, it is the same subfolder of `<worktree>`. Default chosen at design time.
- This holds at a launch while the chat's orchestrator chat exists, the chat is its ticket's newest orchestrated ticket chat (the last id of the ticket's `chats` whose chat is a ticket chat with an `orchestrator`) and that folder exists. Otherwise the chat works in the project root, as the other chats do: after the bundle chat removed the worktree, or after a retry gave the worktree to a newer chat of the ticket. The condition on the orchestrator chat: Default chosen at design time.
- The ticket chat commits on its branch and does not push. The orchestrator rebases the branch onto the project root's current branch, fast-forwards that branch to it, and removes the worktree and the branch after its push ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#bundles)).
- The chats that are not orchestrated ticket chats keep the project root.

#### Reports to the orchestrator

When the state of a chat with an `orchestrator`, an orchestrated ticket chat or a [CI chat](#ci-chats), goes from `starting`, `running` or `waitingPermission` to `idle` or `error`, the engine sends the orchestrator chat one message, as the `system` actor, so its `sender` is the main device's name ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatssend)):

| The turn                                                                                        | Message of an orchestrated ticket chat                                       | Message of a CI chat                                             |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
| was stopped with Stop                                                                           | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> was stopped.`                                           | `CI chat was stopped.`                                           |
| left the chat in state `error`, or its last `result` has `isError` without being a stopped turn | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> ended in error: <error message>`                        | `CI chat ended in error: <error message>`                        |
| ended otherwise                                                                                 | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> finished its turn:`, a blank line, then the final reply | `CI chat finished its turn:`, a blank line, then the final reply |

- `<TICKET-ID>` is the ticket chat's `ticket`. The final reply is the text of the turn's last `assistant` item without `parentToolUseID`, empty when the turn has none. The error message is the text of the turn's last `system` item of level `error` when there is one, otherwise the `summary` of the turn's last `result`.
- A report of a CI chat is a CI failure report unless it is `CI chat finished its turn:` and the first line of the final reply that is not empty, trimmed, is `CI passed.` or `CI not checked.` ([Default: ci](skills.md#default-ci)). Stopped and errored CI chats thus give CI failure reports.
- When the engine has sent a CI failure report to an orchestrator whose metadata holds `questionsBlocked`, an implement chat in which the user has not written, it accepts one question set from that chat despite the key, so that Claude can ask the user how to proceed ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#waiting-for-ci)): the next `chats.postQuestions` of the chat passes the `questionsBlocked` check, and a question set it stores uses up the permission. Another report of a CI chat that is not a CI failure report, and the deletion of the chat, remove the permission. The permission is kept in memory only, and the other requests, `chats.postSymbol` to `chats.postAnimation`, stay refused. Default chosen at design time.
- A turn that ends in `waitingAnswers`, on `ask_questions`, sends nothing: the orchestrator keeps waiting, and the turn that follows the answers reports.
- Nothing is sent while the engine stops, or when the orchestrator chat no longer exists or is being deleted; `engine.log` records the skipped message.
- The orchestrator receives the message in any state ([Chat states](#chat-states)): during one of its turns, the CLI runs it after that turn. A closed orchestrator chat is reopened by the message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).
- The turns of a chat with an `orchestrator` post no "finished" notification, and an orchestrator posts one only for a turn that ends while none of the chats it orchestrates is working; their "needs you" notifications are posted as for every chat ([Notifications](#notifications)).

The other rules of this section are Default chosen at design time, except the message texts, those of a CI chat included, the settings taken from the orchestrator, the branch, the worktree and its folder, the working directory, the removal of an earlier branch and worktree, and the failure when they cannot be created, which the user chose.

### CI chats

In a project that has a [GitHub page](projects.md#github-page), the chat that pushes an implementation starts a CI chat with the MCP tool `watch_ci`, which follows the CI of the pushed commit and reports to it ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#waiting-for-ci)). That chat is the CI chat's orchestrator: the implement chat of a single ticket, another ticket chat that pushed, or the bundle chat after its single push.

- `chats.watchCI` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatswatchci)), called by the orchestrator's Claude, creates a chat of kind `ci` in the orchestrator's project: its `orchestrator` is the calling chat ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)); its `ticket` is the calling chat's ticket when the calling chat is a ticket chat, its `bundle` the calling chat's bundle when it is a bundle chat; the chat id is appended to the `chats` of that ticket or bundle.
- Only a ticket chat or a bundle chat without `orchestrator`, in a project that has a GitHub page, can call it: an orchestrated ticket chat does not push, a CI chat makes no commit, and a project chat or a feedback chat that implements a ticket at the user's request starts no CI chat ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#waiting-for-ci)). Default chosen at design time.
- The CI chat takes the orchestrator's `settings`, its model with its Ultracode toggle, its effort and its permission mode, as they are when `chats.watchCI` is called.
- Its first user message, with `sender` `Claude`, is the CI message `Watch the CI of commit <commit>.`, `<commit>` being the full hash given to `watch_ci` in lowercase. Default chosen at design time.
- Its system prompt is the rendered ci skill alone, without the common skill ([Default: ci](skills.md#default-ci)): with the default skill, Claude waits with the gh CLI for every workflow run of the commit on the project's GitHub repository, then ends its turn with a final reply whose first line is `CI passed.`, `CI failed.` or `CI not checked.`; it changes no file, makes no commit and asks no question. The engine does not enforce these three rules. Default chosen at design time.
- The engine reports as soon as the CI chat's turn ends, so the default ci skill keeps the turn going until every run has ended: Claude runs each command in the foreground, and when the shell tool moves a command that runs past its time limit to the background, 10 minutes for the Bash tool of the Claude CLI, it stops that task and watches again. Default chosen at design time.
- It works in the project root, as every chat other than an orchestrated ticket chat ([Working directory](#working-directory)). It is not an implement chat and has no `questionsBlocked`.
- When its turn ends, the engine sends the orchestrator `CI chat finished its turn:` with the final reply, `CI chat ended in error: <error message>` or `CI chat was stopped.` ([Reports to the orchestrator](#reports-to-the-orchestrator)). After a CI failure report, an implement chat may ask the user one question set.
- It is in the `chats` of its ticket or bundle. It is listed only in the Chats tab of its ticket or bundle, under its orchestrator, as the ticket chats a bundle chat starts, and in the Chats section once that ticket or bundle is deleted ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)). It asks its permission prompts in its own chat, where the user answers them; `watch_ci` itself follows the orchestrator's permission mode, as `implement_ticket` does ([Command line](#command-line)).

### Feedback message

`chats.createFeedback` composes the text of the feedback chat's first user message from the comment and the FeedbackContext ([Feedback](feedback.md#feedback-context)): the line `/hephaestus-skills:feedback`, which calls the internal skill `feedback` ([Internal skills](skills.md#internal-skills)), then the feedback message. Default chosen at design time.

```
/hephaestus-skills:feedback
<comment>

Feedback context:
- App: HephaestusV2 v<appVersion>(<build>)
- Device: <device>
- Project: <project>
- Section: <section>
- Item: <item>
- Screenshot: <screenshot path>
- Attachment: <attachment path>
```

| Placeholder         | Value                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<comment>`         | the comment, trimmed; `(no comment)` when it is empty                                                                                                 |
| `<device>`          | `deviceName`, or `<deviceName>, connected to <mainDeviceName>` when the two differ                                                                    |
| `<project>`         | `<project name> (<projectID>)`, the identifier standing for the name of a project that is not loaded; the line is left out when `projectID` is absent |
| `<section>`         | `section`; the line is left out when it is absent                                                                                                     |
| `<item>`            | `item`; the line is left out when it is absent                                                                                                        |
| `<screenshot path>` | absolute path, on the main device, of the stored screenshot; the line is left out when the feedback has no screenshot                                 |
| `<attachment path>` | absolute path, on the main device, of a stored added file: one line per file, in the order they were added, and none when no file was added           |

- Lines are separated by `\n`; the text does not end with a line break. The `user` item stores this text.
- The screenshot is the message's first attachment and is sent as an `image` block, before the text block ([User messages](#user-messages)); its path is in the text, so Claude can attach it to the ticket. Feedback sent without a screenshot, which the user left out or which could not be captured ([Feedback](feedback.md#screenshot-preview)), gives a message without that attachment, without its `image` block and without the `Screenshot:` line.
- The files that the user added in the feedback sheet ([Added files](feedback.md#added-files)) are the message's next attachments, in the order they were added, and each one has its `Attachment:` line, in the same order, after the `Screenshot:` line, or after the last context line when the feedback has no screenshot: the `Attachment:` lines are the last lines of the text. An added file that meets the image rule of [User messages](#user-messages) is also sent as an `image` block, after the screenshot's. The message gets no `Attached files:` list, since the path of every attachment is already in the text, so Claude can attach each added file to the ticket too. Feedback with neither a screenshot nor an added file gives a message with no attachment and no `image` block.
- The CLI runs the skill with the lines after the first one as its arguments ([Calls](skills.md#calls)).

Example, for feedback sent from a remote window of the MacBook showing the device Studio:

```
/hephaestus-skills:feedback
The lock icon disappears when I drag a locked tab to another position.

Feedback context:
- App: HephaestusV2 v0.4.0(57)
- Device: MacBook, connected to Studio
- Project: Hephaestus V2 (HEPH)
- Section: web
- Item: https://github.com/
- Screenshot: /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/chats/3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301/1B4E28BA-2FA1-11D2-883F-0016D3CCA427-screenshot.png
```

The last lines of that message when the user added a log file and an image in the sheet:

```
- Screenshot: /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/chats/3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301/1B4E28BA-2FA1-11D2-883F-0016D3CCA427-screenshot.png
- Attachment: /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/chats/3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301/9B2D6C1E-0F4A-4E8B-A1C2-3D4E5F607182-web-tabs.log
- Attachment: /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/chats/3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301/5F0C2A7D-8E3B-4D61-9A47-C2B1E0F3D485-tab-strip.png
```

## Haiku naming

A Haiku call names tickets created without a title, bundles created without a title, and every chat after its first user message. A summary call, the same command with another prompt, writes the detail of the chat notifications ([Summary call](#summary-call)).

### Command

```
<CLI path> -p --model haiku --output-format json --no-session-persistence --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config '{"mcpServers":{}}' --system-prompt <naming prompt> <input>
```

- The command is shown with shell quoting; the engine passes each argument as one argv element, without a shell.
- `<naming prompt>` is the text of the internal skill naming-ticket, naming-bundle or naming-chat, or notification-summary for a summary call, read from the library at every call and passed unchanged ([Composition and rendering](#composition-and-rendering)). `<input>` is built as below and never starts with `-`.
- Working directory: `<data dir>/Runtime/claude-cwd/`, an empty folder created when missing ([Architecture](architecture.md#child-processes)); environment: [Environment](#environment). Default chosen at design time.
- Timeout 30 s: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 2 s later ([Signals](#signals)); the provisional title stays.
- At most 2 naming calls run at once; the others wait in first-in first-out order. Default chosen at design time.
- Failures and timeouts are written to `engine.log`.

### Inputs

| Subject | `<input>`                                                                              | Maximum title length | Provisional title                       |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| ticket  | `Ticket description:` + `\n` + the description                                         | 70                   | [Tickets](tickets.md#titles-and-naming) |
| bundle  | `Tickets:` + `\n` + one line `<ticket id>: <ticket title>` per ticket, in bundle order | 60                   | [Tickets](tickets.md#bundle-rules)      |
| chat    | `First message:` + `\n` + the text of the chat's first `user` item that has text       | 60                   | `New Chat`                              |

- The first line of each `<input>` (the label) and the part after it, cut at 4,000 characters: Default chosen at design time.
- When the first user message of a chat has no text, the chat is named from the first later user message that has text.

### Output cleanup

1. Parse stdout as one JSON object. A parse failure, a non-zero exit code or `is_error` true keeps the provisional title.
2. Take `result`, trim it, and keep its first line.
3. Remove a leading `Title:`, `Ticket title:`, `Bundle title:` or `Chat title:` (any letter case) and trim. Default chosen at design time.
4. Remove surrounding quote characters (`"`, `'`, `“`, `”`, `‘`, `’`, `«`, `»`, `` ` ``) from both ends, repeatedly, and trim.
5. Remove trailing periods and trim. Steps 4 and 5 run again, in this order, until neither changes the text: `` `Fix it`. `` and `"Fix it."` both give `Fix it`. Default chosen at design time.
6. When the text is longer than the maximum, cut it at the last space at or before the maximum (at the maximum when there is no space) and trim. Default chosen at design time.
7. An empty text keeps the provisional title.

### Applying the title

- The Haiku title replaces the title only when the current title still equals the provisional title, so a title set meanwhile by the user or by Claude wins.
- The update emits `ticket.updated`, `bundle.updated` or `chat.updated` with origin `system`. Chat windows follow the chat title.
- Naming is not retried, after a failure or after an app restart.

### Summary call

A summary call writes the detail of a chat notification ([Notifications](#notifications)). It runs the [command](#command) with the internal skill notification-summary as its prompt, in the same working directory and environment. After 30 s it receives SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 2 s later ([Signals](#signals)), and the notification takes the fallback detail.

| Event                        | `<input>`                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| finished                     | `Claude's reply:` + `\n` + Claude's last reply of the turn                                                                       |
| needs you, permission        | `Permission request:` + `\n` + `Tool: <tool name>` + `\n` + `Input: <input>`, the input as JSON without indentation, keys sorted |
| needs you, question set      | `Questions:` + `\n` + one line `<label>: <question>` per question, in label order                                                |
| needs you, symbol request    | `Symbol request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                          |
| needs you, color request     | `Color request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                           |
| needs you, sound request     | `Sound request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                           |
| needs you, shortcut request  | `Shortcut request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                        |
| needs you, font request      | `Font request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                            |
| needs you, animation request | `Animation request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                       |

- The part after the label is cut at 4,000 characters, as for naming.
- At most 2 summary calls run at once, besides the naming calls; the others wait in first-in first-out order. While the Claude CLI is unavailable the call fails at once. Failures and timeouts are written to `engine.log`.
- Output cleanup:
  1. Parse stdout as one JSON object. A parse failure, a non-zero exit code or `is_error` true gives the fallback detail.
  2. Take `result`, trim it, and replace each run of white space, line breaks included, with one space.
  3. Remove a leading `Summary:` (any letter case) and trim.
  4. Remove surrounding quote characters (those of [Output cleanup](#output-cleanup)) from both ends, repeatedly, and trim.
  5. When the text is longer than 150 characters, cut it at the last space at or before the maximum, 150 (at the maximum when there is no space), and trim, as naming step 6 does.
  6. An empty text gives the fallback detail.

The inputs, the queue and the cleanup are Default chosen at design time; the 150-character limit and the 30 s timeout come from the user.

## Comment answers

A comment answer is a one-shot call of the Claude CLI that answers a ticket comment that mentions Claude, without a chat, as Haiku naming runs: no session is kept, and the call adds no chat, no transcript and no item to the Chats section or to the ticket's Chats tab. The engine of the main device starts it when `tickets.addComment` stores a new comment of a user that mentions Claude, and again for each Retry ([Mentioning Claude](tickets.md#mentioning-claude), [Comment answers](tickets.md#comment-answers)).

### Answer command

```
<CLI path> -p --output-format json --no-session-persistence --model <model> --effort <effort> --permission-mode dontAsk --tools Read,Grep,Glob --allowedTools <answer tools> --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <data dir>/Runtime/mcp/answer-<answer id>.json --append-system-prompt <rendered comment-answer prompt> <input>
```

- The command is shown with shell quoting; the engine passes each argument as one argv element, without a shell.
- `<model>` and `<effort>` are the `model` and the `effort` of the project's `chatDefaults`, else of the host's `defaultChatSettings` ([Chat settings](data-model.md#chat-settings)); their `permissionMode` and `ultracode` are not used.
- `--tools Read,Grep,Glob` leaves Claude the read-only file tools of the CLI and no other built-in tool. `--permission-mode dontAsk` refuses, without asking, every tool that `--allowedTools` does not list.
- `<answer tools>` is this single argument: `Read,Grep,Glob,mcp__hephaestus__list_projects,mcp__hephaestus__get_project,mcp__hephaestus__list_tickets,mcp__hephaestus__get_ticket,mcp__hephaestus__update_ticket,mcp__hephaestus__set_ticket_status,mcp__hephaestus__list_bundles,mcp__hephaestus__get_bundle,mcp__hephaestus__search,mcp__hephaestus__list_docs,mcp__hephaestus__list_web_tabs,mcp__hephaestus__list_skills,mcp__hephaestus__get_skill,mcp__hephaestus__list_chats,mcp__hephaestus__get_chat`: the tools that read HephaestusV2 data, plus `update_ticket` and `set_ticket_status`.
- `--strict-mcp-config` keeps every other MCP server away. `<answer id>` is a new UUID for each call. Before the call the engine writes the MCP configuration file `Runtime/mcp/answer-<answer id>.json` with file mode 0600, in the format of the [MCP configuration file](#mcp-configuration-file) of chats, with a new answer token; the MCP server maps the token to the actor `claude` with the chatID `answer` and limits it to the tools above and to the comment's ticket ([MCP](mcp.md#authentication)). When the call ends, whatever the reason, the token is revoked and the file is deleted.
- `<rendered comment-answer prompt>` is the internal skill comment-answer, read from the library at every call and rendered for the comment's ticket ([Composition and rendering](#composition-and-rendering), [Default: comment-answer](skills.md#default-comment-answer)). `<input>` is built as [Answer input](#answer-input) specifies and starts with `Ticket:`, never with `-`.
- Working directory: the root of the ticket's project. Environment: [Environment](#environment), without the hook token: the call has no hook.
- The number of calls running at once is not limited. A call starts once the Claude CLI is available; while the status is checking, it waits for the computation to end, at most 20 seconds, as the chat methods do ([Version and status](#version-and-status)).
- A call still running 5 minutes after its start receives SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 5 s later ([Signals](#signals)). Deleting the comment or its ticket, or removing the ticket's project, ends its call the same way at once. A call is registered with the engine's helper processes, so quitting the app ends it as [Shutdown](architecture.md#shutdown) specifies.

The flags, the answer tools and the 5 s before SIGKILL are Default chosen at design time; the 5-minute timeout, the model and the effort come from the user.

### Answer input

`<input>` is the line `Ticket:`, the ticket rendered as `{{ticket}}` ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks)), every chat of the ticket that has metadata listed under `Chats:`, then a blank line, the line `Comment to answer:` and the comment rendered as the comments of `{{ticket}}` are, its attachments included:

```
Ticket:
ID: HEPH-12
Title: Locked web tabs close with Command-W
…
Comments:
- 2026-10-31T12:10:00Z by user:
  @Claude why does this ticket depend on HEPH-9?
Chats: none

Comment to answer:
- 2026-10-31T12:10:00Z by user:
  @Claude why does this ticket depend on HEPH-9?
```

The ticket is rendered as it is when the call starts, so a Retry gets the ticket as it is then. Its attachment paths, and those of the comment, are absolute paths on the main device, which Claude reads with its file tools. The 30,000-character cut of `{{ticket}}` applies to the ticket part only. Default chosen at design time.

### Answer output

When the process exits, the engine reads its standard output as one JSON object:

1. When the output is a JSON object whose `total_cost_usd` is a number of at least 0, the cost is stored in the ticket's project, whatever the outcome, as [Comment answers](costs.md#comment-answers) specifies.
2. The call succeeded when the process exited with status 0, the output is a JSON object, its `is_error` is not true and its `result` is a string that is not empty after trimming.
3. On success, the engine adds the trimmed `result` as a new comment of the ticket with `tickets.addComment` and the actor `claude` with the chatID `answer`, so its author is Claude and it starts no answer. The answer state of the mentioning comment is cleared in the same `ticket.updated`.
4. Otherwise the call failed: the answer state of the comment becomes `failed` and the engine writes `comment answer for <ticket id> failed: <reason>` to `engine.log` with the level WARN, `<reason>` being `timeout`, `the Claude CLI is not available`, `could not start: <error>`, `exit code <n>` (128 + the signal number for a process ended by a signal), `the output is not a JSON object`, `is_error is true`, `the result is empty` or `the answer cannot be posted: <message>`, when `tickets.addComment` fails.
5. When the comment or its ticket no longer exists, nothing is posted and no state is kept; a cost reported by the call is still stored.

The changes Claude made to the ticket with `update_ticket` and `set_ticket_status` during the call stay, whatever the outcome. The log texts are Default chosen at design time.

## Notifications

The engine reports two kinds of chat events, which the Macs showing the chat's device turn into macOS notifications ([UI › Status bar](ui.md#status-bar)); the [Notifications](ui.md#notifications) tab of each Mac's Settings turns them off on that Mac.

| Kind      | API value  | Event                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| needs you | `needsYou` | The chat's state becomes `waitingPermission` or `waitingAnswers` (a question set, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request, a font request or an animation request) from another state. |
| finished  | `finished` | A turn ends ([Turn tracking](#turn-tracking)) and the chat's state becomes `idle`.                                                                                                                                    |

- The `error` state posts nothing ([Chat states](#chat-states)). A turn that ends in `waitingAnswers` posts only the "needs you" notification.
- No "finished" notification for a turn stopped with Stop ([Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn)), for a turn of a chat with an `orchestrator`, an orchestrated ticket chat or a CI chat ([Reports to the orchestrator](#reports-to-the-orchestrator)), or for a turn of an orchestrator that ends while one of the chats it orchestrates, the chats whose `orchestrator` is that chat, is working ([Chat states](#chat-states)). An orchestrator, a bundle chat or the implement chat of a single ticket, thus posts "finished" when its turn ends and none of its chats is working. These chats post their "needs you" notifications as every chat.
- Only a change from a state that `chat.updated` already reported counts. The states the engine computes when it starts, or when it replays the transcripts of a project loaded later ([Data model](data-model.md#loading)), post nothing, so a restart does not post again for the chats that were already waiting.

### Title and detail

- Title: `<project identifier> · <chat title>`, for example `HEPH · Status history for tickets`. Each Mac builds it from the chat's metadata when it posts.
- Detail: a summary of at most 150 characters written by the Haiku model ([Summary call](#summary-call)) from:

| Event                                          | Haiku summarizes                                                                                                     |
| ---------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| finished                                       | Claude's last reply of the turn                                                                                      |
| needs you, `waitingPermission`                 | the tool name and the input of the oldest pending permission request ([Request](#request))                           |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, question set      | the texts of the questions of the set that is not completed ([Posting questions](#posting-questions))                |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, symbol request    | the question of the symbol request without answer ([Posting a symbol request](#posting-a-symbol-request))            |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, color request     | the question of the color request without answer ([Posting a color request](#posting-a-color-request))               |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, sound request     | the question of the sound request without answer ([Posting a sound request](#posting-a-sound-request))               |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, shortcut request  | the question of the shortcut request without answer ([Posting a shortcut request](#posting-a-shortcut-request))      |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, font request      | the question of the font request without answer ([Posting a font request](#posting-a-font-request))                  |
| needs you, `waitingAnswers`, animation request | the question of the animation request without answer ([Posting an animation request](#posting-an-animation-request)) |

- Claude's last reply of the turn is the text of the turn's last `assistant` item without `parentToolUseID`, as in the reports to the orchestrator; for "needs you", that of the turn in progress or of the turn that just ended.
- Fallback detail, used when the summary call fails or times out, and by a Mac whose `AI-generated summary` toggle is off: the first line of Claude's last reply of the turn that is not empty after trimming, trimmed and cut to 200 characters. When the turn has no reply text, the status line text of the state ([Chat states](#chat-states)), `Waiting for your permission` or `Waiting for your answers`, or `Claude finished replying` for a finished turn.
- A finished turn without reply text gets no summary call: its detail is the fallback detail. Default chosen at design time.
- The notification plays the default macOS notification sound.

### Notification events

Each event of the table above emits `chat.notification` twice, with origin `system` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#event-payloads)):

1. At once, with `stage` `immediate` and the fallback detail.
2. When the summary call ends, with `stage` `summary` and the summary, or the fallback detail when the call failed, timed out or gave an empty text.

- The `summary` stage is not emitted when, by then, the chat was deleted, the engine stops, a newer event of the same chat was emitted, or, for "needs you", the chat's state is no longer `waitingPermission` or `waitingAnswers`. The rule on a newer event keeps the latest notification of the chat from being replaced by an older one: Default chosen at design time.
- The engine runs the summary call for every event, whatever the settings of the Macs: a Mac whose `AI-generated summary` toggle is on posts at the `summary` stage, and a Mac whose toggle is off posts at the `immediate` stage ([UI › Notifications](ui.md#notifications)).

## Persistence

| Path (relative to `.hephaestus/`)               | Content                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `chats/<chat id>.yml`                           | chat metadata ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file))                                                                               |
| `chats/<chat id>.jsonl`                         | transcript: `{"op":"add","item":ChatItem}` and `{"op":"update","item":ChatItem}` lines ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-transcript-file))    |
| `chats/<chat id>/<attachment uuid>-<file name>` | the chat's attachment folder: user attachments, the feedback screenshot and tool-result images ([Data model](data-model.md#attachments))     |
| `chats/<chat id>/agents.jsonl`                  | the chat's workflows and agents: one record per line ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-agents-file), [Storage of agents](#storage-of-agents)) |
| `chats/<chat id>/agents/<agent id>.jsonl`       | the transcript of a workflow agent ([Data model](data-model.md#agent-transcript-file))                                                       |

- The line `chats/` of `.hephaestus/.gitignore` keeps all of them out of git.
- The metadata file is rewritten atomically when the title, settings, links, `sessionStarted`, `closed` or `contextUsage` change, when a user message is added and when a turn ends; each rewrite sets `updated` and emits `chat.updated`.
- Each new item appends an `add` line; replacing an item (a permission decision or cancellation, an answer) appends an `update` line. The transcript is never rewritten; loading replays it as [Data model](data-model.md#replay) specifies.
- `chats.delete` terminates the process, keeps the chat's cost in the project's `deletedChats` ([Deleted chats](costs.md#deleted-chats)), removes the chat id from its ticket's or bundle's `chats`, and moves the metadata file, the transcript and the chat's folder, its attachments, workflows and agents included, to the Trash ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsdelete)). Claude's own session files are left untouched. From the moment `chats.delete` starts, the engine ignores every line the chat's terminating process still writes, adds no item, writes no file and emits no event for that chat.
- `ChatMeta`, as returned by the API, is the metadata plus `projectID` and `state` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chat-metadata-in-the-api)).

## Chat methods

The method contract (actors, parameters, results, errors, events) is in [Engine API](engine-api.md#chats). This document specifies what the methods do with Claude:

| Method                   | Specified here                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `chats.create`           | [Launch](#launch), [User messages](#user-messages), [Haiku naming](#haiku-naming)                                                                                    |
| `chats.send`             | [Launch](#launch), [User messages](#user-messages), [Haiku naming](#haiku-naming)                                                                                    |
| `chats.interrupt`        | [Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn)                                                                                                                                  |
| `chats.updateSettings`   | [Changing settings](#changing-settings)                                                                                                                              |
| `chats.rename`           | [Applying the title](#applying-the-title); the title is trimmed and must be one line of 1 to 100 characters ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file))         |

## Swift types

The Claude integration lives in `Sources/HephaestusCore/Claude/`. The type names are Default chosen at design time.

| Type                                | Role                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ClaudeCLI`                         | The login-shell environment, the location of the CLI, its `ClaudeStatus` and the model list ([Claude CLI](#claude-cli)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `ChatManager`                       | The chats at runtime: processes, stream-json mapping, control protocol, the computed `state` and `backgroundWork`, the workflows and agents of each chat, pending permission requests, question sets, symbol requests, color requests, sound requests, shortcut requests, font requests, animation requests, driving devices, chat tokens and idle termination. Chat metadata is loaded with its project.                                                                                                                       |
| `ClaudeProcessLauncher` (protocol)  | Every access of the Claude integration to the machine: `captureEnvironment(processEnvironment:log:)`, `homeDirectory`, `isExecutableFile(atPath:)`, and `start(_:)`, which spawns a process with pipes on stdin, stdout and stderr as the leader of a new process group. The app uses `SystemClaudeProcessLauncher`; tests use an in-memory launcher that replays fixture lines, so they never run the user's shell or CLI and never depend on the user's files ([Engine configuration](architecture.md#engine-configuration)). |
| `AgentTranscriptMapper`             | The mapping of the lines of a workflow agent's transcript file to items ([Agent transcript lines](#agent-transcript-lines)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `WorkflowFolderReader`              | The reads of a workflow folder: the journal and the agent transcripts, each from where its previous read stopped ([Workflow folder](#workflow-folder)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `ChatAgentTracker`                  | The workflows and agents of one chat, their state rules and the sub-agent of each item ([Agents](#agents)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `SFSymbolCatalog`                   | The [symbol catalogue](#symbol-catalogue) of the Mac and its [search](#symbol-search); `SFSymbolCatalog.system` reads the running macOS's files, and tests read their own. The engine checks suggestions and project icons with it, and the pinned symbol card and the Icon popover of Project Settings search it.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `SystemColors`                      | The names of [System colors](#system-colors) in their order, the UIKit values of the names AppKit lacks, and the check of a color: a hex color `#RRGGBB` or a system color name. The engine checks the colors of `chats.postColor` with it; the pinned color card lists its names, and the renderers accept color markers with it.                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `SoundCatalog`                      | The [sound catalogue](#sound-catalogue) of a project on the main device and its [search](#sound-search): the macOS and Simulator runtime sounds, read once (`SoundCatalog.SystemSounds`), and the project's sound files. The engine checks the suggestions of `chats.postSound` with it and answers `sounds.list` and `sounds.read` from it; tests give their own system sounds.                                                                                                                                                |
| `KeyShortcut`                       | A shortcut of the [shortcut notation](#shortcut-notation): `init?(_:)` reads a notation and refuses any other text, `notation` writes it and `keyCaps` gives its key caps. The engine checks suggestions and `inUse` with it, and the transcript, the pinned shortcut card and ticket descriptions and comments draw key caps with it.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `StandardShortcuts`                 | The [standard macOS shortcuts](#standard-macos-shortcuts) and their actions; `action(for:)` gives the action of a shortcut, nil for any other. The pinned shortcut card reads it for its [warnings](#shortcut-warnings).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `FontNotation`                      | The [font notation](#font-notation): parses a font into a text style or a family, weight and size, checks the size, writes a size and reads a font as the rows of the pinned font card show it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `FontCatalog`                       | The [font catalogue](#font-catalogue): the system designs, the families installed on the Mac (`FontCatalog.installed`, read with CoreText the first time it is used), the families of a project's font files and the text styles, with the [search](#font-search) and the [nearest weight](#nearest-weight). The engine checks suggestions with it; the pinned font card and the font markers use it.                                                                                                                           |
| `TitleNamer` (protocol)             | `title(for:) async -> String?`, the seam the engine calls for [Haiku naming](#haiku-naming); nil keeps the provisional title. `HaikuTitleNamer` is the app's implementation; tests provide another.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `NotificationSummarizer` (protocol) | `summary(for:) async -> String?`, the seam the engine calls for the [summary call](#summary-call) of a [notification](#notifications); nil gives the fallback detail. `HaikuNotificationSummarizer` is the app's implementation; tests provide another.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `CommentAnswers`                    | The comment answers at runtime ([Comment answers](#comment-answers)): the answer state of each comment, the running calls with their answer tokens and processes, the timeout, the stop when a comment, a ticket or a project goes, and the costs they report. `CommentMentions` finds the mentions of Claude in a comment's text ([Mentioning Claude](tickets.md#mentioning-claude)) for the engine and the native Markdown view.                                                                                              |
