## System prompts

### Composition and rendering

- The texts of the system prompts are the [system prompt skills](skills.md#system-prompt-skills), internal skills of the skills library that the user edits in the [Internal Skills view](skills.md#internal-skills-view) (View ▸ Internal Skills), outside any project, with Restore Default: `common`, `project`, `ticket`, `bundle`, `ci`, `comment-answer`, `naming-ticket`, `naming-chat`, `naming-bundle` and `notification-summary`. Their default text is in [Default SKILL.md files](skills.md#default-skillmd-files).
- The engine reads them from the library at every launch of a chat process, the first launch and every `--resume` relaunch, and at every comment answer, naming call and summary call, after writing the missing internal skills: a saved edit applies at the next launch of any chat, resumed chats and chats created before the edit included, and at the next call; running processes are not restarted. The text of a skill is its body without the white space and line breaks at its start and at its end. A skill that cannot be read gives the app's default text, with a warning in `engine.log` ([System prompt skills](skills.md#system-prompt-skills)).
- The value of `--append-system-prompt` is the rendered common skill, a blank line (`\n\n`), and the rendered skill of the chat's kind: project, ticket or bundle. For a feedback chat it is the rendered common skill alone: the description of the feedback chat is in the internal skill `feedback`, which its first message calls with the comment and the feedback context ([Feedback message](#feedback-message)). For a CI chat it is the rendered ci skill alone, without the common skill ([CI chats](#ci-chats)). Rendering happens at every launch, so a resumed chat gets the current ticket, bundle and project data.
- The first message of a chat created by `chats.implementTicket`, `chats.implementBundle` or `chats.createFeedback` calls a called [internal skill](skills.md#internal-skills), which holds the steps of its task ([Implement and feedback chats](#implement-and-feedback-chats)), and that of a chat created by `chats.watchCI` is the CI message ([CI chats](#ci-chats)). The skills naming-ticket, naming-chat, naming-bundle and notification-summary are passed unchanged to `--system-prompt` ([Haiku naming](#haiku-naming)).
- The comment-answer skill is rendered for each [comment answer](#comment-answers) call with the values of the comment's ticket and its project, and passed alone to `--append-system-prompt`, without the common skill.
- Rendering fills a skill with its own placeholders ([Placeholders](#placeholders)): it replaces each `{{name}}` of the skill's set with its value in one pass; values are inserted as they are and are not scanned again. Any other `{{…}}`, from a `{{` to the next `}}`, stays as written, for example `{{ticket}}` in the project skill. A unit test renders every default with sample values and checks that no `{{` is left, and docs conformance test 3 checks that every placeholder a default uses is in its skill's set ([Docs conformance tests](ci.md#docs-conformance-tests)).
- The common skill states the rules of [Claude workflow](workflow.md) in imperative form, except the steps of the implementation, of bundles, of the completion comment, of the wait for CI, of Go service projects and of feedback chats, which the called [internal skills](skills.md#internal-skills) state; it tells Claude to use `implement-ticket` whenever it implements a ticket and `implement-bundle` whenever it implements a bundle.

### Placeholders

Each system prompt skill is filled with its own set of placeholders, the skills of the column `Filled in`. The skills naming-ticket, naming-chat, naming-bundle and notification-summary have none: they are passed unchanged. The sets come from the user.

| Placeholder            | Meaning                                                                                                                                        | Example                                                                     | Filled in                                           |
| ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| `{{projectName}}`      | name of the chat's project, or of the project of the answered comment's ticket                                                                 | Hephaestus V2                                                               | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci, comment-answer |
| `{{projectID}}`        | identifier of the chat's project, or of the project of the answered comment's ticket                                                           | HEPH                                                                        | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci, comment-answer |
| `{{projectRoot}}`      | absolute path of the project root on the main device                                                                                           | /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2                                       | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci, comment-answer |
| `{{workingDirectory}}` | the sentence that names the chat's working directory ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks))                                                     | It is your working directory.                                               | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci                 |
| `{{parentMemories}}`   | the memory folders that the chat's project inherits from its parent projects, with their `MEMORY.md` ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks))     | This project is a sub project: besides your own memories, …                 | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci                 |
| `{{deviceName}}`       | name of the main device                                                                                                                        | Studio                                                                      | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci, comment-answer |
| `{{docsPath}}`         | the project's `docsPath`, relative to the project root                                                                                         | docs                                                                        | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci, comment-answer |
| `{{projectType}}`      | the name shown for the project's [type](projects.md#project-types): `None`, `Go service` or `Swift application`; Default chosen at design time | Go service                                                                  | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci                 |
| `{{environments}}`     | the environments of a project whose type is `goService` ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks)); empty for the other types                       | The project's environments, where the Go service runs; …                    | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci                 |
| `{{globalMemories}}`   | the global memories folder and the lines of its `MEMORY.md` ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks))                                              | The global memories folder is /Users/example/.claude/memory. Its MEMORY.md: | common, project, ticket, bundle, ci                 |
| `{{ticketID}}`         | id of the chat's ticket, or of the answered comment's ticket                                                                                   | HEPH-12                                                                     | ticket, comment-answer                              |
| `{{ticket}}`           | that ticket ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks))                                                                                              | ID: HEPH-12 …                                                               | ticket                                              |
| `{{bundleID}}`         | id of the chat's bundle                                                                                                                        | HEPH-B2                                                                     | bundle                                              |
| `{{bundle}}`           | that bundle and its tickets ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks))                                                                              | ID: HEPH-B2 …                                                               | bundle                                              |
| `{{githubRepository}}` | `<owner>/<name>` of the [GitHub page](projects.md#github-page) of the chat's project, `none` when the project has none                         | Azavech/HephaestusV2                                                        | ci                                                  |

### Rendered blocks

The formats of these blocks are Default chosen at design time.

`{{workingDirectory}}`: for a chat that works in its worktree ([Working directory](#working-directory)), `Your working directory is the git worktree <folder> of this project, on the branch <branch>: read, change, build, test and commit the project's files there, not in the project root.`, with the absolute path of the folder of the worktree the chat works in and the name of the branch; for every other chat, `It is your working directory.`

```
Your working directory is the git worktree /Users/example/Library/Application Support/HephaestusV2/Worktrees/HEPH/HEPH-12 of this project, on the branch HEPH-B2/HEPH-12: read, change, build, test and commit the project's files there, not in the project root.
```

`{{environments}}`: for a project whose type is `goService`, a blank line, the line `The project's environments, where the Go service runs; the Kubernetes, Argo CD and API tools take an environment's name:`, then one line per [environment](data-model.md#project-environment) of the project as they apply, in their order, the inherited ones with the project's changes first ([Inherited environments](projects.md#inherited-environments)): `- <name>: <base URL>`, with `; invalid certificates allowed` after the base URL when the environment's `allowInvalidCertificates` is on, or `- <name>` for an environment without a URL, followed, for each Kubernetes field the environment sets, in this order, by `; namespace: <namespace>`, `; deployment: <deployment>`, `; Argo CD application: <argoCDApplication>` and `; kubeconfig file: <kubeconfig>`, each as stored. The line `The current working environment is <name>: use it when the user names no environment.` follows the environment lines, `<name>` being the project's [current working environment](projects.md#current-working-environment) at the launch; it is left out when the project has no environment. The single line `- none: the user adds them in Project Settings` replaces the environment lines when the project has none. For a project of another type, `{{environments}}` is empty. The default common skill writes the block at the end of its first paragraph, right after the project's type, so that its blank line starts a paragraph of its own; every chat of a Go service project except a CI chat, whose system prompt is the ci skill alone and whose default does not use the block ([CI chats](#ci-chats)), gets the current environments at each launch. Default chosen at design time (the place of the block):

```
The project's environments, where the Go service runs; the Kubernetes, Argo CD and API tools take an environment's name:
- local: http://127.0.0.1:8080
- staging: https://staging.example.com; invalid certificates allowed; namespace: orders; deployment: orders-api; Argo CD application: argocd/orders-staging; kubeconfig file: ~/.kube/staging.yaml
- preview; namespace: orders-preview; kubeconfig file: /Users/example/.kube/preview.yaml
The current working environment is staging: use it when the user names no environment.
```

`{{globalMemories}}`: the device's global memories folder, resolved and created at the launch, and the lines of its `MEMORY.md` ([In chats](memories.md#in-chats)): the line `The global memories folder is <folder>. Its MEMORY.md:`, followed by the text of `MEMORY.md` with CR LF and lone CR converted to LF and trimmed of white space and line breaks at both ends.

```
The global memories folder is /Users/example/.claude/memory. Its MEMORY.md:
- [Short answers](answer-style.md) — the user wants short answers in every project
- [No force push](no-force-push.md) — never force-push, in any repository
```

- When `MEMORY.md` is missing or its trimmed text is empty, `{{globalMemories}}` is the single line `The global memories folder is <folder>. Its MEMORY.md has no line yet.` A `MEMORY.md` that is larger than 20 MiB, is not valid UTF-8 or cannot be read counts as missing, and `engine.log` gets the warning `<path> cannot be read: <reason>`.
- The text keeps at most 200 lines and 25,000 characters, the limits of the Claude CLI for a `MEMORY.md` (checked with Claude Code 2.1.309): a text of more than 200 lines keeps its first 200 lines; a text still longer than 25,000 characters keeps its lines up to the last line break before 25,000 characters, none when its first line is longer. A cut text is followed by the line `[Truncated: read <folder>/MEMORY.md for the other lines.]`.
- `<folder>` is the absolute path of the folder on the main device, without final `/`.
- A CI chat has no `{{globalMemories}}`, its system prompt being the ci skill alone, whose default does not use it ([CI chats](#ci-chats)); it still gets the folder with row 19 of the [command line](#command-line) ([In chats](memories.md#in-chats)).

`{{ticket}}`: the ticket's current data, with attachment paths made absolute (`<project root>/.hephaestus/<path>`), as [get_ticket](mcp.md#get_ticket) returns them:

```
ID: HEPH-12
Title: Locked web tabs close with Command-W
Status: in_progress
Created: 2026-10-31T12:00:00Z by claude
Updated: 2026-10-31T12:30:00Z
Links: Depends on HEPH-9 · Related HEPH-13
<description>
Problem
Command-W closes the current web tab even when it is locked.

Current Behavior
1. Lock a web tab.
2. Press Command-W.
3. The locked tab closes.

Expected
A locked tab stays open; the app beeps and shows the tooltip "This tab is locked".

Change
Make Command-W skip locked tabs in the docs and in the web tab strip, with a test that Command-W leaves a locked tab open.

Testing
- Claude: on an isolated instance, press Command-W on a locked tab and check that the tab stays open.
- User: press Command-W on a locked tab and see the beep and the tooltip.

Docs
- [web-tabs.md › Locking](docs/web-tabs.md#locking)
</description>
Attachments:
- screenshot.png (image/png, 183204 bytes): /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/attachments/HEPH-12/5F2B7C10-3A4D-4E5F-8A9B-0C1D2E3F4A5B-screenshot.png
Comments:
- 2026-10-31T12:10:00Z by user (edited 2026-10-31T12:20:00Z):
  Happens only when the tab is not focused.
  Attachments:
  - console.txt (text/plain, 2048 bytes): /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/attachments/HEPH-12/0A1B2C3D-4E5F-4A6B-8C7D-9E0F1A2B3C4D-console.txt
Chats:
- B5A4C3D2-E1F0-4A9B-8C7D-6E5F4A3B2C1D (feedback): Locked tab closes on Command-W
```

- The line `Links:` lists the ticket's links as the Links line of the ticket detail shows them ([Ticket detail](tickets.md#ticket-detail)): the groups `Depends on`, `Blocks` and `Related`, in this order and separated by ` · `, each followed by its ids in ascending ticket number separated by `, `; a group without links is left out, and `Links: none` replaces the line when the ticket has no links ([Ticket links](tickets.md#ticket-links)).
- The description follows unchanged between the lines `<description>` and `</description>`.
- `Attachments: none` and `Comments: none` replace the lists when they are empty; a comment without attachments has no `Attachments:` line.
- After the comments, the line `Chats:` is followed by one line `- <chat id> (<kind>): <title>` for each id of the ticket's `chats`, other than the chat being launched, that has metadata; `Chats: none` replaces the list when there is none.
- A comment line is `- <created> by <author>:`, with ` (edited <updated>)` before the colon when `updated` is set; the comment text follows with every line indented by two spaces.
- A ticket rendering longer than 30,000 characters is cut at the last line break before 30,000 characters and followed by the line `[Truncated: call get_ticket for the full ticket.]`. Default chosen at design time.
- When the chat's ticket no longer exists, `{{ticket}}` is `This ticket was deleted.`

`{{bundle}}`: the bundle's header, then each ticket rendered like `{{ticket}}` after a blank line and a line `Ticket <n> of <count>:`:

```
ID: HEPH-B2
Title: Web tab polish
Tickets: HEPH-12, HEPH-13

Ticket 1 of 2:
ID: HEPH-12
Title: Locked web tabs close with Command-W
…

Ticket 2 of 2:
ID: HEPH-13
Title: Tab titles are cut too early
…
```

When the chat's bundle no longer exists, `{{bundle}}` is `This bundle was deleted.` When the rendered value of `--append-system-prompt` would exceed 400,000 UTF-8 bytes, the tickets of `{{bundle}}` are rendered in full up to that size and every later ticket as the single line `Ticket <n> of <count>: <id> <title> (call get_ticket for its content)`, `<title>` being `(deleted)` for a ticket that no longer exists. Default chosen at design time.

`{{parentMemories}}`: the memories that the chat's project inherits from its parent projects ([Inherited memories](memories.md#inherited-memories)), computed at each launch. It is empty when the project inherits no folder that exists: a top-level project, or a sub project that shares the folders of its ancestors. Otherwise it is a blank line, which ends the paragraph before it: the first paragraph of the default common skill, the paragraph that starts the example below, or, in a Go service project, the environments that the default common skill writes before it (Default chosen at design time), then one part per inherited folder that exists, nearest parent first, each after a blank line:

- The first line of a part is `Memories of the project <name> (identifier <id>), in the folder <folder>:`, with the name and the identifier of the ancestor that gave the folder and the folder's absolute path.
- The lines of the folder's `MEMORY.md` follow: its text without byte order mark, with CR LF and lone CR converted to LF and its leading and trailing white space and line breaks removed, limited as the CLI limits its own `MEMORY.md` (checked with Claude Code 2.1.309). A text of more than 200 lines keeps its first 200 lines; a text then longer than 25,000 UTF-16 code units keeps what comes before its last line break at an offset of at most 25,000 units, other than a line break that starts it, or, without such a line break, the characters that fit in 25,000 units. A cut text is followed by the line `[Truncated: read <folder>/MEMORY.md for the rest.]`.
- When the folder has no `MEMORY.md`, or one that is empty after this cleanup, larger than 20 MiB, not valid UTF-8 or that cannot be read, the line `This folder has no MEMORY.md.` follows instead; a file that cannot be read is logged in `engine.log`.
- The intro, the paragraph that starts the block, tells Claude to save a new memory in its own memory folder or, when it holds for every project, in the global memories folder that [`{{globalMemories}}`](#rendered-blocks) names, never in an inherited folder ([In chats](memories.md#in-chats)). Naming the global memories folder in it is Default chosen at design time.

```
This project is a sub project: besides your own memories, you have the memories that Claude saved for its parent projects, listed below nearest parent first. Read a memory file of these folders with your file tools when it is relevant, as you read your own memories. Never write in these folders: save every new memory in your own memory folder or, when it holds for every project, in the global memories folder.

Memories of the project Suite (identifier SUITE), in the folder /Users/example/.claude/projects/-Users-example-Developer-Suite/memory:
- [User role](user-role.md) — The user is a backend developer who is new to SwiftUI
- [Answer style](feedback_style.md) — Answer in short sentences
```

## Requirements covered

- R28: A ticket's Claude chat has access to everything about the ticket.
- R29: In a ticket's chat, the user has Claude implement the ticket, debug it or do other work on it.
- R30: The user can just ask Claude to implement the ticket.
- R33: Each project can have Claude chats.
- R34: In a project chat, Claude plans things and creates multiple items.
- R35: In a project chat, the user can make Claude work on the app.
- R50: The app integrates with the Claude CLI.
- R60: Then a Claude discussion pops up.
- R61: The discussion creates a ticket.
- R62: When Claude creates a ticket, a small Haiku model names it.
- R63: When a chat is started, a small Haiku model names it after the first message the user sends.
- R64: The chat window takes that name.
- R65: Chat text is fully selectable.
- R66: Chats are shown as chat bubbles.
- R67: Each bubble renders Markdown.
- R68: The chat shows each MCP call and each tool call.
- R69: The user selects the model.
- R70: The model choice includes Ultracode.
- R71: The user selects the effort.
- R72: The user selects the permissions, including Allow All.
- R95: The user answers the questions one by one.
- R97: One of the ways to answer is a recommended answer.
- R98: The user can answer however they want.
- R100: Under Problem, Current Behavior, Expected and Change, Claude explains the problem, the current behaviour, the expected behaviour and what should change.
- R102: The main window's sidebar and inspector are translucent and frosted, like the sidebars of native macOS apps such as Music.
- R103: The project chat in the inspector knows which items the user has open in the main window.
- R107: The pinned questions card can be minimized into a one-line bar and opened again.
- R108: The user moves between the questions of a set, answers them in any order and can change an answer until every question has one.
- R109: The user can skip a question; Claude then leaves that point out and mentions it in its reply.
- R111: Implement All runs the tickets that wait for no other ticket in parallel, each in its own ticket chat on its own branch, coordinated by the bundle chat that started them, which merges the branches and pushes once at the end.
- R112: Main windows and chat windows show the Claude plan's weekly usage in the status bar as a small round progress indicator; a click shows the percentage used, when the week resets and the overage status.
- R113: Claude can close the chat it runs in when the user asks or its work is finished; a closed chat shows `This conversation is over` in place of its composer until the user reopens it.
- R114: Claude marks each SF Symbol it names, and the chat transcript and the pinned questions card show the marker as the symbol's icon followed by its name.
- R115: Claude creates and edits tickets, comments and bundles without a permission prompt in every permission mode; deletions follow the chat's permission mode.
- R116: An update restart waits until all chats are done and Claude is not working.
- R117: Claude asks the user to choose an SF Symbol with `ask_symbol`: suggestions show as icons, any SF Symbol of the Mac can be searched, the user picks one, answers another way or skips.
- R118: Ticket and bundle ids named in chats, ticket descriptions and comments are links that open the ticket or the bundle.
- R119: The Testing section of a ticket Claude writes lists only checks of the behaviour in the running app, never code tests; the code tests to add can be named under Change.
- R120: In a chat, the caption above each message shows who sent it, the user's device or Claude, followed by its date and time.
- R121: The items open in the app reach the inspector chat's Claude as context outside the message text, and the user's message shows them as chips.
- R122: A permission mode chosen while Claude works applies in the same turn, and approves at once the waiting permission requests that the new mode allows.
- R123: Implement, Implement All and the feedback sheet let the user choose the model, the effort and the permission mode of the chat they start; the ticket chats that a bundle chat starts take its settings.
- R124: A ticket can depend on other tickets of its project or be related to them; the other ticket shows the inverse link, Blocks or Related; the user and Claude add and remove links; the ticket list marks a ticket that depends on a ticket that is not done, and Implement asks for confirmation while one is not done.
- R125: The chat lists have a filter button that hides closed chats: the chats closed by Claude and the chats whose ticket is done.
- R128: A macOS notification, titled with the project identifier and the chat title and detailed by a Haiku summary, tells the user when a chat needs them or when Claude has finished replying; each Mac turns each kind and the summary off in Settings.
- R132: Claude asks the user to choose a color with `ask_color`, as one color or a light and dark pair: suggestions show as swatches, any system color can be picked from a grid and any other color with the macOS color panel or a hex field, the user answers another way or skips, and the chat shows the colors Claude names and the answer as swatches.
- R133: In a Swift application project, Claude asks the user to choose a sound with `ask_sound`: the card lists the suggestions and searches the macOS sounds, the installed Simulator runtimes' sounds and the project's sound files; selecting one plays it; the user picks one, answers another way or skips.
- R134: In a Swift application project, Claude asks the user to choose a keyboard shortcut with `ask_shortcut`: suggestions show as key caps, the user can record another combination and sees when macOS or the app already uses the selected one, then picks one, answers another way or skips.
- R135: Claude asks the user to choose a font with `ask_font` and a sample text: the suggestions are drawn in their fonts, every font of the Mac and of the project can be searched, the user picks one, sets its weight and size and sees the sample in it, answers another way or skips.
- R136: In a Swift application project, Claude asks the user to choose a SwiftUI animation with `ask_animation`: each suggestion plays in a lane, the user compares them, sets a preset and its parameters in a Custom row, picks one, answers another way or skips.
- R137: Claude closes other chats of the project when the user asks it to, never on its own, except an orchestrator, which closes the chats it started when its task is done; a chat with a turn in progress is not closed.
- R138: The user closes one chat, or several selected chats of the Chats section, after a confirmation.
- R139: The user sees the workflows and agents that Claude starts in a chat, as rows under the chat in the chat lists, follows each agent's transcript live, and stops a running workflow with all its agents.
- R140: A comment that mentions `@Claude` gets an answer from Claude as a new comment, made by a one-shot call without a chat; typing `@` in the comment composer suggests Claude, mentions are highlighted, Claude may change the ticket's title, description, links and status, a row shows while Claude replies and offers Retry when it could not, and the answers count in the project's costs.
- R143: Claude asks the user only true unknowns and decides points with an evident answer without asking.
- R144: The chat that writes a ticket settles every decision of its implementation with the user before it creates the ticket or rewrites its description, and writes the answers into the description; a feedback chat creates its ticket, and closes, only after every decision is answered.
- R145: A chat started to implement a ticket asks the user no question until the user writes in it: it makes the choices itself, marks them in the docs and lists them, with the decisions it took, in its completion comment; the engine refuses its questions.
- R146: In a Go service project, Claude waits after the push for CI to be green, fixing what fails, then tests the tickets on a test service and sets them done; Go service tickets have no User tests.
- R148: A message of the user in a chat transcript shows each line break of its text as a line break.
- R149: Claude's replies in chats and the comments Claude writes show the model and effort that wrote them.
- R150: Claude shows the user images in the chat with `show_image`: PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP files are copied into the chat and shown, with an optional caption, at the place of the call, without a permission prompt, in local and remote windows; clicking one opens the image viewer.
- R151: Claude can ask multiple-choice questions: the user checks options within the minimum and maximum Claude sets and adds own answers.
- R156: A project can have an icon, an SF Symbol picked in Project Settings from a popover that searches every SF Symbol of the Mac; Claude reads and sets it.
- R161: After an implementation is pushed in a project with a GitHub page, a CI chat follows CI and reports to the orchestrator, which asks the user how to proceed when CI fails and finishes only once CI is green or the user decides so.
- R162: Each environment of a Go service names its namespace, deployment, Argo CD application and kubeconfig, which Claude receives and uses through HephaestusV2's Kubernetes and Argo CD tools.
- R164: The main button of the pinned questions card reads `Submit` only when pressing it sends the answers to Claude, and `Next` on the other questions of the set.
- R167: A sub project's chats always receive the memories of its parent projects, which its Memories section lists read-only.
- R168: The header bars at the top of the panes of the main window's centre column, and the inspector's header, are all 36 points high, so that their bottom separators lie on one line.
- R169: Claude keeps global memories that every chat of every project receives; the user shows and edits them with View ▸ Global Memories.
- R174: A sub project that uses its parent's settings inherits its parent's environments and changes their URL, port, deployment and Argo CD application.
- R175: Ticket descriptions and comments draw the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers, and Claude writes them there when it names one.
- R177: Each Go service project has a current working environment, chosen in the sidebar footer, next to the project identifier, and shared by every device, which the Contract view, Project Settings and Claude use.
- R179: The Chats section lists, in one list, only the chats the user starts in the project (project and feedback chats) and the chats whose ticket or bundle was deleted; ticket, bundle and CI chats show only on their ticket or bundle.
- R180: The detail of a ticket or a bundle lists, in a Mentioned In tab, the other chats of the device that mention it.
- R181: Feedback, Implement and Implement All start their chat by calling the internal skills feedback, implement-ticket and implement-bundle, which, with finish-implementation, hold the instructions of these tasks, are always active in every project, and are shown and edited only with View ▸ Internal Skills.
- R182: Each environment of a Go service has an `Allow invalid certificates` setting, off by default and changeable by a sub project that inherits the environment, with which the Contract view's requests to it accept any invalid server certificate.
- R183: Typing `/` at the start of a chat message lists the skills of the chat's project whose name contains the typed text, to complete the skill's call.
- R184: Claude marks each keyboard shortcut, font, sound and animation it names in a chat reply, and the chat transcript draws the marker.
- R185: Every prompt that HephaestusV2 gives Claude is an internal skill that the user edits with View ▸ Internal Skills, with Restore Default, and that HephaestusV2 puts in the system prompt of the chats and calls that use it.
- R186: A chat shows how much of Claude's context window its conversation uses and what remains before compaction, as a ring and `<n>% left` in the composer that change after each Claude message and stay with the chat.
- R187: A chat shows when Claude Code compacts its conversation: the status line and the context indicator while the compaction runs, a transcript line with its trigger and the size of the conversation before it when it ends, and the number of compactions in the indicator's tooltip.
- R188: The user selects the context window of each chat, Auto, 200K or 1M; a running chat takes a new window once its turn and background work end.
- R189: Every Markdown view of the app draws the SF Symbol, color, font, shortcut, sound and animation markers the same way, and a click on ▶ plays the sound.
- R190: Every chat of a Go service project whose API Explorer shows a contract lists the contract's operations and calls them on an environment through HephaestusV2's API tools, with the credentials and headers stored in the API Explorer; GET and HEAD calls run without a permission prompt.
- R194: Every section of the main window shows a title, and so do the Global Memories and Internal Skills views: Dashboard, Tickets, Bundles, Chats, Web, Builds, Skills, Memories and API Explorer show their sidebar title in a title row above their left column.
- R195: In a chat, the paperclip button of the composer links any internal resource of HephaestusV2 that the user finds with the search panel: a project, a ticket, a bundle, a doc, a code line, a chat, a skill or a web tab.
- R202: The user starts virtual machines from the Ready images in Settings, keeps them between runs, starts, stops and deletes them, and shows the screen of one in a main window, where the mouse and the keyboard drive it; at most 2 run at once, and the app does not restart by itself for an update while one runs.
- R204: Every chat of every project gives Claude full control of virtual machines with the VM tools: Claude starts one freely or joins Claude's one of the same image, waits up to 10 minutes for a free place, drives it one action at a time across chats, acts on the user's only when named and never stops them; Claude's idle VMs stop after the minutes set in Settings, and each VM gets the Claude token of Settings at its start.
